A thrown fetch is `ProbeFailureClass::Transport`, never `NoSuchBlock` — D56 O7 needs a 404 body a throw has none of — and the rendered line **may not name CORS or any cause**: the browser returns the identical `TypeError: Failed to fetch` for CORS rejection, preflight failure, connection refused and DNS failure. One endpoint permanently unreachable is `Unavailable` → `NotPromoted{endpoint-failures}` (D64 §6.2), **never** a promotion on the survivor and never a silent third endpoint. Overrides stay (spec line 137) and gain the CLI's A49 rule: `https://` only, exactly two entries, rendered as text and never as markup. **[D64, 2026-08-11]**: the endpoint-override label is the overlay's endpoints-disclosure line from the shared R18 table — the page composes no wording of its own for it.
- Notes: **[D132, 2026-08-12]** Probe-plan route ruled (docs/decisions/D132-the-pages-probe-plan.md): the page learns what to fetch from a **fifth member `plan` on D130's `verify_rendered` document** — **no sixth export**; D18 §5 R4's list stays at five. Shape, exactly: `"plan":{"blocks":[<u64 ascending, deduplicated>],"receipt":{"tx_hash":"<64 lowercase hex>"}|null}`, both keys always present, no `schema`, no interior version (D65 §4). **The register's framing was wrong in both directions.** The **Bitcoin half was already reachable three ways** — measured, an empty-evidence `verify_online` returns every attested anchor's height as a `u64` beside its line, and the report carries it inside `anchors[].source = "bitcoin-block-<H>"`. The **receipt half was reachable zero ways**: the transaction hash `eth_getTransactionReceipt` needs is in no document the page can see — proven by running a receipt-bearing bundle and searching the emitted bytes (report `false`, overlay `false`, verdict-class `false`), and structurally, because `receipt_echo` returns `None` for `NotAttempted` so a bootstrap overlay is **silent that a receipt exists at all**. So this row turned on **one 32-byte value**. **Cost did not decide it**: a sixth export was built and measured at **+146 B and 0.0199 ms/call** (a decode, 1.2 % of a `verify()`), while the plan *value* costs **+11 368 B (+0.622 %)** — which **both** arms pay. *(**Registrar, 2026-08-12**: that figure was the record's own prediction; the implementing wave measured **+10 377 B (+0.563 %)** by controlled A/B. The naive before/after comparison reads **−3 689 B** and is a trap — the *"before"* build recompiled nothing, which is the same artifact-freshness hazard **R83** records.)* What decided it is that a decode-only export returns a fetch plan for a bundle that **never passed verification**, while a member on `verify_rendered` cannot exist without a passing offline verdict; and that D18's list was closed at five **one wave earlier** with *"additions beyond five remain a decision"*. **The bootstrap is refused**: it costs a whole extra verification (0.96–1.02 × `verify()`, measured, **serial** before any fetch, main thread — no Web Worker under D129's CSP) and answers only the half that was already answered. **This row makes ONE module call on the online path.** `plan.blocks` = every OTS anchor carrying an upgrade group, ascending and deduplicated — **identical to the CLI's set**, because `ProbePlan::from_bundle` becomes the one function both surfaces call (`verify_host.rs::probe_online` is rewritten onto it, behaviour unchanged). Measured: that set is **strictly wider** than the overlay's `attested` set and the difference is **inert** — report, overlay and verdict-class all byte-identical either way, including when the extra height answers **refutingly**. Two rules for the evidence document this row sends: `endpoints.identities` is the CLI's **four**-entry mainnet disclosure list whether or not a receipt is probed (`OnlineEndpoints::identities()` appends the Arbitrum pair before the bundle is consulted), and `[]` is forbidden — measured, it renders *"endpoints: none recorded (pinned defaults)"*, which is false; and `receipt` is **omitted entirely** when no receipt probe was attempted, which is what makes it agree with `ReceiptProbe::NotAttempted`. An **empty plan does not disable the affordance**: zero fetches, and the overlay still renders, exactly as `antseal verify --online` does — there is no frozen wording for "nothing to check" and none is minted. Arbitrum requests reproduce A17 exactly: chain-id guard first (`wrong-chain`), then `eth_getTransactionReceipt` with `["0x"+tx_hash]`, and a reply whose `transactionHash` is not the one asked for is **`payload`**, never a disagreement. D66's request constraints are untouched — D132 says *what* to fetch, D66 says *how*. **Scoping the receipt probe out of v1 is refused**: spec line 137 names *"two public Arbitrum RPCs"* inside the verifier-page section, and the CLI discloses four endpoints regardless, so a page that never probed Arbitrum would either under-disclose or disclose endpoints it cannot use.
- Notes: **[Registrar, 2026-08-12 — wave 19 closing. This row SHIPPED, with one residue registered rather than closed.]** The offline block is **byte-identical before and after overlay activation**, so Accept row 1 is met as written. **Accept row 2 is NOT met and cannot be**: its four named cases (agreement/promotion, disagreement, mismatch→invalid, one-endpoint-down) need an anchor that **verifies** to `attested`, and no committed **bundle** fixture produces one — that is **R84**, a gate blocker for Q237 rather than an effort question, and it lands on R27 as well. The residue this row's own ruling creates is **R85**, and the measured mechanism is **worse than fail-closed**: the page pins Arbitrum One's chain id **42161** in its own template constant, and its pinned RPC pair is mainnet's — so a **Sepolia**-sealed receipt passes the chain-id guard (both endpoints correctly report 42161), `eth_getTransactionReceipt` then returns `null` from both, and the page renders the frozen **agreed-refutation** line *"both RPC endpoints agree the recorded transaction is not on chain"*. A transaction that exists on another chain is **affirmatively refuted** rather than left unconfirmed. `wrong-chain` fires only when a user overrides the endpoints to Sepolia RPCs. Recorded here so it is not rediscovered as a bug — and so the *"fail-closed and correct"* reading, which is what this residue was first framed as, does not survive into the next wave.
- Notes: **[D133, 2026-08-14 — Accept row 2's status is CORRECTED, and the correction is the point.]** The previous note's *"cannot be written at all, on either surface"* was measured **false**: the four cases are **already green at the library layer, on both surfaces and both targets** — `crates/antseal-core/src/verify/orchestration/tests.rs` under `// Accept row 2 — the mock-endpoint outcomes` (native **and** `wasm32-unknown-unknown`, all four calling one `attested_bundle()`), and `crates/antseal-cli/tests/verify_command.rs`, whose disagreement row spawns **two loopback `StubServer`s** and drives the production collector `probe_online` rather than a fixture host. **Cite those rows; do not rebuild them** — a lane reading the old note would write the cases a third time. What row 2 still owes is the **browser** venue named in its own parenthesis, which **R27** writes against D133's materialised fixture, and which R84 unblocked on 2026-08-15.

### R25 — Make the page build reproducible and surface provenance (footer hash + published SHA-256)
- Milestone: M3
- Size: M
- Deps: R23; P: pinned rustc/wasm-pack/wasm-bindgen toolchain (P6/P14). Q30/Q31 (M4) consume the SHA-256SUMS artifact for signing/publication — not ordering deps.
- Spec: Page provenance (line 139); Risks — malicious verifier host (line 186); M3 milestone (line 156)
- Do: Produce a documented, deterministic build script for `verifier-web/`: locked dependencies, pinned toolchain, path-prefix remapping, no embedded timestamps — two independent builds must be byte-identical. Emit a SHA-256SUMS artifact over the page file set for publication (signing itself is Q's). Implement the footer: the page displays its own build hash plus the exact advice line "for high-stakes verification, run `antseal verify` and compare verdicts" (hash-injection mechanism per the open decision).
- Accept:
  - ~~Two clean CI builds (different runners) produce byte-identical artifacts; CI job enforces this~~ — **Re-worded 2026-08-16 by [D135](../docs/decisions/D135-the-two-environment-build-comparison-and-its-tier.md) §4 R3**, which measured that the historical two-runner comparison worked *because* the runners happened to differ in `$CARGO_HOME`, i.e. the environment axis did the work and the runner identity did none. Reads, normatively: **"Two clean CI builds under different environments — differing checkout path and differing `$CARGO_HOME` — produce byte-identical module and glue; a CI job enforces this."** Enforced by **R86** at **deploy tier** (D135 §3 R1) — not a standalone job: it is the `build and check the page` step (`./scripts/pages-publish.sh --build`) inside `.github/workflows/pages.yml`'s pre-existing `publish` job, positioned before `actions/configure-pages` so a red ends the job with nothing staged. **Q238** carries the promotion to a required push context before the repository goes public.
  - SHA-256SUMS artifact generated and handed to Q's signing step
  - ~~Footer shows the build hash matching the published sum, plus the advice string (snapshot/playwright-asserted)~~ — **Corrected 2026-08-16 by [D136](../docs/decisions/D136-the-page-browser-lane-without-playwright.md) §2 R11**, because the comparison as written cannot be made and `(snapshot/playwright-asserted)` is ruled dead on D133. Reads, normatively and verbatim from that ruling: **"The footer's `#page-build` digest equals the SHA-256 of the module the page was built from, and the SHA-256 of the page file equals the sole `SHA256SUMS` entry published beside it — a two-link chain, because a file cannot contain its own hash. The `#advice` string renders verbatim as `MVP-SPEC.md` line 139 quotes it. Asserted natively over the artifact (`verifier-page-build.sh --self-test`, `page_template.rs:145`) and over the rendered DOM by the CDP browser arm."**
- Notes: **[D63, 2026-08-12]** Mechanism + artifact set ruled (docs/decisions/D63-footer-build-hash-and-artifact-set.md): **build-time injection of one declared token** into a committed template (injector refuses an already-injected input; no page-side hash computation, comparison or "integrity OK" affordance may exist — §5 R1); the value is **SHA-256 of the deployed `.wasm` as the browser loads it**, 64 ungrouped lowercase hex, untruncated, taken after every post-`wasm-pack` step; version/format-version/commit render from **R22's export**, not from injected HTML, and R27/Q19 assert the two agree. Published artifact = `SHA256SUMS` over the **served closure**, post-injection, content-decoded, `wasm-pack` byproducts deleted first, `SHA256SUMS` itself excluded; the **signed release copy is the only authoritative one** — a copy served by the page's host carries none. Build script is limited to a closed operation list (wasm-pack · copy · digest · token-substitute · emit sums), which is how spec line 137's *"no build beyond `wasm-pack`"* and line 139's mandated footer hash are held together. **Preconditions measured absent 2026-08-12 and blocking Accept row 1**: D18 unruled; no `wasm-pack`/`wasm-bindgen(-cli)` pin anywhere (deliberate — `docs/wasm-toolchain.md` §4); no `--remap-path-prefix` (the current wasm32 artifact embeds the checkout path 300× and `$CARGO_HOME/registry` 1 274×); no optimizer pin — `wasm-opt`/`binaryen` is named nowhere in the repo. **The advice line is NOT in R18's frozen set** (measured: zero hits in `tests/snapshots/verdict-wording.txt`), so Accept row 3's snapshot is R25's to create; it lives in the page template with a drift test against `MVP-SPEC.md` line 139 (comma included — `SPEC-REVIEW.md:287`'s comma-less variant is not normative), in the `security_assumptions_drift.rs` style, and is **not** added to `wording.rs`. **[D62, 2026-08-12]** R25 must not strip the page's `<meta http-equiv="Content-Security-Policy">`: R23 authors it and it is hash-covered by construction, because the ruled host emits no headers at all (D62 §3 R6). — **[2026-08-12, measured by the R22 lane — a supply-chain constraint on this row's Accept row 1, discovered by building the artifact rather than by reading the tool's docs.]** **`wasm-pack build --release` downloads and runs an unpinned `wasm-opt`.** With every proxy variable pointed at a dead port it *still* fetched binaryen into `~/.cache/.wasm-pack/` — **version 117, dated 2024-02-28**, in a directory that did not exist before the run. **Nothing in this repository pins binaryen**: it is named nowhere, so the fetch breaches **D63 §5 R5**'s closed operation list (wasm-pack · copy · digest · token-substitute · emit sums) and `docs/dependency-policy.md` §5. R22's build script therefore passes **`--no-opt --mode no-install`**, and the price is measured rather than estimated: **1.84 MB vs 1.53 MB** and **2.8 s vs 3 m 05 s**. Two obligations land on this row. **R25 must not re-enable `wasm-opt` without pinning binaryen and bringing it under the dependency policy** — a version, a checksum and a §1 row, on the same terms as every other pinned tool. And **Accept row 1 cannot be satisfied by a build that fetches an unpinned optimizer at build time**: two clean runners each resolving whatever binaryen answers on their day is exactly the non-determinism the row exists to refuse, so a byte-identical pair proves nothing while that fetch is in the build. The four preconditions listed above are unchanged, but the fourth of them — *"no optimizer pin — `wasm-opt`/`binaryen` is named nowhere in the repo"* — was a statement about the **repo**, and this is the measurement of what the **tool** does with that absence: it does not skip the step, it goes and gets one, over the network, unpinned, without saying so. D18 is no longer unruled and the toolchain pins now exist (R22 landed both), so the optimizer is the one precondition of the four that is still open **and** now known to be actively exercised.
- Notes: **[D129, 2026-08-12]** The artifact set is **one file** (docs/decisions/D129-verifier-page-file-shape-and-the-in-artifact-csp.md): `SHA256SUMS` has a single entry, `index.html` post-packaging, and `antseal_wasm.js`/`antseal_wasm_bg.wasm` become build **inputs**, deleted with the other byproducts before the sums are computed — so **D63 §5 R4 is applied, not amended**: its subject is the *complete served closure*, the ruled shape makes that closure exactly one file (nothing is fetched at load time — no `<script src>`, no `<link href>`, no `@import`, no `fetch` of any page resource), and the *"the sums list **is** the deploy list"* invariant is preserved exactly. **What the footer digest names is unchanged**: D63 §5 R2's value is SHA-256 of the module **as `wasm-pack` produced it, before packaging** — measured invariant even under the `--target web`/`no-modules` choice (D129 §1 b), so the target decision does not move the number — and the reader has two independent routes to it, extract-decode-hash from the page alone (**29 ms**, measured, and the in-page digest equalled the on-disk `.wasm`) or `sha256sum` on the release artifact. The **pre-packaging `antseal_wasm_bg.wasm` is published as a named artifact of the SIGNED RELEASE** (Q30/Q31), never as a line in the served manifest: that is *two names for two things*, not D63 §4 way 2's refused *two numbers* — but a served line would oblige the host to serve a 1.84 MB file the page never loads and would break R26's set-equality-both-ways. **Packaging order is fixed**: glue → base64 → footer digest (into **HTML text**, never into script text, so the two substitutions stay independent) → compute script/style hashes over the final text → CSP hashes → `SHA256SUMS`. No fixed point exists: the `<meta>` is outside every hashed element. D63 §5 R1's non-idempotence rule extends to all four tokens. **Eight assertions ride the built artifact** (D129 §5 R9): glue present verbatim as a contiguous substring (this is the testable line between concatenation and *bundling*, and it is what makes this row's sibling Accept *"no framework or bundler in the repo"* checkable); base64 round-trips to the module byte-for-byte and its SHA-256 equals the injected digest; decomposition to the committed template (authored residue measured at **2 858 B** of a 2 470 000 B artifact); no `</script`/`<script`/`<!--` in the glue and a clean base64 alphabet (true today of code we do not author, so asserted); no residual or double-injected token; no reachable default-init or `fetch` of the module; the CSP present with exactly the right hashes and neither `'unsafe-inline'` nor `'unsafe-eval'`; and a headless `file://` load that verifies an R9 vector with **zero** network requests. **The packaging step is measured deterministic** — four packs, including one over a second `wasm-pack` run of the same commit, all `81dc60e2…` — but this does **not** discharge Accept row 1: the module's own preconditions (path remapping, and the unpinned binaryen this row's own `Notes` record) are untouched. Cost recorded rather than hidden: **846 607 B gzipped against 590 127 B**, +43 %. Two riders land on **D63 §5 R5** at its site (see D63's second Amendment section): base64 is named beside *"compute digests"*, and the *"one-token diff"* clause is **narrowed** and replaced by this row's mechanical decomposition check. **Inline compression is refused** — it would erase the wire cost exactly, but `gzipSync(level 9) ≠ gzipSync(level 6)` on the same input, so a compressor version and level would become inputs to the published artifact's identity, which is D63 §7 rule 1's class and this row's own binaryen lesson; the host's `Content-Encoding` delivers the same saving without entering the artifact. Docs obligation from §7 rule 6: a browser's *Save page as → Webpage, Complete* **rewrites the HTML**, so the byte-preserving routes to a comparable copy are `curl` and right-click → *Save link as*
- Notes: **[D131, 2026-08-12]** Build output and test venue ruled (docs/decisions/D131-the-verifier-web-directory-footprint.md). **The built page is NEVER committed and never written into `verifier-web/`**: it is built to **`target/verifier-web/index.html`**, which `/target` at `.gitignore:8` already covers, so **`.gitignore` is not edited at all** — the precedent is `scripts/wasm-pack-build.sh`, which already writes the module to `target/wasm-pack/antseal-wasm/`. A committed artifact is D62 §3 R2's named hazard in the tree — *"a hand-copied artifact between the build that was hashed and the bytes that are served"* — and D129 §6's refused hybrid one level out. Cost of the alternative, measured: a **one-byte** change to a committed one-line page produces a **4 912 507-byte `git diff`** whose `--stat` reads *"1 insertion(+), 1 deletion(-)"*; wrapped at 76 the same change is **876 bytes**, 5 608× smaller — the one real argument for wrapping, and it evaporates when the artifact is not in the tree. **The base64 is ONE LINE**, and **not** on the `//`-skip footgun (real — **49** `//`-prefixed lines at wrap-76, **6.2×** the uniform prediction — but **inert**, since zero of `wording.rs`'s 131 literals is base64-shaped) and **not** on runtime (`atob` accepts newlines and spaces in **both** Chromium 150 and Firefox 140.13.0esr; both arms decode to the identical 1 841 981 bytes) and **not** on the reader's check (extract-decode-hash reproduces the module digest either way, 51 ms vs 29 ms). It is ruled on **determinism**: wrap width is a free parameter entering the hashed script text and therefore the published CSP hash, and five plausible implementations split two ways on it — GNU `base64` (no flag) and Python `encodebytes` wrap at 76; `base64 -w0`, Python `b64encode` and Node `Buffer.toString('base64')` do not. **Call the encoder with an explicit no-wrap argument and assert the token contains no newline**; D129 §5 R9 assertion 4's `[A-Za-z0-9+/=]` alphabet check then stays literal. **Venue**: D129 §5 R9 (1)–(7), D66's `page_csp_permits_connect_to_the_pinned_origins` (which D66 already calls a *no-network build check against the built artifact*) plus D129 §5 R6's non-pinned-`https:`-permitted half, the one-line `SHA256SUMS` and D63 §5 R1's injector non-idempotence all live in **`scripts/verifier-page-build.sh`** with `--build-only` / `--check` / `--self-test`, in `scripts/wasm-pack-build.sh`'s exact shape, wired into `scripts/local-gate.sh` as a self-test-then-lane pair. **They are not a `cargo test`**: their subject does not exist until a build runs, and measured, the sibling wording suite's own runtime moves **0.62 s → 0.87 s → 1.13 s** purely with what happens to be sitting in `verifier-web/` — a test that depends on whether somebody built is not a test. The **advice-line drift test** (D63 §5 R6 / §7 rule 5) is the exception and goes in `crates/antseal-wasm/tests/page_template.rs`, because both its subjects — `MVP-SPEC.md` line 139 and the committed template — are tracked files that exist without a build. Nothing here touches this row's open preconditions: path remapping and the unpinned binaryen stand exactly as D63 §7 rule 1 and this row's own `Notes` leave them.
- Notes: **[Registrar, 2026-08-12 — wave 19 closing. This row shipped EXCEPT its Accept row 1, and the venue count in the note above needs one correction.]** Shipped and green via `scripts/verifier-page-build.sh` (`--build-only` / `--check` / `--self-test`, default `--check`), wired into `scripts/local-gate.sh` as the self-test-then-lane pair D131 §9.2 specifies: the packaging step, the one-line `SHA256SUMS`, and the injector non-idempotence check — which is real, re-running the injector over the built page and matching its *"already injected"* message. **Correction to the venue clause: D129 §5 R9 is EIGHT assertions and only SEVEN are in this script.** Assertion **8** — the headless `file://` load that verifies an R9 vector with zero network requests — is deliberately elsewhere, in `scripts/verifier-page-browser.mjs`, run from `.github/workflows/verifier-page.yml` and **not** from `local-gate.sh`, which says so at its own site; R9 assertion 8's text already routes itself to *"R27/Q19's venue"*. Anyone reading *"the eight assertions are green via the build script"* would be wrong about which machine runs the eighth. **A second correction, recorded because it is the exact class D131 exists to repair**: the one-line-`SHA256SUMS` assertion is **tautological** — the sums string is constructed as a single line and then asserted to be a single line, so it documents D129 §5 R7 rather than testing it, and it cannot fail. It should be given a fault to survive or be restated over the emitted file. **Accept row 1 (*"two clean CI builds produce byte-identical artifacts"*) is NOT satisfied — see R83**: `scripts/verifier-page-build.sh --check` rebuilds the module **only when `target/wasm-pack/`'s two files are missing**, so a green `--check` packages whatever artifact is already there and is not evidence the bytes came from the tree. The optimizer precondition has moved and this row's older `Notes` above now over-state it: `scripts/wasm-pack-build.sh` passes **`--no-opt --mode no-install`**, so this repository's own build path no longer fetches binaryen at all — D63 §7 rule 1 is satisfied by its *disabled* arm and recorded. What stays open is the **pin**, which is what re-enabling `wasm-opt` would need first; path remapping is untouched and still open. **A THIRD obligation of this row is unmet and was not named in the wave's own accounting**: the **advice-line drift test** — D63 §5 R6 / §7 rule 5, routed by D131 §9.2 into `crates/antseal-wasm/tests/page_template.rs` — **does not exist**. That file carries exactly five tests (placeholders present, `verifier-web/` holds exactly the template, the ruled CSP is declared, no `style=` attribute, the glue's default init is never called), and none of them is it; nothing anywhere in `crates/` compares the page's advice line against `MVP-SPEC.md` line 139. The line itself **is** in the template and is spelled correctly, comma included, so this is a missing instrument rather than a wrong string — which is precisely the condition under which a string drifts unnoticed, and precisely the class D63 §7 rule 5 exists to prevent. **Accept row 3 is therefore not satisfied either**, on its snapshot-assertion half.
- Notes: **[Registrar, 2026-08-15 — Accept row 1's FIRST clause is MET BY MEASUREMENT; its SECOND is unbuilt and is now R86. The row stays open on that clause and on row 3.]** **The builder's machine no longer reaches the artifact.** `scripts/wasm-pack-build.sh` derives both roots D63 §7 rule 1 names from the environment doing the building — never hard-coded, which is the property under test — and passes them on the one `wasm-pack` command through `CARGO_TARGET_WASM32_UNKNOWN_UNKNOWN_RUSTFLAGS`. That channel is not a preference: of four candidates measured on cargo 1.92.0 it is the only one that **merges** with `.cargo/config.toml`'s getrandom `--cfg` — `RUSTFLAGS`, `CARGO_ENCODED_RUSTFLAGS` and `--config 'target.cfg(target_arch="wasm32").rustflags=[…]'` each **replace** the whole rustflags selection and measured the cfg count to **0**, the third being the one that looks safe and is not; `[profile.release] trim-paths` is refused by this toolchain as unstabilized. Scoped to the invocation and **not** to `.cargo/config.toml`, which answers D18 §10's rider by not touching the input at all: the wasm32 **debug** cache and the entire native cache see no flag change.
  **Six reproductions, module AND glue byte-identical**, at `08c074c`: baseline (25-character checkout path), a 122-character path, and a different `$CARGO_HOME` — module **1 853 031 B**, `baee3fc9125a04429232dcb8510985b570bb682f81cc1f54ef54f8dab54522a3`; page **2 516 397 B**. Verified independently by the registrar on the built artifact: **zero** occurrences of this machine's checkout path or `$CARGO_HOME`, **52** `/cargo/registry` placeholders, and the workspace remap's placeholder absent entirely — the strings that remain are relative (`crates/antseal-core/src/…`), which is *why* the workspace root never leaked at `debuginfo=0`.
  **Why the row may NOT be ticked.** Accept row 1 reads *"Two clean CI builds (different runners) produce byte-identical artifacts; **CI job enforces this**"*. The first clause is met and is now enforced locally at the packaging step by **R83**'s guard, which this row's Accept row 1 hereby names as the mechanism (R83's Accept row 3). The second clause has **no instrument**: the two-runner comparison was run by hand and `pages.yml` builds on one runner. That is **R86**, and until it exists the row's own words are unsatisfied. **Accept row 3 is unchanged and still open** on the missing advice-line drift test.
  **Two corrections to D63 ride here** and are recorded at that decision (2026-08-15 correction section): §5 R5 (i)'s `--locked` is **necessary but not sufficient** — with the lock deleted, `wasm-pack build … -- --locked` exits **0** and **recreates** it, because `cargo metadata` runs first and re-resolves (the probe picked `thiserror 2.0.20` where the lock names `2.0.19`), so the script passes the flag **and** asserts `sha256(Cargo.lock)` is unmoved across the build; and §7 rule 1's forward-cited **300 / 1 274** occurrence counts are the **debug** artifact's, where this release module measures **0** and **52**. **§1 (j) itself is correct and is not corrected** — it names the debug profile in its own closing sentence; the site that misleads is the precondition that carries its numbers forward.
  **The size arithmetic is recorded in the form that is true.** 52 × 3 B of path-length predicts **156 B** against the **192 B** two-runner delta measured, and the local fix predicts 520 B against **512 B**; the residual is data-section and offset encoding. The cause is right and is proven by **byte-identity**, not by counting characters — which is why the evidence is two roots varied, not a multiplication.
  **A consequence that is owed to R26, not to this row**: the footer digest moved, so any page deployed before this change publishes a digest its own bytes no longer reproduce, and a **redeploy** is owed before the live artifact and a fresh build agree.
- Notes: **[Registrar, 2026-08-16 — wave 21 closing. This row CLOSES, and the `Notes` above are corrected in the one place they are now false.]** **Accept row 1's second clause is met by R86** at the tier D135 §3 R1 ruled, and Accept row 3 is met at the wording D136 §2 R11 replaced it with — both re-written in the `Accept` block above rather than argued here. **The `Notes` claim that must not be carried forward**: the 2026-08-12 note says the advice-line drift test *"does not exist"* and that `crates/antseal-wasm/tests/page_template.rs` *"carries exactly five tests … and none of them is it"*. **It exists.** Measured by the registrar 2026-08-16, `grep -c '^#\[test\]' crates/antseal-wasm/tests/page_template.rs` → **7**, and the fifth of them, at **`page_template.rs:145`**, is `the_footer_advice_line_has_not_drifted_from_the_spec`, which reads `MVP-SPEC.md` at run time and pulls the sentence out of it rather than restating a literal. **D136 §1 (k) is itself now stale by one** — it records the same measurement as **six** and calls the drift test *"the sixth"*; the seventh, `the_footer_names_the_module_not_the_page_as_the_subject_of_its_digest` at `:244`, landed later the same day with R85 act 1's D136 §2 R12 label repair. The locator holds and only the count moved; the correction is recorded at D136. **The boundary that survives and is the reason the row needed the browser arm too**: that test asserts the **template**, via `template()` — not the built page and not the rendered DOM, which is what D136 §2 R12's three CDP assertions add. **Evidence for the tick, and the machine that produced it.** Accept row 1: six reproductions at `08c074c` recorded in the note above, plus R86's enforcement step, both **on this local host**; the two-runner half was proven on a hosted runner exactly once, by hand, at `08c074c`. Accept row 2: `SHA256SUMS` emitted by `scripts/verifier-page-build.sh`, in the gate's `verifier-page` lane. Accept row 3: `crates/antseal-wasm/tests/page_template.rs:145` (template) and the CDP browser arm (rendered DOM), the latter **local only** — `scripts/verifier-page-browser.sh` runs in `scripts/local-gate.sh` and in a `workflow_dispatch`-only workflow that has never been dispatched. **Q237 must name this machine for every clause of this row**; a `Q14`-style line citing only the check would certify a green whose provenance nobody recorded. **What this row still does not claim**: the environment axes proven independent are the **checkout path** and **`$CARGO_HOME`** only — the six builds shared a locale, `TZ`, `HOME`, user and host, so D63 §7 rule 2's list stays partly discharged.

### R26 — Deploy the page to the chosen host at the one canonical URL
- Milestone: M3
- Size: S
- Deps: R25; P: host + domain decision and access (P3); Q: docs referencing the URL; U: CLI prints the same URL in `reveal` output
- Spec: Page provenance — canonical URL, hosting is an M3 deliverable (line 139); Pre-M0 naming/domain check (line 3)
- Do: Deploy the reproducible-build artifacts to **GitHub Pages** from `aed900/antseal` via a manually-triggered Actions publishing workflow, at the one canonical URL **`https://antseal.org/`** (apex, trailing slash); Enforce HTTPS on; apex A + AAAA records per D62 §1 (g). **The host sends no configurable headers** — record its cache behaviour (`max-age=600`, fixed) rather than configuring it, and put every policy the page relies on **inside the hashed artifact** (D62 §3 R6). Record the deploy procedure so releases redeploy deterministically. Export the canonical URL as a single Rust constant (`antseal-cli`, D62 §3 R8) consumed by U28, and enforce value-identity across docs and page by checker, not by import.
- Accept:
  - Canonical URL serves the page; the **decoded** body of every published artifact hash-matches the published SHA-256SUMS, fetched **twice** (`Accept-Encoding: identity` and `gzip, br`) with both decoded hashes equal; set equality both ways over SHA-256SUMS; `Content-Type` asserted per artifact; `http://` `301`s and never `200`s; a mismatch inside the host's 600 s cache window is INCONCLUSIVE and re-polled, not red (D62 §3 R3)
  - Drag-and-drop verification of a real bundle succeeds against the hosted page
  - **Exactly one canonical URL value** exists across all surfaces — one Rust definition, every other occurrence byte-equal, pattern-anchored scan with a named allow-list for the records that discuss other names (D62 §3 R8; Q28's `tasks/Q.md:481` predicate)
- Notes: **[D62, 2026-08-12]** Host + URL ruled (docs/decisions/D62-verifier-page-host-and-canonical-url.md): `https://antseal.org/` on **GitHub Pages** — chosen because D2 already put source, CI and release channel inside GitHub, so every other host **adds** a party to spec line 186's threat and none removes one; Pages on the private repo needs Pro and the account is on Pro (docs/ci-verification.md:1978; maintainer-confirmed in session 2026-08-12), so **no visibility change and Q65 is not triggered**. The host emits no security headers at all (measured on `cli.github.com`/`bundler.io` with Enforce-HTTPS on), so **P3's HSTS-with-preload requirement is retired and replaced** by Enforce HTTPS + an optional CAA record (maintainer question — a wrong CA pin breaks renewal). `antseal.dev` is **not needed**: 8 near-neighbours are free and `.com` is unobtainable, and P3's `.dev → .org` redirect would have minted the second working URL line 139 forbids. **The host choice does not move line 186's residual** and the published hash sits in the same trust domain as the page — say so in the docs. Pre-deploy maintainer action: **remove the parking A records** so the URL fails closed instead of serving a third party's scripted page over plaintext (measured 2026-08-12T00:42Z; re-probed at 00:58Z the same name answered `502 Bad Gateway` from `openresty`, so what it serves is not even stable). Pages DNS and any CAA record are deliberately **deferred to this row** so certificate issuance is verified end to end. **R26 must not add a Content-Security-Policy at the host** — there is no host configuration surface, and R23 owns the in-artifact `<meta>` policy (D62 §3 R6). **[D63, 2026-08-12]** The Accept row's hash-match clause — D63 wrote this note against the row's then-current wording, *"served bytes hash-match the published SHA-256SUMS"*, which D62 replaced in the same act with the decoded-body form above; the two rulings agree and only the spelling moved — is scoped by D63 §5 R4: the set is the **served closure** (`index.html` + every subresource), comparison is on **content-decoded** bytes so gzip/br are transparent, and the check also asserts the load fetches nothing outside the set. The authoritative sums are the **signed release** copy; any copy served by the host itself is non-authoritative and must not be cited by docs or footer. **A host that rewrites, minifies, or injects into served bytes cannot satisfy this and is disqualified — a D62 selection criterion** (D63 §10 (iii)), and D62 §1 (e) measured that Pages' origin does not rewrite the body: the only transformation in play is the content-coding.
- Notes: **[D129, 2026-08-12]** The served closure is **one file** (docs/decisions/D129-verifier-page-file-shape-and-the-in-artifact-csp.md), so this row's set-equality check runs over a single `index.html` with `Content-Type: text/html`. Two riders. The two-encoding tripwire (D62 §3 R3 rule 3) matters **more** under this ruling, not less: one 2.47 MB text file is exactly what a transform-happy intermediary rewrites, and the whole payload is inside it. And a browser's automatic `GET /favicon.ico` is **not** a served artifact — it 404s (measured on the multi-file arm over http) — so **the set-equality check must be written over served *responses*, never over requests**: this row's Accept asserts *"no artifact is served under the page's own paths that is absent from `SHA256SUMS`"*, a 404 serves no artifact and violates nothing, and a request-based check would go red on it while the deploy is correct. R23 declaring a `data:` icon removes the request entirely, but the check must be right without that. The page's CSP is R23's `<meta>`; **this row still adds none at the host**, and there is still no host surface to add one at
- Notes: **[D131, 2026-08-12]** This row deploys **`target/verifier-web/index.html` as produced by R25 in the same workflow run**, never a file from the tree (docs/decisions/D131-the-verifier-web-directory-footprint.md): the built page is not committed and `verifier-web/` holds only the template. This **confirms D62 §3 R2 rather than changing it** — the Actions publishing source exists precisely so that *"R25's reproducible build is the thing that produces the deployed bytes"* and so that no hand-copied artifact sits between the build that was hashed and the bytes that are served; under D131 §3 R4 there is no tree copy to hand-copy, so the hazard is structurally unreachable rather than merely forbidden. Everything else in this row's D129 note stands: one file in the closure, `Content-Type: text/html`, the two-encoding tripwire, and the set-equality check written over served **responses** rather than requests.
- Notes: **[R26 EXECUTED — deployed and verified 2026-08-14, at commit `08c074c`.]** The page is **LIVE at `https://antseal.org/`**, published by `.github/workflows/pages.yml` (`workflow_dispatch`, run `31847839638`, every step green) from `target/verifier-web/` as R25 built it in the same run — so D62 §3 R2's hand-copied-artifact hazard stayed structurally unreachable, exactly as D131's note predicts. **Site state** (`GET repos/aed900/antseal/pages`): `cname antseal.org`, `build_type workflow`, `protected_domain_state verified`, `https_enforced true`, `https_certificate state=approved` expiring **2026-11-12**. **DNS as deployed**: four apex `A` records `185.199.108–111.153`, the two Porkbun parking records **removed**, the `www` CNAME to `pixie.porkbun.com` **deleted rather than repointed** (D62 refused the `.dev → .org` redirect as *"the second working URL spec line 139 forbids"*, and a `www` redirect is that shape; GitHub additionally documents that a subdomain pointed at the apex breaks Enforce-HTTPS), no `AAAA`, no `CAA` — the CAA deliberately deferred until after first issuance, since a wrong pin breaks it. **Accept, measured against the live origin, not the workflow's own step**: served **2 517 337 B**; `content-type: text/html; charset=utf-8`; `cache-control: max-age=600` and **no security header of any kind**, which is D62 §3 R6's whole reason the CSP lives in the artifact; the identity and gzip fetches **decode to byte-identical bodies**, so the host does not rewrite the body and is not disqualified by D63 §10 (iii); served digest `e0a89dbd25a229ed0db39ec0fb78ec060d5b824b4987a112b31bc7655d77a488`. **The deploy also bought the first two-runner comparison this project has had, and it FAILED**: the runner's page was 2 517 337 B against this machine's 2 517 081 B at the same commit, isolating R25's `$CARGO_HOME` leak (R83/R25, fixed since). **STANDING DEBT, and the reason this row's evidence must be read with its date**: R25's `--remap-path-prefix` fix moved the footer digest to `baee3fc9125a04429232dcb8510985b570bb682f81cc1f54ef54f8dab54522a3` and the page to 2 516 397 B, so **the live bytes above no longer reproduce from HEAD** and a redeploy is owed. Until it runs, a reader who builds the page and compares hashes gets a mismatch — precisely the suspicion line 139's provenance story exists to resolve — so the published number must not be cited as authoritative in the interim.
- Notes: **[Registrar, 2026-08-16 — the STANDING DEBT above is DISCHARGED, and its reasoning is corrected rather than carried forward.]** **The redeploy ran**: run **31873422229**, workflow `pages.yml`, event `workflow_dispatch`, `conclusion: success`, `run_attempt: 1`, `created_at 2026-08-15T08:01:00Z`, `updated_at 2026-08-15T08:02:50Z` — 1.8 min wall against 8.4 min for the first deploy (`31847839638`), which is the cold-versus-warm difference D136's own header measures. **`head_sha = 9317a35b3adaef56b03eaa22a80a2b76e232a7d9`**, corroborated by the origin's `last-modified: Sat, 15 Aug 2026 08:02:40 GMT`. **Measured against the live origin, not against a workflow step**, from this local host by `curl` and read-only `gh api` GETs: **2 516 397 B** — the post-remap size R25 predicted, no longer the 2 517 337 B pre-remap page — `sha256 ea7e9447584be131443a6948c70ea2f5a624882d6ea7d1f953e6c08d04f43951`; `content-type: text/html; charset=utf-8`; `cache-control: max-age=600`; `etag "6a801d20-2665ad"`; **no security header of any kind**, which is D62 §3 R6's whole reason the CSP lives in the artifact; and the identity and gzip fetches **decode to byte-identical bodies** (both 2 516 397 B, both `ea7e9447…3951`), so the host does not rewrite the body and is not disqualified by D63 §10 (iii). Site state (`GET repos/aed900/antseal/pages`): `cname antseal.org`, `build_type workflow`, `protected_domain_state verified`, `https_enforced true`, `https_certificate state=approved` expiring **2026-11-12**. **Re-measured by the registrar at the wave-21 close rather than transcribed**: `gh run list --workflow pages.yml --json databaseId,conclusion,headSha,createdAt,updatedAt` returns **exactly two runs, both `success`** — `31873422229` at `9317a35…` (08:01:00Z → 08:02:50Z, 1.83 min) and `31847839638` at `08c074c…` (2026-08-14T22:45:19Z → 22:53:43Z, 8.40 min) — which is the entire deploy history of this project, and is also why **R86's step has executed zero times remotely**: both runs predate it.
  **The old note's reasoning is wrong in a way that would mislead the next reader, and is corrected here rather than repeated.** It says the live bytes *"no longer reproduce from HEAD"*. **The module carries an `ANTSEAL_SOURCE_COMMIT` stamp** — D135 §1.5 measured that the stamp moves the module's bytes and not its length, `08c074c` and `9317a35` producing byte-differing modules of identical 1 853 031 B size with **no compile input changed between them** — so the footer digest is a function of **the commit**, and the live artifact reproduces from **`9317a35` and from no other commit**, including every commit this wave adds. The honest claim is *"reproducible at the commit it was deployed from"*, never *"reproducible from HEAD"*, and that is the form R25 and this row now use. The live footer reads `id="page-build">page build <code>70235b8b6192b983b1d1eb3b926e5e5925257cd806b6763eeb2aff76539403c0</code>` — the **module's** digest under a label saying "page", which is exactly the conflation **D136 §2 R12** repaired this wave in `verifier-web/index.template.html`, so the live page predates the repair and the next deploy carries it.
  **One thing a `curl` cannot see, recorded so no row claims it**: `id="build"` is **empty in the static bytes** (`id="build"></p>`) — the provenance line is filled by JS at run time from the module's build info. Any row claiming to have read the served provenance line is claiming something a static fetch cannot produce; only the browser arm can.

### R27 — Build the M3 end-to-end suite: CLI + headless-page verification, parity, offline and disagreement cases
- Milestone: M3
- Size: L
- Deps: R16, R21, R23, R24; Q: playwright job + local static server in CI (Q19); S: MockBackend/devnet fixtures; A: recorded anchor fixtures for pending/attested/proven states (A25)
- Spec: Verification — M3 bullet (line 174); M3 milestone (line 156)
- Do: Script the full M3 loop: seal a fixture work (library/devnet path), `reveal` a subset via the CLI, then (a) `antseal verify` offline asserting the expected verdict; (b) playwright loads the page from a local static server, drops the same bundle (setInputFiles), and asserts the rendered headline, per-anchor states, redaction view, and layer sections — string-equal to the CLI's R18 wording (parity gate); (c) an offline-context run (playwright network blocked) proving full verification incl. anchors completes with zero requests; (d) online-mode runs with playwright route interception on both CLI (mock HTTP) and page: endpoint agreement → promotion, and the endpoint-disagreement case → advisory disagreement, offline verdict untouched.
- Accept:
  - CI job green: reveal→CLI-verify and reveal→page-verify on the same bundle, verdict strings identical
  - Offline-context page run makes zero network requests and reaches a full verdict
  - Endpoint-disagreement case asserted on both CLI and page renderings
  - Suite covers a partial reveal (blackouts + placeholder file visible in the page DOM) and a full reveal with raw mirror
- Notes: **[D130, 2026-08-12]** The parity gate's subject is now well defined and its limits are stated. Both surfaces read **one assembly**: the page renders `verify_rendered`'s document, the CLI folds over the same `RenderedVerdict` / `RenderedRedaction`, so a string difference is a defect and not a rendering opinion. Three riders. **(i)** Parity holds over the R9 corpus and **must not be extended to hostile-path fixtures without excluding `redaction.files[].header_line`** — the terminal and DOM escapes neutralise different byte sets *by design* (D67 §3 R6), and measured, all nine vector paths are unchanged by either policy, so on this corpus the strings are equal. A hostile path belongs in each renderer's own suite asserting its own escape. **(ii)** The storage-linkage row is a known hazard: the export takes no options so the layer always runs (D128 §3 R5), while the committed vectors pin the **suppressed** tuple — compare against a native run under `VerifyOptions::new()` exactly as `scripts/wasm-boundary.mjs`'s header already explains, never against the vector file. **(iii)** Two boundary rows are owed with the fifth export: the document's members are all present, and **`verify_rendered(b).report` is byte-identical to `verify(b)`** — required because after D130 the page calls `verify_rendered` and not `verify`, so without it the gate's subject and the page's path diverge silently.
- Notes: **[D132, 2026-08-12]** The online cases are plan-driven, and **the committed corpus cannot serve them**. Route the playwright intercepts off `verify_rendered(b).plan` — `**/block-height/{H}` and `**/block/{hash}/header` per `plan.blocks` entry, the JSON-RPC POST when `plan.receipt !== null` — so the fixture and the intercepts cannot drift. **Measured over all 21 R9 vectors: every probe plan is `{"blocks":[],"receipt":null}`.** Twenty have no anchors at all; `multi-file-anchored/mixed`'s OTS anchor is offline **`invalid`**, so it can never promote; **zero** vectors carry a receipt. So *agreement/promotion*, *disagreement*, *mismatch→invalid*, *one-endpoint-down* and every receipt case need fixtures that **do not exist** — built the way `orchestration/tests.rs::attested_bundle()` builds one (R6 fixture, typed anchor substitution, re-encoded through `encode_bundle`, the `.ots` stamping the rebuilt bundle's own `anchor_digest`), plus a receipt-bearing twin. One case the corpus **can** serve and should: an online confirmation over any R9 vector makes **zero fetches** and still renders the overlay, identically on both surfaces — the cheapest proof that the plan-driven path invents no work. Two boundary rows are owed beside D130's: `plan.blocks` is an ascending duplicate-free integer array, and `plan.receipt` is `null` or an object whose `tx_hash` matches `/^[0-9a-f]{64}$/`. **Parity note**: after D132 both surfaces derive their probe set from one function, so a defect in it is a defect in **both** and is **invisible to a rendering-comparison gate** — the core equality test (`ProbePlan::from_bundle` ≡ what `probe_online` probes) is the only instrument that can see it and must be red-capable, not merely present.
- Notes: **[Registrar, 2026-08-12 — wave 19 closing. This row is BLOCKED, and the blocker is a fixture hole rather than effort.]** **R84** records it: no committed **bundle** fixture *verifies* to an `attested` OTS anchor — two of them carry the sealer-claimed `status: attested`, and the verifier renders every anchor in every committed bundle `Invalid` — so this row's `Do` clause (d) and Accept row 3 — endpoint agreement → promotion, and the endpoint-disagreement case — **cannot be written at all**, on either surface. That makes **R84 a gate blocker for Q237**, since the M3 gate's own clause list names the endpoint-disagreement case on both surfaces. The correction to D132 §9.2 above, recorded rather than applied silently: **`plan` itself IS fixtured** — both `every-anchor-kind-*` bundle cases carry an OTS upgrade group and the with-receipt twin carries a transaction hash, because `ProbePlan::from_bundle` reads the upgrade height **regardless of verdict** — so what is missing is only an **attested** anchor, not a populated plan. Until such a fixture exists, `plan.blocks` and *the attested-narrow set* are **indistinguishable by committed data**, which is precisely the difference D132 §1 (c) measured as inert; the inertness is proven by construction and not by the corpus.
- Notes: **[D133, 2026-08-14 — UNBLOCKED; R84 closed 2026-08-15.]** The fixture arrives as `target/verifier-web-fixtures/attested-ots-960767.sealproof`, materialised by `scripts/verifier-page-browser.sh` and asserted by `crates/antseal-core/tests/page_fixtures.rs`; a second, `attested-plus-invalid-ots.sealproof`, exhibits the wide-versus-narrow difference. **Route off `verify_rendered(b).plan`** (D132), never a hard-coded height — for the first fixture `{"blocks":[960767],"receipt":null}` — so fixture and intercepts cannot drift. **Interception is `Fetch.enable` + `Fetch.requestPaused` + `Fetch.fulfillRequest`/`failRequest`** over the existing CDP session: **there is no playwright in this repository and none is to be added** (D129 ruled the driver dependency-free), which is a correction to this row's own *"(R27 playwright routes)"* wording and to the M3 `Gate:` clause that inherits it. The four cases differ **only** in the mocked response, all of it real captured mainnet: both endpoints replay the committed 960767 captures (promotion); both return **960768's** real header (mismatch → `Refuted`, `anchor-ots-online-header-mismatch`); the two return different real headers (disagreement); one is failed (`NotPromoted{EndpointFailures}` — **never** a promotion on the survivor, D66). Using a real header from a different real block is deliberate: it exercises O6 with bytes an attacker could actually produce and needs no new capture. **Keep the zero-fetch row** over the existing R9 fixtures — it is the proof the plan-driven path invents no work, and it now has a non-empty counterpart.
  **Three obligations arrive with the unblocking.** (1) **D132's parity warning is not discharged by any of this**: both surfaces derive the probe set from one function, so a defect in `ProbePlan::from_bundle` is invisible to a rendering-comparison gate and the core equality row is the only instrument that can see it. (2) **`verifier-page-browser.mjs` asserts only `RESULT`/`FAILURE`/`TIMEOUT` per bundle** (D133 §8 item 6), so nothing yet distinguishes an `attested` render from any other verdict — the current twelve-bundle green must not be read as parity. (3) **The mismatch case's premise is enforced rather than remembered**: `assert_no_attested_anchor_at_the_inert_height` fails if any fixture ever becomes attested at **960768**, which would turn the mismatch header into a correct one. **D130 §7.3 (i)**: the parity comparison over these fixtures needs no `header_line` exclusion — they are not hostile-path fixtures.
- Notes: **[Registrar, 2026-08-16 — wave 21 closing. This row CLOSES, and it corrects three documents on the way, at their citations.]** **Evidence, and the machine.** `./scripts/verifier-page-browser.sh --check` drives **13 fixtures** and asserts **86 rows** — the baseline it replaces was 12 fixtures and 15 rows, tri-state only. Warm wall clock **37.9 s**; `--self-test` 19.1 s; `--seed-test` 24.6 s. **Machine: this local host.** The lane is `workflow_dispatch`-only (`.github/workflows/verifier-page.yml`, `on:` is that trigger and nothing else) and **has never run on a hosted runner**, so **Q237 must name the local host for every clause this row proves**. The native side of the parity comparison is `antseal verify <fixture>`'s own stdout, captured per fixture from a binary built in the same run rather than from a committed snapshot — deliberate, because both surfaces read one assembly (D130 §3 R3) and a frozen expectation file would turn every intentional wording change into a red gate with no key.
  **Correction 1 — the mismatch case does not render what two records promise, and it is not a fixture choice.** With **both** endpoints serving block 960768's real header against the embedded 960767 header, the rendered row is `ots-1: agreed online evidence contradicts the embedded header (anchor-ots-online-header-mismatch), but a pending attestation out-votes the refutation; recorded, not promoted` — `NotPromoted{RefutationSuppressed}`, class `outcome`, **not** `refuted`. The artifact is A25's **merged** `.ots`, so it carries evaluable **pending** calendar branches and `classify_ots`'s **O5 out-ranks O6/O7**. The frozen corpus had already recorded the mechanism: `ots-upgraded-online-block-absent` is pinned at `state: pending, suppressed: ["anchor-ots-online-block-absent"]`. **`testdata/vectors/v1/anchor/anchor.json` holds exactly ONE upgraded OTS artifact**, so no committed material can drive an agreed refutation to `invalid` at the bundle layer. Three sites are wrong as written and are corrected at their citations rather than restated: **D133 §3 R8**'s perturbation table (its `mismatch → invalid` outcome word) and **D133 §5.2** (its `overlay row `Refuted` with code `anchor-ots-online-header-mismatch``) — both carrying a dated correction section on that record; **this row's own D132 note above**, whose list of cases needing fixtures names *"mismatch→invalid"*, and **this row's D133 note above**, whose case table says *"mismatch → `Refuted`"* — both false as predictions and true only as descriptions of what D133 expected; and the **M3 `Gate:` line's** clause that inherits them, which **Q237** carries. The lane asserted what D56 actually promises — the code reaches the rendered row and **nothing is promoted** — plus a differential row proving *"carries the code"* is not true of every non-promoted outcome. The product half is **R88**.
  **Correction 2 — D130's parity comparison as stated is not the one that holds.** The sentence is at **D130 §5, `### R27 / Q19 — the parity gate`, clause 1** (*"string for string after the CLI's indentation is stripped"*) — **not** at §7.3, which is where this row's own D133 note and D133 §5.2 both cite it and where no such clause exists. Indentation is not the only difference: the CLI prints `"{slot}: {state_line}{tag}"` as **one** line (`crates/antseal-cli/src/verify_out.rs:156`) while the page puts `"{slot} {tag}"` in one `<p>` and `state_line` in the next. **A lane taking that sentence literally builds a gate that reddens on a correct page.** Corrected at D130.
  **Correction 3 — the twelve-bundle green was VACUOUS for bundles 2–12, and that retroactively qualifies two waves.** `dropBundle` polled *"is `#result` hidden?"*, but the page unhides `#result` on the first success and **never re-hides it**, so from the second drop the exit condition was already satisfied by the **previous** bundle's DOM. Proven by measurement rather than by reading: the first parity run reported one fixture's row against another fixture's anchor block. Fixed with an explicit reset before each `setFileInputFiles` — which is why the arm got slower: **it now actually waits**. Wave 19's and wave 20's *"twelve bundles rendering in Chromium"* claims should be read as **one bundle witnessed, not twelve**.
  **Correction 4 — R10/R12/R13/R14 belong to Q19, not to this row.** **D136 §2 R15** assigns them, and the wave's own brief assigned them here. They were implemented — they live in `scripts/verifier-page-browser.mjs`, which only this lane could write — but **R27 may not tick on them**; they are **Q19's** and **R25 Accept row 3's**. D136 §2 R15 is explicit in both directions: Q19 may not tick on *"R27's suite is green"*, and R27 may not tick on *"the lane runs"*.
  **Correction 5 — the browser lane's recorded marginal cost.** D136 §2 R9 priced it at **~12.5 s**. The honest warm figure after this row is **~38 s**, and the increase is not overhead: it is the driver waiting for 13 real verifications instead of racing past them (Correction 3), plus 5 browser sessions for the online cases. **Re-recorded, not re-decided** — the ruling that the lane rides `local-gate.sh` is untouched, and the correction is at D136.
  **Two smaller ones.** `.github/workflows/verifier-page.yml:134` says *"the **twelve** materialised bundles"* — it is now **thirteen**. And `MVP-SPEC.md` line 139's *"the page footer displays **its own** build hash"* remains the source of the page/module conflation D136 §2 R12 repaired; the spec is **frozen**, so this is **flagged at Q237, never edited**.
  **Three residues recorded on the row rather than lost.** (1) The **`[H]` headline-tag branch of the parity extractor is unexercised** — no committed fixture renders a headline-eligible anchor offline, so `"{slot}: {state}[H]"` has never been compared. (2) A **second options divergence D130 does not name**: `crates/antseal-cli/src/commands.rs:659` builds `VerifyOptions::new().with_verify_at_unix(now)` while the export passes `VerifyOptions::new()` (`verify_at_unix: None`), reaching the TSA chain validator. Inert on this corpus — every committed TSA artifact is an opaque placeholder and 13/13 render identically — but a fixture with a real TSA token would put a clock on one side only. Documented at the site. (3) The CLI-side core equality row (`crates/antseal-cli/src/verify_host/tests.rs`, 4 tests green) was **read, not fault-planted**: planting needs a write outside the lane's scope. The page-surface equivalent **was** built and proven red-capable.

### R28 — Keep verification working across all released format versions (page + pipeline)
- Milestone: continuous
- Size: S
- Deps: R9, R23; F: per-version codec dispatch + version vectors (F10); Q: indefinite CI retention of per-version vectors (Q6)
- Spec: Format stability (line 123); Verification — vectors retained forever (line 167)
- Do: For every released manifest/bundle format version, extend the R9 vector manifest with that version's bundles → expected reports, and keep a CI job running `verify_bundle` (native + wasm32) and the hosted-page verification path over the full historical set. The page advertises and accepts all released versions; a release checklist item forbids shipping a version bump without its vectors.
- Accept:
  - CI fails if any released version's vectors are missing, altered, or fail on CLI, wasm32, or the page path
  - Release checklist (with Q) includes the per-version vector gate
  - Page displays the verified bundle's format version for every historical vector

### R29 — Retire R5's hand-built smoke fixture in favour of R6's constructor
- Milestone: M0
- Size: S
- Deps: R6 (landed)
- Spec: Verification (lines 167–169) — one source of truth for "what a valid bundle looks like"
- Do: `verify::pipeline::tests::fixture` is ~280 lines of hand-built work that predates R6 and, by its own module comment, lives outside `test_util` deliberately so "nothing outside R5 can depend on it and R6 has nothing to unpick". R6 now exists and covers a strict superset of its shapes (its three-file work *is* R6's `shapes::multi_file`). Decide whether to (a) rewrite R5's tests against R6's shapes and delete the hand-built fixture, or (b) keep it as a deliberate independent construction and add an assertion that the two agree. Either is defensible; what is not defensible is leaving two unrelated definitions of a valid bundle drifting apart, since a bug that reached both would be invisible to both.
- Accept:
  - One of the two options implemented, with the reasoning recorded
  - If (b): a test asserting R5's fixture and the corresponding R6 shape produce the same verification report shape (not the same bytes — the RNG seeds differ by design)
  - R5's stage-order pins keep working unchanged either way; they are the reason the fixture exists and must not be weakened to make the merge easier
- Notes: Surfaced at R6/R7 (M0 wave 5). Low risk, and cheapest to do before R9's vectors bind either fixture's bytes.
- Rider (R9): R9 landed and binds **R6's** bundle bytes (`bundle_sha256`) and the reports derived from them, for 21 shapes. It does not touch R5's fixture, so neither option is blocked and the cost of R29 is unchanged — but the asymmetry it warned about is now concrete: one of the two definitions of "a valid bundle" is a frozen, CI-enforced artifact and the other is unpinned hand-built code. A bug reaching both is still invisible to both, and the frozen half now lends it false authority. Option (b) got cheaper in one respect (the R6 side of the agreement assertion is a committed artifact, not just live code) and option (a) got slightly dearer (deleting R5's fixture must not disturb the stage-order pins that R9's vectors do *not* cover — R9 pins outputs, R5 pins the order they are produced in). Verdict: more urgent, not more expensive.

### R30 — Assert the report's native↔wasm32 byte-match over R6-constructed bundles
- Milestone: M0
- Size: S
- Deps: R6 (landed), R9; Q: the wasm32 execution lane (P14/Q5, landed)
- Spec: Verification — WASM bit-match (line 169); M0 milestone (line 153)
- Do: R6 is `test-vectors`-gated precisely so the wasm32 lane can build bundles *in-process*, and today the wasm32 lane runs its unit tests (including R6's own) but nothing asserts that a report serialized on wasm32 is byte-identical to the native one for the same input. R9 will assert this over *committed* vectors; this asserts it over *constructed* ones, which covers shapes no vector file pins and costs nothing extra to run. Add a test that, for each named R6 shape, serializes the `VerificationReport` canonically and compares against a value the native lane computed — or, more cheaply, compares a digest of the serialized report against a committed per-shape constant that both lanes check.
- Accept:
  - Every named R6 shape has a report digest asserted on both native and wasm32
  - A shape whose bytes change fails both lanes with the same message, so a drift is diagnosed once rather than twice
  - No committed artifact carries secret material beyond what the bundle legitimately discloses (R6's existing hygiene assertion extends to it)
- Notes: Surfaced at R6 (M0 wave 5). Deliberately *not* folded into R9: R9 owns the committed-vector contract and its retention policy, whereas this is an in-process property with no artifact to retain. Sequence it after R9 so the report serialization it digests is the frozen one.
- Rider (R9): R9's committed vectors now assert exactly this over 21 of R6's shapes — every named catalogue entry except the three the vector deliberately omits. R30's remaining value is therefore the *constructed* half: it should assert over `shapes::catalogue()` directly, so a shape added to R6 after R9's file was frozen is covered from the moment it exists rather than at the next re-emit.
