### R59 — Decide R11's final home and build its bundle-shaped half
- Milestone: M3
- Size: S
- Deps: R11, R21; D34
- Spec: Core user flows — verify `--live` (line 38); Storage-linkage layer (line 119)
- Do: R11's M1 half landed in `antseal-net::live::manifest` — the only crate that can see both `StorageBackend` and the manifest types, and a shape adapter rather than one of the *drivers* D34 sends to the `antseal_cli` lib (argued in R11's execution note). At M3, when R21 lands in the cli lib, take one of two paths deliberately: (a) build the bundle-shaped adapter through the public `StorageRecord::new` extension point and keep the split — net owns "how to check", the cli lib owns "what to check"; or (b) relocate the module into the cli lib beside R21. Either way, produce the bundle-shaped constructor (a bundle's embedded ciphertexts + its encrypted-manifest record → `Vec<StorageRecord>`) that R11 deliberately deferred.
- Accept:
  - The placement is a recorded decision, not a default — with D34 re-read against the shape R21 actually takes
  - The bundle-shaped adapter exists and is covered by R6-constructed fixtures; a relocation, if chosen, moves no behaviour and changes no cross-crate contract
  - `--live` over a bundle covers unit ciphertexts **and** the encrypted-manifest blob (R11 accept row 3, at bundle shape)
- Notes: Discovered by R11 (lane θ) 2026-08-02. The M1 placement was made under a concurrent-lane constraint on `crates/antseal-cli`; that constraint must not be mistaken for the argument, which stands on its own (a shape adapter is not a driver). — **[2026-08-12, registrar]** This row's `Do` opens *"At M3, when R21 lands in the cli lib"*, and **R21 did not land in the cli lib**: it landed in `antseal-core` under D69 §3 R4 and R22's binding needs (reconciled in full on R21's row and Notes). So **arm (b) — *"relocate the module into the cli lib beside R21"* — no longer names a reachable location**, since core cannot see `StorageBackend` and R21 is not in the cli lib to be beside. Arm (a) is the surviving arm on the *"beside R21"* argument alone; the cli lib is still a defensible home on its own merits (it is where D34 sends the driver, and where U30's wiring lives), but that case must now be made rather than inherited. Take this together with **R80**, which owes the same missing conversion at the report shape for the identical cycle reason.

## Open decisions (R)
- Verifier-page host + domain (one canonical URL) — **[2026-08-12] RESOLVED (D62)**: `https://antseal.org/` on GitHub Pages from the private repo (Pro-enabled), constant in `antseal-cli`, decoded-body hash check with a two-encoding tripwire, `.dev` refused (docs/decisions/D62-verifier-page-host-and-canonical-url.md). **Consumer list corrected**: the constant is owned by **R26** and consumed by **U28** (the only Rust consumer) and **Q22/Q28** (docs) — *not* by R16, which disclaims it in its own `Do` (`tasks/R.md:198`), and *not* by R25, whose subject is the build, the SHA-256SUMS and the footer.
- Footer build-hash mechanism (build-time injection into HTML vs runtime self-hash of the fetched wasm) and exactly which artifact set the published SHA-256 covers — blocks R25; by M3. — **[2026-08-12]** RESOLVED (**D63**): **build-time injection**; the footer digest is SHA-256 of the deployed `.wasm` **as loaded** (64 ungrouped lowercase hex, never truncated), the identity fields come from R22's module export, and the advice line is the spec's verbatim sentence. The **published `SHA256SUMS` covers the complete served closure** (`index.html` post-injection + every subresource, content-decoded, byproducts deleted) and is authoritative **only** in its signed-release copy. The chicken-and-egg is cut by using **two different sets**: the footer names the one artifact that cannot contain it; the manifest sits outside its own list and covers everything. Runtime self-hash refused (dies under `file://`, defends nothing); the **dichotomy is half overturned** — it is a real choice for the digest and a false framing for the footer (docs/decisions/D63-footer-build-hash-and-artifact-set.md).
- `verify_bundle` error mode: fail-fast single distinct error (tamper-matrix authoritative) vs collect-all for rendering, and which the report exposes — blocks R1/R5/R7; by M0. — **[2026-07-27]** RESOLVED (D27): hybrid — fail-fast typed first-error (`Result<VerificationReport, VerifyError>`) is the sole normative mode the tamper matrix/Q7 bind to; `VerifyFailures` is rendering-only collection, primary-first by construction, first element must equal the fail-fast error (docs/decisions/D27-verify-error-mode.md).
- Confirm the derived strictness rule: a bundle revealing all of a file's non-mirror units MUST carry `file_salt` (+ `s_root` when a fine tree exists) or hard-fail (`FullRevealMaterialMissing`) — blocks R4/R7; by M0 (format-freeze relevant).
- Raw-mirror inclusion on whole-file reveals: always automatic (implemented default) vs a future opt-out — blocks R13/R16 final UX; by M3. — **[2026-08-11]** RESOLVED (D70): automatic; no reservation minted for the opt-out (the mirror-less full-reveal shape is already frozen verifier tolerance); both mirror ⟷ full-reveal directions ruled builder guarantees (docs/decisions/D70-raw-mirror-auto-include.md).
- Online-overlay ↔ headline presentation: exact layout for how a `--online`-promoted anchor supplies the headline while the offline cryptographic verdict stays distinct and visible — blocks R17/R18 (snapshots freeze it); by M3.
- `--json` report schema stability commitment (is the report schema versioned/stable for scripting from M3?) — blocks R21; by M3. — **[2026-08-12]** RESOLVED (**D65**): the blanket promise is overturned and replaced by a **three-tier partition per member** — tier A `result.report` (already frozen, carried **byte-verbatim**, 21 vectors + bit-match), tier B the envelope wrapper (stable-additive from M3 under `ENVELOPE_VERSION`), tier C everything else inside `result` (reviewed, unpromised until U32). The question's noun was wrong: `verify --json` is **not** the report, it is an envelope of members (D64 §6). `REPORT_VERSION` is never a `--json` schema version. **R21's constraint**: hand U30 the report's **bytes**, never a parsed `serde_json::Value` — `serde_json::Map` is a `BTreeMap` in this build, so a round trip alphabetizes the report and destroys D29 rule 1's wire order (docs/decisions/D65-json-schema-stability-commitment.md).
- CORS viability of the pinned esplora/Arbitrum endpoints from browsers (fallback/override policy if a default blocks browser fetch) — blocks R24; by M3. — **[2026-08-12]** RESOLVED (**D66**): all six pinned endpoints are browser-usable and no default changes, but **conditionally and for opposite reasons** — esplora has no `OPTIONS` route (preflight 404s), so the page must stay **preflight-free** (`GET`, at most `Accept: text/plain`, `credentials: 'omit'`, `mode: 'cors'`); Arbitrum's preflight is mandatory and works. Measured in Chromium against the real `blockstream.info`: succeeds with the safelisted header, throws with one extra — a curl probe reads `ACAO: *` either way. A thrown fetch is `Transport`, never `NoSuchBlock` (fail-closed by construction), and the rendered line **may not name any cause**, because four causes produce one indistinguishable `TypeError`. No promotion on a surviving endpoint, no silent third endpoint; overrides stay and gain A49's `https://`-only rule. **A44 gains an esplora arm** — it had none (docs/decisions/D66-browser-endpoint-cors-and-fallback-policy.md).
- Disclosure-preview snippet length and binary-snippet format — blocks R15; by M3 (small).

## Cross-domain expectations
- F: strict RFC-8949-canonical bundle/manifest codec with typed structs, version dispatch, size/count/depth caps, empty-anchor support, encode API usable by R6/R13, raw-CBOR mutation helpers for tamper fixtures, and per-version vector layout.
- C: AEAD open/seal (XChaCha20-Poly1305, AAD), HKDF label derivations (`k_u`, `unit_salt`, `path_salt`, `file_salt`, `k_m`), salted commitment recomputation (`unit_commit`/`path_commit`/`canon_commit`/`raw_commit`), strict hybrid Ed25519+ML-DSA verification with full `sig_policy` enforcement, distinct signature-error variants, and the "hybrid (PQ)" vs "Ed25519-only" scheme datum; C owns the signature/`sig_policy` tamper rows run through my harness.
- G: `canonicalize_v` pinned to descriptor-recorded Unicode versions; leaf-exact GGM sub-cover + boundary Merkle path computation (builder side); range verification (salt derivation from cover, leaf rebuild, boundary-path check to `fine_root`) with over-broad/non-leaf-exact cover rejection; full fine-tree rebuild from `s_root` + bytes; the n=6 unbalanced and leaf-exact-cover M0 fixtures.
- S: deterministic BLAKE3-256 address recomputation (S4, per D32/D35 — one blob = one chunk, `blake3` alone) as a WASM-safe `antseal-core` function; `StorageBackend::get_data` + `MockBackend`; devnet fixtures for R27; the M1 devnet E2E itself (which may call R11); the S15 persistence primitive under R11/R21.
- A: per-anchor offline state machine over all 7 states with verified times and artifact/fetch-date metadata (WASM-safe); anchor tamper rows (M2); online-confirmation primitives — must-agree pinned endpoint pairs (esplora ×2, Arbitrum RPC ×2), WASM-safe header-match/promotion core consumable from browser-fetched responses; pinned endpoint defaults; anchor artifact types stored in the vault; the opportunistic-upgrade hook invoked before reveal.
- U: clap surfaces for `reveal`/`verify` (flags `--all/--units/-o/--include-receipt/--yes/--online/--live`), confirmation-prompt + `--yes` utility, vault read APIs (manifest, `W`/derivation handle, staged ciphertexts, receipts, anchor artifacts, paths, addresses), canonical-URL printing in `reveal` output, `show` consuming R15's preview function, global `--json` plumbing.
- Q: CI infra — wasm32 build + test execution from day one, snapshot-test tooling, playwright job + static server, cargo-fuzz jobs, indefinite per-version vector retention; page release signing (minisign/cosign) + publication channel; docs carrying the canonical URL; host/domain decision partnership.
- P: workspace scaffolding for `verifier-web/` and the wasm-bindgen crate; exact toolchain pins (rustc, wasm-pack, wasm-bindgen) required for the reproducible build; product-name/domain reservation feeding the canonical URL.

### R60 — R54's linear-time guard is contention-sensitive, so a required lane is unreliable when the machine is busy
- Milestone: M0
- Size: S
- Deps: after R54
- Discovered by: 2026-08-02 (the row names no discovering lane).
- Problem: `verify_scan_shape::at_cap_no_reveal_bundle_verifies_in_linear_time` (`crates/antseal-core/tests/verify_scan_shape.rs`) measured **3.22x** against its 3.0x threshold at machine load 3–4, and sat comfortably under it at load 0.6. Its premise — *"machine speed cancels between the two measurements"* — holds only if contention is **equal** across both wall-clock measurements, which is false under the parallel-lane pattern this project actually uses. So a required lane is unreliable exactly when the machine is busy, which is exactly when it runs.
- Do: **rule which of the row's three options governs** — count work (iterations) rather than wall clock; serialize the guard; or widen it with a documented load precondition — and record the reason where the guard is read, because the next person to see it red will be deciding whether to believe it under load.
- Accept:
  - The ruling is recorded at the guard itself with its reason, and the premise sentence that is now known to be conditional is corrected rather than left standing.
  - The guard is **shown to still fail** on a genuinely superlinear scan after the change — whichever option is taken, the instrument must be demonstrated red against a planted quadratic, or the row has traded a flaky check for a blind one. R54's own subject is a scan whose cost is not bounded, and a guard that cannot see it is worse than none.
  - If the widen-with-precondition option is taken, the precondition is **machine-checkable or refused**: a documented load bound nobody measures is the same defect one level up.
  - The measured pair the row records — 3.22x at load 3–4, under threshold at load 0.6 — is reproduced or superseded by a fresh measurement, so the ruling rests on numbers rather than on the recollection of them.
- Notes: the row's standing operational advice — *"if a gate goes red here, check load average before believing it"* — is the workaround this row exists to retire, and it should be deleted from the row when the ruling lands, not left as a second answer. R57 gives `proof_unit_refs`' tautological arm "the R54 treatment" and inherits whatever is decided here; a ruling that does not say whether it binds R57 will be re-asked by it.
- **Entry authored 2026-08-10 under Q134 from `TODO.md`'s R60 row alone — no other source existed.** Transcribed from the row: `Milestone` (its M0 section — the row sits directly beneath R54 in "Verification pipeline core (R)"), `Size`, `Deps`, `Discovered by`, the `Problem` including both measurements, and the three options. **Authored here, not transcribed: the whole `Do`, the whole `Accept` and the `Notes`** — the row offers a menu of three and makes no choice, and names no acceptance criterion. Per D115 §3.6 the `Do` is to make the choice, and this entry deliberately does **not** make it. The `Accept`'s load-bearing clause — that the guard be shown red against a planted quadratic whichever option wins — is authored, and is the one thing all three options can be checked against. The test name was confirmed present in `crates/antseal-core/tests/verify_scan_shape.rs` on 2026-08-10; no `Spec` field appears because the row names no spec line, no decision and no doc section.

### R61 — Render the suppressed-anomaly list, and pin the CLI/page wording asymmetry it creates
- Milestone: M3
- Size: S
- Deps: R18 (the wording table); A39 (the datum); R17 (aggregation)
- Discovered by: **D53 §6 / D56 §4** (2026-08-02).
- Problem: two coupled gaps. (1) A39's suppressed-anomaly list has no wording, so the information best-evidence-wins removes from the anchor state has nowhere to surface. (2) R18's premise — *"the one authoritative wording table in `antseal-core` (shared verbatim by CLI and page — renderers receive final strings, never compose their own)"* — assumes both renderers see the same data. They do not: the CLI holds `AnchorVerdicts`, while R22 hands the **page** only the report's canonical bytes, and report v1 is frozen with no field for anomalies (`AnchorResult` has five fields; D29/R32, Q14). So there is exactly one class of datum the CLI can render and the page cannot, and R18's snapshot set would silently encode that asymmetry as "wording we happened not to write".
- Spec: Verifier web page — advisory overlay distinct from the offline verdict (MVP-SPEC.md line 137); Verification M3 (lines 156, 174)
- Do: Add the anomaly wording to R18's table — one line per suppressed refutation, framed as *advisory* and explicitly subordinate to the anchor's state ("this anchor is `proven`; one certificate path in it did not verify") so it can never read as a demotion. State the CLI/page asymmetry in the wording table's own header as a **declared** divergence with the report-version reason, and pin it with a test rather than leaving it as an omission. **[D64, 2026-08-11]**: the online overlay's declared non-string surfaces join the same header — page chrome only (the activation affordance, page CSS, the busy affordance; D64 §5) — every other overlay surface is shared-verbatim strings from R18's table.
- Accept:
  - Every `AnchorAnomaly` code has a rendering; an exhaustive-enumeration test fails when a new code lacks one (the R18 pattern).
  - A snapshot test pins the CLI rendering of a `proven`-with-suppressed-anomaly anchor, and a second asserts the **page** binding for the identical bundle contains none of that wording — so the asymmetry is asserted, not assumed.
  - The advisory lines never appear in the headline, and never change the state string (test over a fixture with anomalies on a headline-eligible anchor).
  - Positioning review: no "forged", no "notary", no unqualified accusation — the artifact may be an honest bundle a relay appended to.
- Notes: the fix that removes the asymmetry is a `report_version: 2` field, which is a deliberate format event (see `report.rs`'s `REPORT_VERSION` doc for the coupled-edit list). Do not take it as a side effect of this task.

### R67 — Retire the M0 anchor-inertness equality; it was never able to report red
- Milestone: M2
- Size: S
- Deps: R12 (D94)
- Discovered by: **the D94 planner** (2026-08-06).
- Problem: `m0_anchor_artifacts_are_inert_until_r12_wires_the_anchor_stage` is named in **five** committed places — D84 §2, `docs/security-assumptions.md`, the Q14 freeze-gate plan's row N1 (which calls it *"the row's own load-bearing evidence … it pins, as an equality, that no verdict depends on artifact internals"*), `bundle_mutators.rs`'s `GraftAnchors`, and the test itself — as the test **written to go red when R12 lands, with the instruction to invert it**. It cannot. Its whole assertion is `assert_eq!(drive(&mutated), Outcome::Verified)`, and `drive` discards the report and returns one bit: did the bundle verify. D84's own rule **F2** guarantees that bit never moves. Two propositions were conflated: *P1, no anchor byte changes accept/reject* (true forever, and what the test measures) and *P2, no verdict depends on artifact internals* (M0-only, and measured by nothing, ever).
- Do: keep the test and **re-title it as the permanent F2 guard**. Do not invert it — F2 forbids what the inversion would assert, and with the fixture's synthetic bytes every flip yields `invalid` either way, so no report-level assertion is satisfiable there. Correct all five sites so none still predicts a red that cannot happen. P2's successor is A21 rows 1–2 against A25's real material.
- Accept:
  - The test's name and doc state what it measures (F2, the accept/reject bit) and what it does not (any verdict value).
  - All five sites agree, checked by grep rather than by memory; none still says "invert at R12".
  - A planted F2 breach — an anchor byte that changes accept/reject — turns it red.

### R68 — The report-pin assertions offer two causes and the true one is a third
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: R12 (D94)
- Discovered by: **the D94 planner** (2026-08-06).
- Do: both pinned-report assertions tell the reader that *"exactly one of two things happened: the report byte format changed … or this target diverged from the other"*. When R12 lands, neither is true: the format is frozen and unchanged, the targets agree, and a **recomputed verdict** moved. A message that enumerates causes and omits the real one sends the next reader to the wrong remedy — re-pinning as a format event, or hunting a divergence that is not there. Add the third class.
- Accept: both messages name FORMAT / VERDICT / FIXTURE events and point at D94; the vocabulary matches Q107's.

### R69 — A new report value landed post-freeze with nothing red
- Milestone: M2
- Size: S
- Deps: R12
- Discovered by: **the D94 planner** (2026-08-06).
- Problem: R12 adds `SupportingEvidenceResult::ArbitrumReceipt` — a **new value in report v1's value space, after the freeze** — and no committed artifact renders it, while the storage-linkage slot's doc comment still calls its own future arm *"a pre-Q14 extension per D29"*. Nothing goes red if either is wrong.
- Do: Add a receipt-bearing twin of `fully_populated_report()` to `crates/antseal-core/src/verify/mod.rs`'s test module, with a sibling `EXPECTED_CANONICAL_JSON_WITH_RECEIPT` and snapshot test, and sweep it for secrets alongside the control. Reuse `report.rs`'s receipt operands via shared `#[cfg(test)] pub(crate)` consts so the standalone and composed pins move together. **A committed vector is refused, not merely costly** (D105 §5.1): a 22nd case in `testdata/vectors/v1/report/verification-reports.json` dies on `vector-freeze.sh`'s first verdict-event check (*"the case count moved (21 -> 22)"*), and a second `report` document dies on `check_shape_coverage`'s per-document demand for all 13 `REQUIRED_SHAPES`. The instrument is D29's own fixed-fixture snapshot (Consequences bullet 1). Also correct `report.rs`'s `StorageLinkageResult` doc — located by **symbol**, per the 2026-08-11 correction below; this clause read *"(live line **`:299`**, not `:226-231`)"*, and `:299` has since rotted the same way `:226-231` did — per D105 §3, and R12's `:482-484` field-list phrasing per D105 §9 edit 2.
- Accept: The D29 fixed-fixture snapshot renders `arbitrum-receipt` **in composition**, on native and wasm32; the twin differs from the control by exactly +63 B at `supporting_evidence` and nowhere else, asserted literal-against-literal so it cannot be satisfied by transcribing serializer output; deleting the arm or changing its fields reddens it; `vector-freeze.sh` reports `GREEN` and 0 `REPORT_DIGEST_BY_SHAPE` rows move.
- **`Do` and `Accept` REPLACED 2026-08-09 by [D105](../docs/decisions/D105-post-freeze-report-value-additions.md); the Accept as written was unsatisfiable.** It read: *"Commit a vector that opts a receipt in, and correct the doc comment."* / *"a committed case renders the receipt arm; deleting the arm or changing its fields reddens it; the doc comment describes what shipped."* The vector it required cannot be committed — **not expensively, at all**. Route A (a 22nd case in the frozen `report` document) trips `vector-freeze.sh`'s append-only path, whose one exception refuses on its **first** check: *"the case count moved (21 -> 22); a verdict event re-values existing cases and adds none"*. It is not a format event and not a fixture event, so **no legal class exists for it**. Route B (a second `report` document) trips `check_shape_coverage`, which runs **per document** and demands all 13 `REQUIRED_SHAPES`. Neither this row's Accept, nor D94 §11's R69 sketch, nor the recon had read either gate. The right instrument was named by D29 in 2026-07-27 and has been sitting in `verify/mod.rs` ever since, hand-built (which is why it can still render `AnchorState::Absent`, a state the pipeline no longer emits) and already enumerating all seven anchor states in one document — one slot short of the whole value space.
- **Stale citation corrected 2026-08-09 (D105 §7 drift 1):** the scope correction below cited `crates/antseal-core/src/verify/report.rs:226-231` for the `StorageLinkageResult` claim; the live line is **`:299`**. ~~`D94:506` cites `:365-366` for the same claim and is **not corrected — a ruled record is immutable**; the drift is recorded here.~~ — **Rule withdrawn 2026-08-11 (`Q187`).** D117 RULING 1 **refuses** *"a ruled record is immutable"*: a resolved decision's body takes dated corrections, and only a **silent** change is forbidden. D94's citation — in its §11 `R69` bullet — is still uncorrected, but no longer for that reason: it is outside `Q187`'s scope, which struck the A22 Accept-row count in the same record's `Q109` bullet, and D117 §2.3 (e) puts a correction with the lane that measures it. **Whoever takes it must re-measure first; three things have moved since 2026-08-09, all confirmed 2026-08-11.** (a) The quoted phrase is no longer a live claim anywhere — `report.rs` records only that the slot *said* it *"until D105"* — so there is nothing to restate, only a withdrawal to record. (b) **`:299` is itself stale**, and points into a signature-verdict doc comment. (c) The stale locator has drifted onto a **different symbol**: at D94's own commit `:365-366` was the receipt arm's doc, the thing that bullet is about; today those two lines are `StorageLinkageResult`'s slot doc, so a reader following the citation lands somewhere plausible and finds no error at all. D117 RULING 6 forbids swapping one line locator for another, so the repair is a citation by **symbol** — which is why this is left as measured work rather than done in passing.
- **Scope correction, 2026-08-06 (measured at R12's landing):** the receipt arm's own doc comment *was* corrected by R12, but the claim it carried is **not unique to it**. `StorageLinkageResult` (`crates/antseal-core/src/verify/report.rs:299`) says the same thing about **R20 at M3** — *"until then the slot reports not-evaluated — a pre-Q14 extension per D29"* — and Q14 executed on 2026-07-28, so R20 will add a post-freeze variant to a serialized enum for exactly the same reason, one milestone later. Whatever this task rules for `SupportingEvidenceResult` must be applied to `StorageLinkageResult` in the same edit, or R20 arrives at an unruled question a second time. Measured: **no committed vector renders `arbitrum-receipt`**, so the value that shipped at R12 is still exercised by nothing.
- **This entry cited `:299` three times and declared it stale once — corrected 2026-08-11 (wave-15 A/R bookkeeping).** The 2026-08-11 withdrawal bullet above establishes that **`:299` is itself stale** and *"points into a signature-verdict doc comment"*; the `Do` and the 2026-08-06 scope correction went on using it as the live locator for `StorageLinkageResult`, so one entry asserted and denied the same fact. **Re-measured here and the withdrawal bullet is right on all three counts.** `report.rs:299` is a line of the doc comment for the `sig_policy` verdict variant that exists because `PolicyLabel::Other` is reachable — a signature-policy doc, nothing to do with storage linkage. `StorageLinkageResult`'s type-level doc and its enum are both **elsewhere in the same file**, cited here by symbol and not by line, because D117 RULING 6 forbids swapping one line locator for another and this entry has now been bitten by that exactly once. **The quoted phrase is also gone**: *"until then the slot reports not-evaluated — a pre-Q14 extension per D29"* survives nowhere as a live claim — `report.rs` now records only that the slot's doc *said* it *"until D105"* — so the 2026-08-06 measurement's subject no longer exists to be corrected, only its history to be recorded. **That dated measurement is left standing rather than rewritten**: it was true when taken, and what has changed is the file, not the finding. **And the repair the `Do` asks for reads as already discharged**, which no bullet of this entry says: `StorageLinkageResult`'s type-level doc now carries a heading recording that the slot *"said … until **D105**"* and that the claim was *"false when read and false when written down"*, so the correction landed with D105 and the `Do` has gone on asking for it since 2026-08-09. Re-adjudicate that clause before executing it rather than repairing a doc that already reads correctly. **Its companion locator has rotted the same way and is not re-adjudicated here**: the `Do`'s *"R12's `:482-484` field-list phrasing"* now lands on two `AnchorState` variant doc comments, which are not a field list. **Still not this entry's to fix**: D94 §11's `R69` bullet carries the same rot at `:365-366`, where the citation has drifted onto a **different symbol** so a reader following it finds nothing wrong; it is outside `Q187`'s scope and unowned, and it is reported up rather than repaired here.
- Notes: D105 §9's edit set also carries a third doc repair this task owns — `report.rs:478`'s heading *"# Adding this arm moved no pinned byte"* gains a sentence recording that **zero moved pins is a correct *format* signal and an empty *coverage* signal**, which is the whole of why this row exists: the reassurance and the gap were one sentence. The general rule the gap implies (**R-VAL**, and making the report's enums an enumerable set) is **Q127**, not this row. One slip in the ruling itself, found in execution and recorded rather than propagated: D105 §9's gate text demands `vector-freeze.sh` report `unchanged`, a string that exists only in its `--update` branch — the bare run prints `GREEN`, which is what the Accept asks for.

### R73 — The report's sealer-claim surface is documented inconsistently
- Milestone: M2
- Size: S
- Deps: D95
- Discovered by: **the D95 planners** (2026-08-06).
- Problem: three separate defects in one surface. (1) `AnchorResult::fetch_date`'s doc cited *"MVP-SPEC.md line 127+"* for a rendering requirement that passage does not contain — `fetch date` appears at lines 108 and 114 only, both as bundle content (**fixed at landing**). (2) The `WorkMetadata` group over-claimed in **two** docs, not one, and the count this entry carried was wrong. Re-measured at execution: `WorkMetadata` has **six** children (`crates/antseal-core/src/verify/report.rs:159-183`), of which exactly **one** is a verifier statement — `work_id` (`:163`), recomputed over the embedded body bytes. `format_version` (`:168`) is decoder-enforced rather than reproduced (a body declaring any other version dies as `ManifestError::UnsupportedFormatVersion` at `crates/antseal-core/src/manifest/body.rs:1298`, and the reported value is the accepted constant from `body.rs:1180`); `title` (`:166`), `app_version` (`:170`) and `claimed_time_informational_only` (`:178`) are sealer text; `signature_scheme` (`:182`) is a stage that has not run, `NotEvaluated` until C14 — neither a claim nor a verdict, and the child this entry did not count at all. So the *sealer-claim* figure of three survived re-measurement; the *child* figure did not, and the over-claim covered five of six. The two docs: `VerificationReport::work`'s **field** doc read *"Work-level metadata as verified from the bundle"* (`report.rs:119`) — the string this entry and TODO.md both quote, and quote correctly — while `WorkMetadata`'s own **type** doc read *"Work-level metadata, verified from the manifest embedded in the bundle (MVP-SPEC.md line 98)"* (`report.rs:157-158`), a second over-claim in different words that neither document had noticed. Only `claimed_time_informational_only` carried a caveat; `title` carried none at all, and `app_version`'s *"informational only"* note lived one layer down on the accessor at `manifest/body.rs:1184` and never reached the report. (3) `FileReveal::total_size` and `UnrevealedFilePlaceholder::size` are manifest-declared figures, and for a wholly unrevealed file nothing can contradict the latter, yet its doc calls it *"the only datum a placeholder exposes"* without saying whose datum it is.
- Do: give every sealer-recorded report field the same caveat in its own doc, and correct **both** `WorkMetadata` group docs so neither claims a check the pipeline does not perform. `AnchorVerdict`'s byte-exact `invalid`-slot pin used `"fetch_date":"2026-08-02"`, a shape the emitter can never produce (it renders decimal POSIX seconds) — **fixed at landing** to `"1785000100"`.
- Accept: no report field's doc claims verification the pipeline does not perform; the one committed byte-exact example of a rendered fetch date agrees with the one real producer.
- Notes: the two reveal sizes are **in scope** and were fixed here, because R73's own remit is every sealer-recorded report field and both are exactly that — `file.size()` from the manifest file table (`crates/antseal-core/src/verify/pipeline.rs:1201` and `:1164`). Measuring them sharpened the caveat rather than just adding one: they are not unconstrained. `check_tiling` (`crates/antseal-core/src/verify/structural.rs:307`) requires each file's non-mirror units to be sorted, non-overlapping and to exactly tile `[0, size)`, and it runs on every reveal shape including a reveal with no revealed units (`structural.rs:28-34`), so neither figure can disagree with the manifest's own unit table. What separates the two is what sits *under* the number: `FileReveal::total_size` has opened units beneath part of its range, while a wholly unrevealed placeholder has none, leaving the tiling check as its only constraint. Both now say declared-not-measured, and the placeholder says it is the weaker of the two. **Documentation-only, verified as such**: `git diff` over `report.rs` shows no changed line that is not a `///` line, the six field declarations are unchanged in name, type and order, and no rendered string moved — so `vector-freeze` (`testdata/vectors/v<n>/*.json`) and `format-freeze` (`docs/format/`, per `scripts/format-freeze.sh:52-68`) take no input from this file and neither manifest names a `.rs` path. R18's wording snapshots do not exist to re-bless: R18 is unstarted at M3 and `crates/antseal-core/src/verify/` contains no wording table.

### R74 — R18 gains the `fetch_date` label row, and the claimed/verified marker gets a home (D95 rider a)
- Milestone: M3
- Size: S
- Deps: R18; D95
- Discovered by: **the D95 planners** (2026-08-06).
- Problem: D95's ruling is *render + label*, and half the label is now paid, which narrows this task rather than closing it.
  - **The evidence this entry was built on is stale, and is recorded here as stale.** D95 §4 (`docs/decisions/D95-sealer-recorded-anchor-metadata-in-the-report.md:192-193`), D95's register row, D98 (`D98-status-anchor-state-vocabulary.md:240`) and TODO.md all state that `is_verified` has *"three repo-wide hits, all definition-or-test"*. Re-measured 2026-08-07: **eight** hits across `.rs` files outside `target/`, and **two of them are production**. `crates/antseal-cli/src/status.rs:594` (`fn source_label`) chooses between *"verified by this run"* and *"claimed by the artifact — this run did not verify it"*, and `status.rs:610` emits `"verified": source.is_verified()` in the `--json` envelope. The other six are the definition (`crates/antseal-core/src/anchor/model.rs:636`), two doc-comment references (`status.rs:43`, `status/tests.rs:354`) and three test assertions (`status/tests.rs:380`, `model.rs:1255`, `crates/antseal-core/src/anchor/verdicts/tests.rs:1235`). Both production callers landed in **wave 8 via U23**, under D98 rider 1c — so every document above was accurate when written and none was updated when `status` shipped.
  - **The consequence is a re-scope, not a correction.** The claimed/verified marker already has a home on the CLI status surface, and `status` is where D53 §4's *"MUST render as such"* is now genuinely mechanised: it holds `AnchorVerdict`s, so `AnchorVerdict::source()` hands it an `AnchorSource` with the discriminant intact, and the module never crosses the report boundary in either direction (`status.rs:40-46`). What still lacks the marker is the surface that cannot reach that datum: **the report/page boundary**. `AnchorVerdict::to_anchor_result` (`crates/antseal-core/src/anchor/model.rs:948`) collapses `AnchorSource::{Verified, Claimed}` to a bare `Option<String>` through `identity()` (`model.rs:628-632`) at `model.rs:962`, and R22 hands the verifier page *only* the report's serialized bytes — so the page is structurally incapable of honouring the obligation no matter how carefully it is written. The pin that looks like it covers this reads `"source":"claimed-tsa"`, where `claimed-tsa` is the fixture's own identity string and not a marker. (D95 §4 cites the collapse at `model.rs:963`; measured, it is `:962`.)
- Do: two halves, one already paid forward. (a) R18's `fetch_date` row — **written into R18's own entry above** rather than left here, so R18 cannot freeze its wording set without it; nothing further is owed by this task. (b) Give the report/page boundary a claimed/verified marker. A sixth `AnchorResult` field is a `REPORT_VERSION` event D53 §6 forbids casually; R18 embedding final display strings is what R22 already says the page receives, so the obligation is dischargeable at M3 **without** a version bump — and `source_label`'s two strings are the shape to carry over, since a second, differently-worded answer to the same question is exactly the divergence R18's one-authoritative-table rule exists to prevent.
- Accept: the wording set covers `fetch_date` (via R18's entry); a claimed source is distinguishable from a verified one **by something the page can act on**, given only report bytes; the CLI and page answers to "was this identity verified?" come from one table and are string-equal; no document still cites the three-hit figure or states the obligation without a mechanism.
- Notes: the stale figure survives in four places this task does not own — D95 §4 and its register row, D95's copy in `docs/decisions/README.md:81` and `TODO.md:704`, D98 `:240`, and `TODO.md:470`. Correcting a resolved decision's body is a decision-amendment question, not an implementation one; recorded here so the next reader of any of them has the measured number.

- Milestone: M2

- Deps: R12 (D95 §6)

### R76 — `AnchorResult::source`'s doc states an imperative its named consumer cannot honour
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: after R74
- Spec: D53 §4 — the *"MUST render as such"* rule the doc states.
- Discovered by: **the R73/R74 lane** (2026-08-07).
- Problem: `AnchorResult::source`'s doc states D53 §4's *"MUST render as such"* in the imperative, without noting that its **named consumer cannot honour it**. `to_anchor_result` (`crates/antseal-core/src/anchor/model.rs`) collapses `AnchorSource::{Verified, Claimed}` through `identity()` at the report boundary, so R22's page never receives the distinction. R74 now records the mechanism gap; the doc at the point of use does not point at R74, so a reader arriving at the imperative has no way to learn it is currently undischargeable.
- Do: add the one-line cross-reference — the doc at `AnchorResult::source` points at R74, where the mechanism gap is recorded.
- Accept: a reader at `AnchorResult::source` is told, in the doc itself, that the distinction does not survive `to_anchor_result` today and where that is recorded (R74); the imperative is not deleted or weakened, because D53 §4 still says it.
- Notes: XS and it should stay XS — this is a cross-reference, not the fix. Closing the gap itself is R74's, and this row exists so that the three days between reading the imperative and finding R74 stop happening.
- **Entry authored 2026-08-10 under Q134 from `TODO.md`'s R76 row alone — no other source existed.** Transcribed from the row: `Milestone` (its M2 section), `Size`, `Deps`, `Discovered by`, the `Spec`'s D53 §4 reference, the `Problem`, and the `Do` — the row supplies the imperative (*"One-line cross-reference"*) with both the document and the target named. **The `Accept` is authored** as the observable form of that `Do`; the row states no criterion. Its second clause — that the imperative must not be weakened — is this entry's addition and not the row's, and is there because the cheapest way to make a doc consistent with its consumer is to delete the rule, which would lose D53 §4. `to_anchor_result` was confirmed present in `anchor/model.rs` on 2026-08-10.

### R77 — `SignatureScheme::Other`'s doc promises an R18 rendering report v1 cannot carry
- Milestone: M3
- Size: XS
- Deps: R18 (the table that was supposed to discharge it); D105 (what a report field costs)
- Spec: Verifier web page — the signature label (MVP-SPEC.md line 132); Reveal bundle — report contents (line 114)
- Discovered by: **the R18 lane** (2026-08-11), while authoring the row the doc names. Not found by reading the doc — found by trying to satisfy it.
- Problem: `SignatureScheme::Other`'s field doc says *"R18 renders it as the policy's own algorithm list rather than a slogan."* **R18 cannot, and neither can anything else on the page side.** Report v1's `WorkMetadata` carries `signature_scheme` and **no algorithm list**, and R22 hands the page only the report's canonical bytes — so the renderer the sentence addresses has nothing to render from. R18 therefore froze the row at what the report supports (a description that names no algorithm) and asserted exactly that, rather than silently leaving the promise unsatisfied. This is the same class as **R76** — a doc stating an imperative its named consumer cannot honour — on a different field and by a different mechanism, and the recurrence is the reason this is a row rather than a ledger line: two instances make it a pattern in `report.rs`'s docs, not an accident.
- Do: pick one and record which, at the doc: **(a)** correct the sentence to say what report v1 supports and point at this row for why (cheapest, doc-only, zero format cost); or **(b)** declare it a CLI/page divergence in R18's declared-divergences header — the CLI holds the manifest and *could* name the policy's algorithms, the page provably cannot — which is R61's shape and R61's header slot; or **(c)** rule that the list belongs in the report, which is a **FORMAT EVENT** (a new field on `WorkMetadata` costs `REPORT_VERSION`, D105) and must be argued as one, not slipped in. Do not delete the sentence without replacing the information: a reader needs to know why `Other` is vague.
- Accept:
  - The doc at `SignatureScheme::Other` states only what a renderer can actually do with report v1, and names where the constraint is recorded.
  - If (b): the divergence is in R18's header **with a test**, not in prose alone — R61's unfulfilled obligation is not extended by a second undertested declaration.
  - Doc-only unless (c) is ruled: no field, no variant, no serialization change; `REPORT_VERSION` stays 1 and the frozen registry is untouched.
- Notes: R18's row is frozen and correct as it stands — the wording set asserts `Other`'s row names no algorithm, so whichever arm is taken, the **test** is already the pin and only the doc moves (unless (c), which moves the format). Precedent for the shape: **R76** is the identical repair on `AnchorResult::source` and is still open; whoever takes one should take both, since the second costs a line once the first is written.
- Notes: **[Registrar, 2026-08-16 — wave 21 closing. CLOSED on arm (a), and the `Do`'s arm (b) died on a premise measured false in BOTH halves.]** **Arm (a) taken**: the doc at `SignatureScheme::Other` now states only what report v1 supports and names where the constraint is recorded; doc-only, `REPORT_VERSION` stays 1, no field, no variant, no serialization change, and the frozen registry is untouched. **The correction this row's own `Do` needs.** Arm (b) reads *"the CLI holds the manifest and could name the policy's algorithms, the page provably cannot"* — **both halves are false**. `VerifyOutcome` (`crates/antseal-core/src/verify/orchestration.rs:912`) carries **no manifest**, so the CLI does not hold it at render time; and `verify_rendered` **already decodes the bundle a second time** for D132's `plan`, with `SealProof` and `sig_policy()` both public, so the page **can** reach it. There is no asymmetry, therefore no divergence to declare, therefore no R18 header slot to fill. **Arm (b) died on its premise, not on cost** — which matters, because a later lane rereading the arm list would otherwise price it and take it. **Machine: this local host** (`cargo test -p antseal-core`, in the wave-21 gate's `test` lane, 2 871 tests). **R76 stays open** (M2, `AnchorResult::source`, same file), and **R87** now sweeps the same class across three further sites; whoever takes one should take all four.

### R78 — The bundle builder discards a self-check finding it now computes for free
- Milestone: M3
- Size: XS
- Deps: after R20 (the layer, and D128's default that makes the finding exist), R13 (the self-check and `assert_beyond_self_check`, which is the named home); D70 §7.3 (what belongs in that function and why); ~~D84 rule F2 (why the layer can never fail a bundle on its own)~~ — **Corrected 2026-08-16 by the R78 lane**: F2 is the **anchor** blast-radius rule and governs the *verifier*, which may not fail a stranger's bundle. The linkage layer borrows its **shape** and says so in those words (`crates/antseal-core/src/verify/storage_linkage.rs:25-28`); its own non-gating rests on MVP-SPEC.md line 118, project rule 4 and the `Result`-free return type. The distinction is load-bearing for this row, because F2 governs the verifier while R78 governs whether the **builder** emits its own bad bundle — the opposite direction
- Spec: Reveal bundle — the two proof layers, rendered distinctly (MVP-SPEC.md lines 116, 119); Manifest storage record (line 98); M3 milestone — storage-linkage layer (line 156)
- Discovered by: **the D128 planning lane** (2026-08-12), while pricing what the ruling deliberately left on the table — D128 §10 (iv), flagged in §3 R7 as *"the one place this ruling leaves value on the table on purpose"*.
- Problem: after D128 the builder's mandatory self-check — `verify_bundle(&bytes, &VerifyOptions::new())` at `crates/antseal-core/src/builder/mod.rs:626` (line as measured 2026-08-12) — **evaluates the storage-linkage layer and then throws the whole report away**, keeping only the accept/reject bit. That bit is structurally silent about linkage: `check_storage_linkage` returns no `Result` (D84 rule F2), so a total mismatch is a *value in a report nobody reads*. And a mismatch is not a cosmetic disagreement: the recorded unit address lives in the **signed** manifest body (`work_id = SHA-256(manifest body)`), so a mismatch means the builder embedded a ciphertext that does not address to the address the signed manifest records — i.e. the builder is about to ship a bundle whose recipient's linkage check will fail, **for a reason internal to the builder**, with every other gate green. The cost of noticing is already paid: the layer has just run, on bytes the builder itself assembled.
- Do: consume the self-check's report inside the builder instead of discarding it, and refuse or warn on a storage-linkage mismatch. `assert_beyond_self_check` (~~`builder/mod.rs:631`~~ — **Corrected 2026-08-16: `:631` is inside the block comment above the CALL, the call is at `builder/mod.rs:642` and the function is DEFINED at `builder/mod.rs:718`; a lane sent to the call site edits the wrong end**, D70 §7.3) is the named home — the function whose whole subject is *"the guarantees the self-check structurally cannot make"*, re-derived from the emitted sections plus the signed manifest. **Answer, do not assume, whether the finding is a hard build error or a warning, and record the answer with its reason at the code.** The case for **hard**: the named home is an *assert* that refuses the build on any violation, and D70 §7.3 set that precedent deliberately for exactly this class — an internally-detectable defect in a bundle about to leave the builder. The case for **warning**: a hard arm makes the builder refuse bundles that verify (the linkage layer gates nothing on the verify side, by ruling), so it is a new refusal class that no `BuildError` variant covers and no caller expects. Whichever arm is taken, it must be reachable in a test that constructs the mismatch deliberately — `StorageAddresses::{RealExceptUnit, RealExceptManifest}` (added by R20 for exactly this shape) are the fixture handles, and `Placeholder` must keep whatever behaviour the arm assigns it, because every committed fixture is a total mismatch under it.
- Accept:
  - The builder's self-check no longer discards the storage-linkage finding: a bundle built with a bent **unit** address and one with a bent **manifest** address are each detected inside `build_bundle`, and the test names which arm (error or warning) fires and asserts the exact variant or the exact rendered text.
  - The chosen arm is recorded at the code with its reason, naming D70 §7.3 and D128 §3 R7 / §10 (iv) — a reader must not have to re-derive why an *assert* home was or was not used.
  - `Placeholder`-addressed fixtures are dispositioned explicitly (they are total mismatches by construction — `fixture_address` is a per-unit fill pattern and is not BLAKE3 of anything, so every unit mismatches and the manifest half cannot match either), so the whole committed catalogue does not become a builder refusal by accident. If the hard arm is taken, this is the load-bearing case, and R6/R13's fixture catalogue must still build.
  - Zero format cost: no report field, no variant, no wire key, no error code outside the builder's own (which is deliberately outside the Q52 frozen universe, per R13); `REPORT_VERSION` stays 1 and no frozen vector digest moves.
- Notes: This is a **row and not a ledger line** because it changes what a user's `reveal` produces — the Q112 class, per protocol rule 8. It is XS in code and not XS in judgement: the sizing covers the wiring, not the arm choice, and a lane that picks silently has not done the row. Ordering: it cannot land before R20 implements D128 (until then the self-check's options suppress nothing but the layer does not run by default either), and it is independent of R21/R22 — nothing on the verify side changes, because the layer already gates nothing there.
- Notes: **[Registrar, 2026-08-16 — wave 21 closing. CLOSED; the arm is recorded at the code and the row's own `Deps` and `Do` are corrected above rather than restated.]** The builder consumes the self-check's report instead of discarding it, inside `assert_beyond_self_check` (`crates/antseal-core/src/builder/mod.rs:718`), with the arm and its reason recorded at the function. **`BuildError`'s family-3 doc was falsified by this row's own change and was amended in the same edit** — it read *"The assertion variants are unreachable through the public API by construction"*, which stops being true the moment a caller-input assertion joins the function; amending it in the same act is the right disposition and is exactly the failure mode R76/R77/**R87** are about, caught before it shipped. **Machine: this local host** — `cargo test -p antseal-core` including the new `crates/antseal-core/tests/builder_storage_linkage.rs`, in the wave-21 gate's `test` lane (2 871 tests, PASS). `Placeholder`-addressed fixtures are dispositioned explicitly, so R6/R13's committed catalogue still builds. **Zero format cost held**: `REPORT_VERSION` stays 1, no frozen vector digest moved, and `./scripts/vector-freeze.sh` is green without `--update`.

### R79 — The online and live layers follow opposite rules for the same hazard, and one of them is wrong
- Milestone: M3
- Size: S
- Deps: after R21 (the composition that renders both layers into one verdict, which is what made the asymmetry visible), R11 (the taxonomy and the string's producer); D67 §3 R6 (the value-vs-rendering split R19 and R21 both applied); D69 §3 R6 (why neither layer can reach the exit code — which is what confines this to a display question and not a verdict one)
- Spec: Reveal bundle / verifier — the two proof layers, rendered distinctly (MVP-SPEC.md lines 116, 119); the sealer in the adversary position beside the display guardrail (line 121); core user flows — verify (line 38)
- Discovered by: **the R21 lane** (2026-08-12), while composing the online overlay and the live section into one rendered verdict. It could not have been seen earlier: the two layers had never rendered side by side.
- Problem: **the same hazard is governed by two opposite rules, one crate apart, and the project holds both.** The online layer's `ProbeFailureClass` is a **closed** class — three variants, no free-form detail — and states its reason in the strongest available form: *"an adversary-controlled endpoint must not get a byte channel into a display line (the same rule `agree.rs` applies to its `Payload.reason`), and the wording for each class is core's … never the host's"* (`crates/antseal-core/src/verify/overlay.rs`, `ProbeFailureClass`'s doc — `:83-87` **as measured 2026-08-12**, cited as a symbol because a line into `crates/` mid-wave is a coin flip). The **storage** boundary is the same shape by the same argument and does the opposite, in four hops with no taxonomy, no length cap and no classification key: `StorageError::Network { reason }` renders as `network error: {reason}` from whatever the backend adapter put in it → `live_check` maps a non-`NotFound` failure to S15's `PersistenceOutcome::FetchError { reason: error.to_string() }` (`crates/antseal-net/src/live.rs`, at the `get_data` match) → R21 carries it into `LiveBlobOutcome::FetchFailed { reason }` → `wording::live_blob_fetch_error_line(subject, reason)` interpolates it into a display line. **The R21 lane carried the string faithfully and said so** — documented under D67 §3 R6's escape-at-the-renderer split, exactly as R19 does for a sealer-authored `path` — rather than inventing a taxonomy that is not R11's; the asymmetry is therefore shipped **and recorded**, not hidden. Two measurements sharpen it. **(i) The two docs disagree about who authored the bytes**: core's `FetchFailed` calls the string *"host-supplied"* and *"host-authored text"*, while its actual producer is the `Display` of an error raised at the network boundary — the host is a courier, not the author, and the doc's word is what makes the channel look already-governed. **(ii)** Under `--live` the addresses probed are the ones the **bundle** names, and spec line 121 puts the sealer in the adversary position; escaping (D67's half, and a renderer's job) answers only *"can this forge a row"*, never *"should this channel exist"* — which is the question the online layer already answered **no** to, in the same crate, for the same reason.
- Do: **rule which of the two disciplines governs, and apply the ruling to both layers.** Patching one site is explicitly not the row: the defect is the disagreement, and a fix that leaves the other doc standing recreates it. The arms, one to be recorded with its reason at the code: **(a) the closed class wins** — mint a live failure class over the variants `StorageError` actually justifies, map it at the `antseal-net` boundary while the variant is still in hand (never by re-parsing the rendered string), drop the free-form `reason` from the rendered line, and amend `PersistenceOutcome::FetchError`'s doc so the taxonomy is the contract; **(b) the free-form channel wins** — then `ProbeFailureClass`'s doc asserts a project-wide rule the project does not hold, and the honest repair is to state what the closed class actually buys (WASM-safety and one wording home, both real) and to give the live `reason` a **stated bound** — cap, escape, hygiene test — so it is a governed channel rather than an ungoverned one; **(c) the split is deliberate** — the online endpoints are third parties the verifier chose from its own config while the storage boundary is the verifier's own client — in which case both docs must say so, and the difference stops being an accident. Whichever arm, the ruling must **name the other layer from both sites**, so no future reader can find one discipline without finding the other.
- Accept:
  - One recorded ruling, cited from **both** sites — `ProbeFailureClass`'s doc and `PersistenceOutcome::FetchError`'s / `LiveBlobOutcome::FetchFailed`'s — so the two can never again be read independently.
  - A hostile-string test exists either way: a backend whose error `Display` carries an LF, a frozen verdict string and a long run of bytes is driven through `live_check` to the rendered line, and the test asserts the ruled outcome — under (a) that none of it reaches the line, under (b)/(c) that what reaches it is escaped, capped and still exactly one line.
  - If (a): the mapping is **wildcard-free** over `StorageError`, so a new variant fails to compile rather than falling into a catch-all — the discipline R11 already applied to `UnitKindTag`.
  - Zero format cost either way: no report field, no wire key, `REPORT_VERSION` stays 1 — `PersistenceOutcome` is `antseal-net`'s own serialized type and never enters report v1 (R11 execution note 2).
  - Exit codes untouched: D69 §3 R6 makes the live layer exit-code-free **at the type level** (R21 landed it as an absence — the live and linkage values are never parameters of `verdict_exit_rung`), and this row must not be the thing that reintroduces them.
- Notes: **the Q112 class** — shipped code contradicting a policy stated in the tree — so it mints a row immediately under protocol rule 8 rather than going to the ledger. S covers the mapping and the tests; the **ruling** is the expensive half, exactly as R78's arm choice is, and a lane that picks silently has not done the row. Ordering: after R21 (it needs the composition to exist) and after R11 (which owns the taxonomy an (a)-shaped fix extends). Independent of U30 — but U30 is the first surface on which an ungoverned `reason` is actually **printed to a user**, so landing this before U30 is worth more than landing it after.
- Notes: **[Registrar, 2026-08-12 — wave 19, appended here rather than minted as a new id, because this row is already the *"two layers, opposite rules, same hazard"* row.]** After D130 the escaping question was re-opened deliberately, and `LiveSection`'s `FetchFailed { reason }` came out of it **still unescaped on the CLI path**, though its own doc states the rule in the strongest form — *"It is host-authored text that reaches a display line, so a renderer escapes it for its own surface exactly as it escapes `FileRedaction::path` (D67 §3 R6's value-vs-rendering split)."* Four sites carry it verbatim: the CLI clones `PersistenceOutcome::FetchError`'s reason into `LiveBlobOutcome::FetchFailed`; `LiveSection::new` takes **no escape closure** at all, unlike `RenderedVerdict::new(&report, &offline_verdict, escape)` beside it, and its construction site has `escape` in scope and does not pass it; `wording::live_blob_fetch_error_line` interpolates the reason raw; and `verify_out.rs` pushes the finished line straight into the output, with `escape_for_terminal` present in that file but never on the live path. **`row.subject` is host-authored too, and is equally unescaped** — the same four hops carry both. **This was named by the D130 lane rather than acted on, and correctly**: `LiveSection` is not a member of D130's rendered document, so it fell outside D130 §3 R9's *"every sealer- or artifact-authored value the document embeds"* — the rule that produced the four escapes on `RenderedVerdict`. The gap is therefore not an oversight in D130; it is this row's own subject arriving at a fourth site, and it strengthens rather than changes the row: the online layer's closed `ProbeFailureClass` and the live layer's free-form `reason` now differ **inside one rendered surface**, printed to a user by U30 today.
- Notes: **[Registrar, 2026-08-16 — wave 21 closing. CLOSED on arm (a), and the decisive measurement INVERTS this row's own arm (c).]** **Arm (c) reasons that the online endpoints are third parties while the storage boundary is the verifier's own client. Measured, it is the other way round.** `agree.rs` makes `EndpointFailure::Payload.reason` a **`&'static str` on purpose** — *"no adversary-controlled bytes can reach a log line through it"* — and `WrongChain` carries two `u64`s, so the only free-form string on an online display line is the verifier's **own configured URL**. Meanwhile `crates/antseal-cli/src/backend.rs`'s `map_ant_error` ended in a **wildcard** `other => StorageError::Network { reason: format!("{other}") }` over all of `ant_core::data::Error`, whose `Protocol(String)`, `InvalidData(String)`, `Network(String)` and `RemotePut` variants can carry remote-supplied text. **The remote-byte channel existed only on the storage side**, which is the opposite of what arm (c) supposes and the reason (a) is the ruling. **What landed**: new closed class `antseal_net::FetchFailureClass` (`crates/antseal-net/src/live.rs:129`, `ALL: [Self; 2]` at `:147`), **wildcard-free over all nine `StorageError` variants in one `of()`**, with `NotFound` returning `None` because it is an *answer* and not a failure to get one; cited from `live.rs`, `live/manifest.rs` and `verify/overlay.rs`, each naming the other, so the two docs can never again be read alone. The lane also closed the **second half of this row's own `Notes`** — `row.subject`: unescaped is true of the *type* and false of the *values*, whose grammar is `unit <digits>` | `unit <digits> (raw mirror)` | `encrypted manifest` — and the **fourth hop** at `crates/antseal-cli/src/verify_out.rs:334`. **The cost is stated at the code rather than hidden**: `AntCoreBackend::get_data` reports its own BLAKE3 re-check failure through the same `StorageError::Network` as a timeout, so the class cannot separate them; that distinction lived only in the free-form string and recovering it is an **S2-taxonomy event**, not this row's. **Residue registered as R89**, not left in a report: `LiveBlobOutcome::FetchFailed` still carries a `String`, which is type-precision debt rather than an open channel. **Machine: this local host** — `crates/antseal-net/tests/live_display_channel.rs` (hostile-string drive through `live_check` to the rendered line, plus the wildcard-free compile-fail assertion), in the wave-21 gate's `test` lane.
- Notes: **[Registrar, 2026-08-17 — locator currency, not a re-opening.]** Every `antseal-net` locator in this entry is **pre-R89** and will mislead anyone who follows it: R89 moved `FetchFailureClass` out of `crates/antseal-net/src/live.rs` (where R79 put it at `:129`, `ALL` at `:147`, with `of`/`label`/`token`) into `crates/antseal-core/src/verify/orchestration.rs:263`, deleting `ALL`/`of`/`label`/`token` from `antseal-net` and leaving the boundary classifier `fetch_failure_class` (`live.rs:164`) plus a re-export (`:74`). The test this row's evidence cites, `live_display_channel.rs`, still exists and still passes — but its `the_fetch_failure_vocabulary_is_finite_and_static` case (`:296`) now sweeps a **core-owned** type from `antseal-net`'s suite and its own doc comment (*"the value `antseal-net` produces has no free-form slot at all"*) is false as written; that is instrument-class and is in `docs/instrument-ledger.md`, not a row.

### R80 — R21's live-mode Accept has no bridge from a real backend
- Milestone: M3
- Size: XS
- Deps: after R21 (the `LiveInputs` shape and the rendering it feeds), R11 (`live_check`, `records_from_manifest`, the public `StorageRecord::new` extension point); D34 (whose placement rule is what the test's home turns on). R59 is the sibling question, not an ordering dep.
- Spec: Verifier — the storage layer and `--live` (MVP-SPEC.md line 119); core user flows — verify (line 38); M3 milestone (line 156)
- Discovered by: **the R21 lane** (2026-08-12), by measuring its own Accept row 3 instead of declaring it satisfied.
- Problem: R21's Accept row 3 reads *"Live mode renders match/mismatch/fetch-failed per blob against `MockBackend`"*, and its two halves landed in two crates that cannot meet. R21 proved the **rendering** in `antseal-core` — four outcomes against their frozen R18 wording rows, five precedence cases, and counts that partition the rows — and R11 proved the **fetching** against `MockBackend` in `antseal-net`. **Nothing builds R21's `LiveInputs` from an actual `live_check(&MockBackend, …)`**, and it is not an oversight: `antseal-net` depends on `antseal-core`, so core cannot dev-depend on net without a dependency cycle. The whole claim *"the live layer works end to end"* therefore rests on two proofs that have never been composed, and the composition is one test's worth of code sitting on the one seam no crate owns.
- Do: land one test **outside `antseal-core`** that runs `live_check` over a `MockBackend` seeded so that one blob is identical, one differs, one is absent and one fails to fetch; convert the resulting `PersistenceReport` into R21's `LiveInputs`; and assert the rendered `LiveSection` — the same rows R21 pins in-crate, reached through the real primitive rather than through hand-built inputs. Home is `crates/antseal-net/tests/` (net can see both crates today) or `crates/antseal-cli/` (where D34 sends the driver, and where U30 needs the identical conversion). **Choose deliberately and record why**: whichever crate hosts the test is where the `PersistenceReport → LiveInputs` conversion lives, and that conversion is U30's wiring, not the test's.
- Accept:
  - One test, outside `antseal-core`, driving `live_check` against `MockBackend` and asserting R21's rendered live rows — all four outcomes plus the section's aggregate precedence (R11 execution note 3's ladder, seen through R21's renderer).
  - R21's Accept row 3 is discharged **by citation to this test**: the row names `MockBackend`. ~~and after this row that name appears in exactly one place on the live path.~~ — **Struck 2026-08-16: unachievable, and its premise was already stale when the row was written.** This row's `Problem` says *"nothing builds R21's `LiveInputs` from an actual `live_check(&MockBackend, …)`"*, true on 2026-08-12 and false since **U30** landed `collect_live`: `crates/antseal-cli/tests/verify_command.rs:459` already drives it over a `MockBackend`. So `MockBackend` is in **two** places on the live path before this row starts, and deleting the CLI's would remove a passing test. The clause is struck rather than ticked around — the first half stands and is met.
  - The conversion is a named function with a **production** home, not a helper inside a `#[cfg(test)]` module — otherwise U30 writes it a second time and the two can disagree.
  - No new dependency edge: `antseal-core` gains no dev-dependency on `antseal-net`, and `core-dep-graph`'s reviewed set is unchanged.
- Notes: **R59 is the same question one layer down, and the two should be taken together** — R59 owes R11's bundle-shaped adapter (a bundle's embedded ciphertexts + its encrypted-manifest record → `Vec<StorageRecord>`) through the public `StorageRecord::new` extension point, unbuilt for the identical cycle reason. A lane that builds R59's adapter gets R80's fixture for free, and a lane that builds R80's conversion has decided R59's placement whether it says so or not. **R59's arm (b) — *"relocate the module into the cli lib beside R21"* — no longer names a reachable location**: R21 landed in `antseal-core` (see the D34 reconciliation on R21's row and Notes), and core cannot see `StorageBackend`. The cli lib survives as a home on its own merits; the *"beside R21"* argument for it does not.
- Notes: **[Registrar, 2026-08-16 — wave 21 closing. CLOSED, with one Accept clause STRUCK above rather than ticked around.]** **Home ruled `antseal-net`**: `antseal_net::live_inputs` (`crates/antseal-net/src/live/manifest.rs:453`) is the one production projection, and `collect_live` (`crates/antseal-cli/src/verify_host.rs:412`) now **delegates** to it — so U30's conversion and this one cannot diverge, which was the row's actual worry. `collect_live` also lost a parallel `subjects: Vec<String>` that could fall out of step with its rows. The bridge test is `crates/antseal-net/tests/live_section_bridge.rs`, outside `antseal-core`, driving `live_check` over a `MockBackend` seeded for all four outcomes. **Machine: this local host**, in the wave-21 gate's `test` lane.
  **A correction to the wave's own brief, recorded because the opposite correction would have introduced a defect.** The brief said this row's Accept row 4 names the dep-graph lane *"wrongly"* and should read `dep-graph`. **It does not and it should not.** Measured 2026-08-16: `bash scripts/ci-lanes.sh --list` names the **script lane** `dep-graph` (`scripts/ci-lanes.sh:69`, dispatched at `:1443`), while **`core-dep-graph` is the GitHub Actions job id and required-status context** (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:222`, `name: core-dep-graph`). Accept row 4 is about the **reviewed set a required context guards**, so `core-dep-graph` is the correct name there; what the lane *ran* was `bash scripts/ci-lanes.sh dep-graph` (green, no edge added). Both names are right, for different things, and collapsing them would have made a correct Accept clause wrong. **The general rule this leaves**: cite the script lane as ``scripts/ci-lanes.sh `dep-graph` `` and the CI job as `core-dep-graph`, which is the form `docs/instrument-ledger.md` already uses.

### R81 — `verify --live` cannot check the encrypted-manifest blob, so it is silent about the one blob the offline layer does check
- Milestone: M3
- Size: XS
- Deps: after U30 (the `--live` driver, and `collect_live`, which is where the absence is documented), R11 (`LiveSubject::EncryptedManifest`, `records_from_manifest`'s `encrypted_manifest` parameter, and the public `StorageRecord::new` extension point), R20 (the offline linkage layer — the only code in the tree that rebuilds the blob); D128 §3 R1 is the reason the two halves always render together (the offline half runs on **every** verify, so there is no configuration in which the asymmetry is hidden)
- Spec: Verifier — the storage layer and `--live` (MVP-SPEC.md line 119); the two proof layers rendered distinctly (line 116); Manifest storage record — anchored bytes ≠ stored bytes (line 98); core user flows — verify (line 38); M3 milestone (line 156)
- Discovered by: **the U30 lane** (2026-08-12), while building the bundle-shaped adapter that `StorageRecord::new`'s own docs name as M3's — by trying to add the manifest row and finding no public route to the bytes it would have to compare against.
- Problem: **one storage section, two halves, and they disagree about their own subject.** R11 defines `LiveSubject::EncryptedManifest` and `records_from_manifest` already takes an `encrypted_manifest: Option<(Address, &[u8])>` to build that row, so the live side's *shape* has always admitted it. What it cannot obtain is the **bytes**. The blob the sealer uploaded is `XChaCha20-Poly1305(k_m, nonce, MANIFEST_AAD, manifest_bytes)`, and reproducing it is exactly what R20's offline layer does — `check_storage_linkage`'s manifest arm calls `antseal_core::crypto::manifest_aead::recompute_manifest_blob`, which is **`pub(crate)`**. `encrypt_manifest` is public but draws a **fresh random nonce**, so it cannot reproduce the uploaded blob; `decrypt_manifest` runs the wrong direction. So the offline half checks the manifest and every unit, while `--live` checks every embedded **unit** ciphertext and nothing else — and the rendered section says which subjects it *found*, never which it could not *ask about*. A user reading a clean live section is being told less than the sentence sounds like. **The brief's framing — "the fix is a one-word visibility change" — is refused by the function's own rustdoc**, which argues the `pub(crate)` deliberately: *"The module docs' structural claim — no public API accepts a caller-supplied encryption nonce — is what keeps `(k_m, nonce)` single-use … A `pub` version of this function would be a general encrypt-under-a-chosen-nonce API wearing a verification name."* That is a nonce-reuse guardrail, not an oversight, and this row must not spend it by default. The countervailing measurement, which is why the arm is arguable rather than closed: `ManifestLinkageSubject`'s fields — `nonce`, `k_m`, `manifest_bytes`, `recorded_address` — are **already public**, because `check_storage_linkage` is a public stage, so the claim as written governs *functions that encrypt*, not the material itself.
- Do: **rule the route, then wire it — and the ruling is the expensive half.** The arms, one to be recorded with its reason at the code: **(a) widen `recompute_manifest_blob` to `pub`** — the cheapest edit and the one the discovery named, refused unless its own rustdoc's structural claim is rewritten in the same change, because leaving both standing ships a doc that its neighbour falsifies (the R79 shape, one crate over); **(b) mint a verification-shaped `pub fn` in `verify::storage_linkage`** taking the `ManifestLinkageSubject` the public stage already takes and returning the recomputed blob (or its `ContentAddress`), so the nonce arrives inside a named linkage subject rather than as a free parameter and the encrypt-door stays shut — still a widening, and still to be argued rather than assumed; **(c) compare addresses instead of bytes on the live path** — S15 fetches the blob and the recorded address is in hand, so an address comparison needs no plaintext-side recomputation at all, at the cost of changing `StorageRecord`'s shape, which currently requires a ciphertext copy to compare against (R11's, not this row's, so taking (c) makes this row R11-owning rather than XS); **(d) rule the absence permanent and render it** — cheapest of all and the only arm that is honest without any new API, but it must then *appear*: the live section states the subjects it could not reach, so the gap is a sentence a user reads rather than a comment in `collect_live`. **(d) is not exclusive with the others** and should ride whichever is taken, because a section that reports three of four subjects and says so is strictly better than one that reports three and stops. Whichever arm: the manifest row must be **reachable in a test that bends the manifest blob deliberately** and asserts the live section sees it — the offline layer's `StorageAddresses::RealExceptManifest` (R20's fixture handle) is the shape, driven through `MockBackend` rather than through hand-built inputs, which is R80's bridge and the reason these two are cheaper taken together.
- Accept:
  - `verify --live` over a work whose manifest blob was replaced on the network renders a **manifest** row in the live section, distinct from the unit rows, and the divergence is visible in the rendered output and in `--json` — or, under arm (d), the section states in R18-frozen wording that the encrypted manifest was not checked, and a test asserts that sentence is present on every `--live` run.
  - The chosen arm is recorded at the code with its reason, naming `recompute_manifest_blob`'s `pub(crate)` rustdoc and this row — a reader must not have to re-derive why the one-word change was or was not taken, and if it *was* taken, the rustdoc's structural claim is rewritten in the same change rather than left to contradict it.
  - The `collect_live` comment that records the absence is **deleted or rewritten in the same change**; a documented gap outliving its fix is the defect this row is made of, one layer up.
  - Zero format cost: no report field, no wire key, no new error code, `REPORT_VERSION` stays 1 and `ENVELOPE_VERSION` stays 1. `LiveSubject::EncryptedManifest` already exists and already serializes, so the live document gains a row it was always shaped for, not a key.
  - Exit codes untouched — D69 §3 R6 makes the live layer exit-code-free at the type level (R21 landed it as an absence; the live and linkage values are never parameters of `verdict_exit_rung`), and this row must not be the thing that reintroduces them.
- Notes: **the Q112 class** — shipped code that says less than its rendering implies — so it mints a row under protocol rule 8 rather than going to the ledger, and it changes what a user's `verify --live` reports. **XS in code, not XS in judgement**, exactly as R78 is: the sizing covers the wiring, and a lane that widens the visibility without answering the encrypt-door argument has not done the row. **Take it with R80.** R80 owes the `PersistenceReport → LiveInputs` bridge from a real `MockBackend`, which is the only place a bent manifest blob can be presented to the live path at all; a lane holding that fixture gets this row's test for free, and a lane doing this row without it is hand-building the inputs it was supposed to drive. The asymmetry is **recorded, not hidden**: `collect_live`'s comment states the bound in the lane's own words (*"`--live` proves persistence for every unit ciphertext the bundle embeds and says nothing about the encrypted manifest … the fix is one visibility change in a crate this lane may not write"*) — this row keeps that history and refuses only its last clause, on the rustdoc the lane could read but did not quote.
- Notes: **[Registrar, 2026-08-16 — wave 21 closing. CLOSED on arm (b), and the venue its last clause reached is itself the finding.]** **Arm (b) taken**: `pub fn recompute_manifest_storage_blob(subject: &ManifestLinkageSubject<'_>) -> Option<Vec<u8>>` at `crates/antseal-core/src/verify/storage_linkage.rs:166`, taking the linkage subject the public stage already takes, so the nonce arrives inside a named verification type rather than as a free parameter. **`crypto::manifest_aead::recompute_manifest_blob` stays `pub(crate)`** (`crates/antseal-core/src/crypto/manifest_aead.rs:349`) — the encrypt-door is not spent — and **both neighbouring docs were rewritten in the same change** so neither falsifies the other, which is this row's own R79-shaped requirement. The `collect_live` absence comment is **deleted**, replaced by the ruling at the site (`verify_host.rs`), per the Accept clause that a documented gap must not outlive its fix. **The delegation is proven rather than asserted**: planting a fault in the new public function reddens **four of R20's own tests**.
  **The last Accept clause closed at the deepest venue that exists, and the venue is the finding.** `crates/antseal-cli/tests/verify_live_manifest.rs` (4 tests) builds `shapes::multi_file()` with `StorageAddresses::Real`, preloads all **6** embedded unit ciphertexts honestly, and places an impostor at the address the **signed storage record** claims. Pinned exactly — rendered `      encrypted manifest: the network served different bytes`; `--json` `{"subject":"encrypted manifest","outcome":"different","line":"encrypted manifest: the network served different bytes"}` — with `counts.checked == 7`, `identical == 6`, `different == 1`, `verdict == Divergent`, the manifest row **last**, and all six others `unit `-prefixed and `Identical`. Three companions keep it honest: an honest-network control so `Different` cannot be a constant of the row, R20's `RealExceptManifest` at the CLI route, and a grammar pin on `MANIFEST_SUBJECT` so the *"exactly one manifest row"* filters cannot go vacuous. Two faults planted in `collect_live` (mislabel, suppression), each **RED by message**; `verify_host.rs` restored and **sha256-verified byte-identical** after each. **Machine: this local host**, `cargo test -p antseal-cli --test verify_live_manifest`.
  **The bound on that evidence, registered as U75 rather than hidden**: `crates/antseal-cli/src/commands.rs:740-745` returns `backend::unavailable("verify")` for `live` **before any verification**, un-`cfg`-gated, so `collect_live` has **no caller in `commands.rs` at all** — measured by whole-tree grep, its only call sites are `verify_command.rs:459` and this new file. **This row's Accept row 1 read literally is therefore not satisfiable by any process invocation** until U36 is wired into the `verify` handler. **The row still ticks**: it owed a manifest row in the live section and the manifest row is there at every hop that exists — production collector, production projection, production renderer, production machine document — and the missing hop is a property of `verify --live`'s wiring, not of this row's work. **R90** records a second observation the lane made and deliberately did not act on: `collect_live` emits unit rows in bundle-reveal order while `records_from_manifest`'s rustdoc promises manifest order, and nothing asserts either.

### R82 — The machine-interface snapshot cannot represent both feature builds, so the `ant-backend` machine surface is witnessed by nothing
- Milestone: M3
- Size: S
- Deps: after U30 (the third `Outcome` arm and the envelope's current shape — the last row to move `json-envelopes.txt`), U36 (the backend seam whose two `#[cfg]` arms are the divergence); U3 (the fixture registry and the rule that fixtures render through the **real** producer); U13 (the row the feature-ON sentence names as pending, and which has shipped); D65 (the tier partition that turns the envelope into a documented contract); Q153 (the ruling that keeps `heavy-features` local-only, which is why nothing remote can see this). Not an ordering dep: U69, which corrects a different fixture in the same file for a different reason — but the two touch the same committed snapshot and a lane taking one should read the other.
