- Spec: CLI surface — `--json` machine-readable output (MVP-SPEC.md lines 147–149); core user flows — seal / restore / reveal (lines 34, 36, 40); M3 milestone (line 156)
- Discovered by: **the R22 lane** (2026-08-12), on a `--features ant-backend` run of the CLI suite; **attributed to HEAD by the wave owner** in the same act, by checking out HEAD's `backend.rs` and HEAD's snapshot and reproducing the failure there. This is the **Q112 class** — a product defect found *by* an instrument, minted immediately under rule 8: it governs what a user's `--json` run returns in the build that can actually reach the network.
- Problem: **one committed file, two machine surfaces.** `crates/antseal-cli/tests/snapshots/json-envelopes.txt` is a single snapshot, rendered through the real producers per U3's rule and compared byte-for-byte by `envelope_fixtures_match_the_committed_snapshot`. But `backend::unavailable` has **two `#[cfg]` arms with different text** — the feature-OFF arm says the build has no storage backend compiled in and names `--features ant-backend`; the feature-ON arm says the command is not yet wired to the backend it has. Three registered documents route through that function (`seal`, `restore`, and — since U28/U29 — `reveal`), so under `--features ant-backend` three lines of the snapshot differ from the committed bytes and the test fails. **A single file cannot document both builds**, so whichever one it documents, the other build's machine surface is pinned by nothing at all.
  **The red pre-dates wave 18, and that was measured rather than assumed**: HEAD's `backend.rs` already carries both arms and HEAD's `json-envelopes.txt` already holds the feature-OFF text, so the test was already failing under that feature before this wave began. Wave 18 made it **one line worse** — `reveal` became the third command through the seam — and did not create it.
  **It is invisible to CI by design, not by accident.** The only lane that builds this feature is `heavy-features`, which **Q153** ruled **local-only** (475 packages resolved against the default build's 120) and which is additionally diff-triggered, so a green 19-of-19 remote run is silent on it by construction. The defect can therefore sit through any number of green waves.
  Two things lift this above a ledger line. **(a) The feature-ON text is false.** It reads *"connecting it to this command's argument handling lands with **U13**"* — and U13 has shipped. `seal` is wired; `restore` and `reveal` are not, but they are not waiting on U13, so both messages send a user to an already-closed row. Three independent lanes have now reported that same sentence (U28, U29, R22), which is what makes it a standing defect rather than a stale comment. **(b) D65 has just made the envelope a documented contract with tiers** — the `command`, `ok`, `v`, `network` and `error.class`/`error.exit_code` keys are tier A and B, and the whole point of the registry is that a reviewed snapshot moves when they do. A second build whose machine surface drifts unwitnessed is a contract with no instrument.
- Do: **rule how a feature-varying machine interface is pinned, then fix the U13 sentence in the same act.** The arms, one to be recorded with its reason at the fixture registry: **(a) a second committed snapshot** (`json-envelopes.ant-backend.txt` or equivalent) selected by `#[cfg(feature = "ant-backend")]`, which keeps both texts and doubles the blessing surface — and must then answer who runs the second one, given Q153; **(b) a feature-parameterised fixture** — one file whose feature-varying documents are rendered per-arm and compared per-arm, so the committed bytes carry both texts and neither build is unwitnessed; **(c) make the message feature-invariant** — one sentence true of both builds, which deletes the divergence at its source, costs the feature-ON arm the specific thing it says, and is the only arm that leaves one snapshot correct everywhere. Whichever is taken, the **U13 sentence goes in the same change**: it must name what is actually pending for the command it refuses (`restore`'s and `reveal`'s own wiring), not a shipped row.
- Accept:
  - `cargo test -p antseal-cli --features ant-backend` runs `envelope_fixtures_match_the_committed_snapshot` **green**, and so does the default build — both asserted, because the defect is precisely that one of the two was never checked.
  - The chosen arm is recorded at `machine_mode.rs`'s fixture registry with its reason and a pointer to this row, so the next lane does not re-derive why one file is or is not enough.
  - **No `--json` envelope key moves**: `ENVELOPE_VERSION` stays 1, no key added, removed or renamed, and D65's tier A/B members are byte-unchanged in the default build's document set. Only the `error.message` string of the feature-ON arm is in scope, and it is tier C. — **Amended 2026-08-16 by U73**: this clause is **exactly right about the envelope** and **incomplete as a description of the two builds**. Measured: both arms of `backend::unavailable` produce `"class":"network-failure","exit_code":23` and differ only in `message` (`crates/antseal-cli/src/backend.rs:225-229`; both `seal` documents in the committed snapshot carry 23), so this row's scope note holds and nothing here widens it. But for a **vault-less `seal` with a valid plan** the feature build never reaches that envelope — `seal_over_backend`'s feature-ON arm opens the vault first and the command exits **2**, not 23. So the two builds' observable `seal` **exit codes** do diverge; the divergence is a *sequential gate ahead of the seam*, not a property of the seam's envelope. U73 measured it and ruled it correct.
  - **The feature-ON refusal names no shipped row.** A test — not a review — pins that the message does not name `U13`, or names only rows that are open; a sentence pointing at closed work is the defect this row is half made of, and it has already recurred three times.
  - The fixtures are still rendered through the **real producer** (U3's rule, and the reason `unavailable` is `pub` at all) — no arm may be satisfied by hand-copying the second build's text into a file, which is the failure mode U19 and U3 both already paid for.
- Notes: **the divergence is deliberate and must not be deleted carelessly** — `unavailable`'s own rustdoc argues both arms in the strongest form (*"The two arms say different true things, which is the point: a message blaming the missing feature would be a lie in a build that has it"*), so arm (c) is a real option but is a **retraction of a recorded design choice** and has to be argued as one, not slipped in as a snapshot repair. **Take it with U69**: that row corrects a different `restore` entry in the same committed file for an unrelated reason (an envelope no build can emit), and two lanes blessing `json-envelopes.txt` from different premises will collide. **The row is S rather than XS because the ruling is the expensive half**, exactly as R79 and R81 are: the wiring is a file and a `#[cfg]`, and the question of who runs the second arm — given Q153's local-only ruling — is the part that outlives it.
- Notes: **[Registrar, 2026-08-16 — wave 21 closing. CLOSED on arm (b), and it closes `Q113` in the same change — one defect, two prefixes.]** **Arm (b) taken — the fixture is feature-parameterised.** `render_fixture()` (`crates/antseal-cli/tests/machine_mode.rs:372`, ruling in its rustdoc at `:334-371`) now renders **both** arms' refusals for every command still routing through the seam, **in the default build as much as the feature one**, so the `ant-backend` machine surface is documented by the file every CI job compares instead of by a file only a local `--features ant-backend` run would read. `seal` and `restore` are the two; `reveal` left the seam at **U72** and `verify --live` refuses before it has a registered command envelope of its own. `json-envelopes.txt` is **48 lines** today. The divergence is asserted rather than assumed (`machine_mode.rs:1431-1435`, `assert_ne!` over the two arms' messages with *"the divergence is deliberate and load-bearing"* as its message), and the fixtures are still rendered through the **real producer** per U3's rule. **The U13 sentence went in the same act**, as the row requires. **Machine: this local host, both builds**, measured by the registrar in the tick: `cargo test -p antseal-cli --test seal_command` → **23 passed, 0 failed**; `cargo test -p antseal-cli --features ant-backend --test seal_command` → **23 passed, 0 failed (47.73 s)**. That second figure is the whole point of the row: it is the first time the `ant-backend` surface has been green rather than unwitnessed.
  **`Q113` is the same defect under an M2 prefix and is ticked by this one change** (`TODO.md`, entry `tasks/Q.md`, *"The `json-envelopes.txt` machine-interface snapshot is feature-dependent and only one variant is committed"*). Neither census saw the other because Q113 is M2-tagged and this row is M3-tagged. **Both tick, neither is struck**: rule 1 forbids deletion, ids are permanent, and a strike would move the counts for a reason that is not a withdrawal. The precedent is in `TODO.md`'s own change log at M2 wave 8 — *"two found one defect under two different prefixes — reconciled at bookkeeping, in the register rather than in the code"*. Q113 closing after M2's gate is ordinary post-gate accretion; M1 had 11 rows open at its gate.
  **One correction owed to this row's own Accept**, applied above rather than restated: for `seal` the two builds' observable **exit codes** diverge too (23 against 2), which is **U73**'s measurement and not this row's change. The envelope scope note is untouched and remains correct.

### R83 — A green page-build `--check` is not evidence the packaged bytes came from the tree
- Milestone: M3
- Size: S
- Deps: after R25 (the row whose Accept row 1 this defeats, and whose build script carries the defect), R22 (the module build the page packages, and the `--self-test`/`--check` script shape R25 copied); D63 §5 R5 (the closed operation list and the no-network build fence), D129 §5 R9 (the eight built-artifact assertions, seven of which run here), D132 §1 (f) (the unexplained size gap this row is the mechanism for). Not an ordering dep: D63 §7 rule 1's optimizer pin, which is a separate open precondition on the same Accept row.
- Spec: Page provenance — reproducible build and published hash (MVP-SPEC.md line 139); Risks — malicious verifier host (line 186); M3 milestone (line 156)
- Discovered by: **the D132 planning lane** (2026-08-12), while pricing a sixth export and finding its A/B measurements contaminated; **re-measured and re-aimed by the registrar** in the same wave, because the lane's report named the wrong script.
- Problem: **the freshness of the artifact is asserted by nobody.** `scripts/verifier-page-build.sh` builds the wasm module **only when `target/wasm-pack/`'s two files are absent**; when they are present it proceeds straight to packaging. So `--check` — the mode `scripts/local-gate.sh` runs, and the default mode — packages, hashes, asserts and would hand onward **whatever artifact is already sitting in `target/`**. Every one of D129 §5 R9's assertions still passes, and that is not a bug in them: each compares the built page against **the module it was built from**, which is exactly the property a stale module preserves. Nothing anywhere compares the artifact against the sources.
  **Measured, not reasoned.** A `--check` run reported `Finished in 1.40s` with no `Compiling` line, and the artifact it passed over was **14 066 B larger** than a genuine rebuild of the equivalent export surface — the same magnitude and direction as the **14 677 B** D132 §1 (f) recorded and could not explain. That record's own §7 and §8 leave it open in the sharpest available form: *"Either the committed artifact is not from HEAD, or the build is not reproducible from source alone. R25's whole premise is that it is."* This row is the mechanism that makes the first disjunct survivable, and it is why the second cannot be ruled out by a green gate.
  **Consequence on the ship path**: **R25's Accept row 1** — *"two clean CI builds (different runners) produce byte-identical artifacts; CI job enforces this"* — is **not satisfied**, and cannot be satisfied by a script that will re-use an artifact it did not produce. Two runners each re-using their own stale `target/` would agree with themselves and prove nothing.
- Do: **make the build script prove the artifact is the tree's, and say which mode does it.** The arms, one to be recorded with its reason at the script's own header: **(a) always rebuild in `--check`**, matching `scripts/wasm-pack-build.sh`'s shape, which `rm -rf`s its out-dir and rebuilds unconditionally in every mode — the cost is a cold build in the gate (measured cold at 5.11 s for the page lane today, plus the module's own build); **(b) rebuild and require byte-equality with what was there**, which is the reproducibility assertion R25 actually owes and turns the staleness check into the Accept row rather than into a precondition for it; **(c) a freshness predicate** — artifact mtime against the newest tracked source, or a recorded source-commit stamp compared to `HEAD` — which is cheap and is the arm most likely to be wrong in the direction that matters, since neither handle sees uncommitted edits. Whichever is taken, **the `--self-test` must plant a stale artifact and watch the check go red**: the class this row belongs to is the assertion that cannot fail.
- Accept:
  - A **planted stale artifact** — a module built from a deliberately modified source, left in `target/wasm-pack/` — makes `scripts/verifier-page-build.sh --check` exit nonzero **with a message naming staleness**, verified by its message and not by its exit code alone.
  - The green path still runs in the gate's budget, and `scripts/local-gate.sh`'s recorded per-lane cost is updated to the measured figure rather than the old one.
  - **R25's Accept row 1 is stated against this mechanism**: whatever arm is chosen, the row's *"byte-identical artifacts"* clause names the thing that now enforces it, so a later reader cannot satisfy the row with two agreeing stale builds.
  - D129 §5 R9's seven in-script assertions are unchanged in content and in count — this row adds a precondition to them, it does not re-scope them.
- Notes: **the brief that raised this row named the wrong script, and the correction is load-bearing.** `scripts/wasm-pack-build.sh --check` was measured to `rm -rf "${OUT:?}"` and run `wasm-pack build` unconditionally, in every mode — it cannot pass over a stale artifact, and it additionally compares report bytes through `scripts/wasm-boundary.mjs` against a freshly emitted native corpus. Its staleness surface is its **`--self-test`**, which accepts an existing module rather than forcing one. A lane that fixes `wasm-pack-build.sh` will change nothing about this defect. **A second correction rides here**, because the same brief carried it: D63 §7 rule 1's optimizer precondition is *"pinned by exact version **or** disabled"*, and this repository now satisfies it by the **disabled** arm — `wasm-pack-build.sh` passes `--no-opt --mode no-install`, so `wasm-pack build --release` does **not** fetch binaryen on this repository's own build path any more. What remains open is the **pin**, which is what re-enabling `wasm-opt` would require first; saying the build still fetches an unpinned binaryen 117 is a statement about the tool's default, not about this repo. **One further finding recorded at R25 rather than here**, because it is that row's assertion: the one-line `SHA256SUMS` check is **tautological** — the sums string is constructed as one line and then asserted to be one line — and it is the same *"assertion that cannot fail"* class D131 was written to repair.
- Notes: **[Registrar, 2026-08-15 — CLOSED on arm (b), and the 14 066 B this row was minted on is now EXPLAINED.]** `scripts/verifier-page-build.sh` **rebuilds unconditionally** and then requires **byte-equality** with whatever module was already present, failing with a message naming `STALE ARTIFACT` and printing both digests plus what the stale bytes would have contaminated (the page, its CSP hashes, and the footer digest the page publishes about itself). Arm **(a)** — always rebuild — is refused at the site as insufficient: it makes the packaged bytes current without ever asserting they *reproduce*, which is the half R25's Accept row 1 actually owes. Arm **(c)** — mtime or a commit stamp — is refused because neither handle sees an **uncommitted** edit, which is the state a developer is actually in.
  **The planted fault is verified by MESSAGE.** `--self-test` overwrites five bytes at offset 1024 of the real module — a plausible artifact from a different build, the exact shape of the defect — runs the build path, and requires both a nonzero status **and** `STALE ARTIFACT` in the output, because a crash exits nonzero too. It restores the module afterwards.
  **Cost, re-measured on the new shape** and recorded in `scripts/local-gate.sh` where the old figure lived: `--check` **1.49 s / 1.42 s**, `--self-test` **1.29 s**, against the superseded **0.39 s / 0.37 s** — **kept beside the new numbers on purpose, because the delta IS the defect**: the old figure was the price of skipping the build. A run after a source change pays **1 m 08 s**, which is why the lane still sits after the wasm lanes; the common warm path relinks in 0.65 s.
  **Accept, clause by clause.** Row 1 — met, by message. Row 2 — met; the gate figure is updated rather than estimated. Row 3 — met at **R25**, whose Accept row 1 now cites this mechanism by name, so the row can no longer be satisfied by two agreeing stale builds; its **second** clause is unbuilt and is **R86**. Row 4 — met: D129 §5 R9's in-script assertions are unchanged in content and in count, this row adds a precondition in front of them.
  **The size gap is resolved, and the resolution is recorded at both sites.** The un-remapped control build **reproduced the pre-change in-tree artifact exactly**, from a checkout path 97 characters longer — which refutes the *"the build is not reproducible from source alone"* half of D132 §1 (f)'s disjunct and leaves **staleness** as the explanation of both this row's 14 066 B and that record's 14 677 B. Recorded in D132's 2026-08-15 note and in D63's 2026-08-15 correction. **The boundary on that inference is stated where it is made**: it is a property of this build at `08c074c`, not a re-measurement of two artifacts that no longer exist.

### R84 — No committed bundle fixture verifies to an `attested` OTS anchor, so R27's online cases cannot be written at all
- Milestone: M3
- Size: S
- Deps: after R27 (the suite whose four online cases this blocks), A25 (the calendar-bound campaign that produced the one committed artifact which does reach `Attested`, and the only source of a real upgraded `.ots`); R24 (its Accept row 2 is the other casualty), R6 (the fixture constructor any new case is built from), F13 (the committed bundle vector set), D79 (the upgrade group whose presence the plan reads), D132 §1 (c)/(g) (the measurements that name the hole). Blocking: **Q237**, the M3 gate.
- Spec: Verification — M3 bullet (MVP-SPEC.md line 174); Anchoring — `--online` semantics (line 108); Verifier web page — online mode (line 137); M3 milestone (line 156)
- Discovered by: **the D132 planning lane** (2026-08-12), which measured every probe plan over the R9 corpus empty; **re-measured and re-worded by the registrar** in the same wave, because the row was first framed as *"no fixture carries an attested anchor"*, which is false of the sealer-claimed status and true only of the verified state.
- Problem: **the claimed status and the verified state are different things, and only one of them is fixtured.** Two F13 bundle cases — `every-anchor-kind-with-receipt` and `every-anchor-kind-no-receipt` — carry the sealer-claimed `"status": "attested"` in their committed bytes, with an upgrade group. But the verifier renders **every anchor in every committed bundle `Invalid`**, and that is pinned on purpose: `vector_every_anchor_kind_bundle_is_all_invalid_and_unanchored_at_m2` asserts it, and its own doc states the point — *"the bundle records four different sealer-claimed statuses … and the report renders one answer for all four."* So the corpus proves the verifier is not fooled by a claim, and proves nothing about what happens when an anchor is genuinely attested.
  **What that makes unwritable.** R27's `Do` clause (d) and its Accept row 3 name *agreement → promotion*, *disagreement*, *mismatch → invalid* and *one-endpoint-down*; R24's Accept row 2 names the same four. All four require an anchor that verifies to `attested`, because promotion is defined off that state. None of them can be written against committed data, on either surface. The M3 gate's own clause list names the endpoint-disagreement case on both surfaces, so **this is a gate blocker for Q237 rather than an effort question**, and it cannot be discharged by scheduling.
  **A second thing the hole hides.** After D132 both surfaces derive their probe set from `ProbePlan::from_bundle`, which is **strictly wider** than the overlay's `attested` set. D132 §1 (c) proves that difference inert by construction — report, overlay and verdict-class byte-identical either way, including under a refuting answer. It cannot be proven inert by the corpus, because with no attested fixture `plan.blocks` and the attested-narrow set are **indistinguishable by committed data**. The construction argument is the only one there is, and a reader should know that.
- Do: ~~**commit an attested-anchor-bearing bundle fixture, and a receipt-bearing twin.**~~ — **[2026-08-15, AMENDED by D133 §3 R2/R9]** **materialise** an attested-anchor-bearing bundle fixture into `target/verifier-web-fixtures/` — **never committed**, on `scripts/verifier-page-browser.sh:12-19`'s already-argued precedent that a second binary copy under another name is a second source of truth that agrees on the day it is written — from material that **is** committed and frozen; and **the receipt-bearing twin is DEFERRED to R85**, whose open question *is* the twin's `tx_hash` target. Note against D133 §9, which says *"R84's Accept is amended"*: the twin and the *"what does its transaction hash point at"* question are clauses of **this `Do`**, not of the `Accept` — the `Accept`'s own amendment is the word *"committed"*, marked at rows 1 and 2 below. The shape already exists as an in-test constructor — `crates/antseal-core/src/verify/orchestration/tests.rs::attested_bundle()` — built from an R6 fixture by typed anchor substitution, re-encoded through `encode_bundle`, with the `.ots` stamping the rebuilt bundle's own `anchor_digest`; promoting that shape to a committed fixture is the shortest honest route. The real upgraded artifact A25 already produced is the input that makes it more than a hand-built claim. Decide and record two things in the same act: whether the new cases enter the **F13 bundle vector set** (which prices a fixture event against D105's rules) or live beside it as R27-owned fixtures, and what the receipt twin's transaction hash points at, given **R85**.
- Accept:
  - A ~~committed~~ **materialised** fixture verifies to at least one anchor in state `attested`, asserted by state and not by the bundle's claimed `status` field — with a negative control proving the assertion can fail. — **[2026-08-15, AMENDED by D133 §3 R2]**: the fixture file is **not** committed; what is committed is every byte it is built from (two frozen vector documents) **and the assertion itself**, `crates/antseal-core/tests/page_fixtures.rs`, which runs on every `cargo test` and goes red if the format moves under the fixture. The row's intent — an attested state that is *demonstrated*, not claimed — is met by the assertion's venue, not by the artifact's location.
  - `ProbePlan::from_bundle` over that fixture yields a non-empty `plan.blocks`, and the difference between it and the attested-narrow set is exercised by a case where the two **differ**, so D132 §1 (c)'s inertness has a witness in ~~committed data~~ **committed material and a committed assertion** and not only in argument. — **[2026-08-15, AMENDED by D133 §3 R2/R11]**, same reason; the witness is `vector_page_fixture_wide_plan_exceeds_the_narrow_overlay_set`, wide `[960767, 960768]` against a narrow set of **one row on both fixtures**.
  - R27's four online cases and R24's Accept row 2 are writable against it — that is the row's purpose, and the gate clause is the acceptance.
  - Any frozen-byte movement is priced and recorded before it happens: if the new cases join the F13 set, the freeze rules that govern an added case are cited on the change, not discovered by it.
- Notes: **two corrections to how this row was first stated, kept on the record because both change what a lane would go looking for.** (1) **`plan` itself IS fixtured**: both `every-anchor-kind-*` twins carry `ots_anchors[0].upgrade.block_height = 900000`, and the with-receipt twin carries `tx_hashes[0]`, because `ProbePlan::from_bundle` reads the upgrade height **regardless of verdict** — stated normatively in `verify/plan.rs`'s module doc (*"whatever the offline verdict made of that anchor"*) and pinned by `an_upgraded_ots_anchor_puts_its_height_in_the_plan_whatever_its_verdict`. What is missing is an **attested** anchor, not a populated plan. (2) **One committed artifact does reach `Attested`** — the A25-bootstrap upgraded `.ots`, driven there by `row_5_attested_not_headline` — so the gap is specifically an attested-anchor-bearing **bundle**, and the material for one already exists in the tree. Corroborates D129 §10 (iii) and D132 §1 (g), which measured all 21 R9 report vectors yielding `{"blocks":[],"receipt":null}` — twenty with no anchors at all, and the one that has anchors offline `invalid`.
- Notes: **[Registrar, 2026-08-15 — CLOSED, and the row ships having lost its premise. D133 ruled; two of that record's own rulings were corrected by this lane; a third proposed correction was refused.]** **The problem statement was false at its root.** *"R27's `Do` clause (d) and its Accept row 3 … and R24's Accept row 2 … cannot be written at all, on either surface"* — measured false. All four cases are already written and green in `crates/antseal-core/src/verify/orchestration/tests.rs` under a section header reading `// Accept row 2 — the mock-endpoint outcomes`, on native **and** `wasm32-unknown-unknown` (the `wasm32-core-tests` lane runs that crate's `--lib` tests), and again in `crates/antseal-cli/tests/verify_command.rs`, whose disagreement row drives **two real loopback `StubServer`s** through the production collector `probe_online` — 22 rows, green, 1.67 s. What was missing is the **browser venue's fixture**: `scripts/verifier-page-browser.mjs` receives bundles as **file paths** via `DOM.setFileInputFiles`, and no file on disk verified to `attested`. **Read the row's title as *"no bundle FILE verifies to `attested`"***.
  **What shipped.** `crates/antseal-core/tests/page_fixtures.rs` — five `vector_`-marked tests, `#![cfg(not(target_arch = "wasm32"))]` — reads two frozen documents (F13's `empty-anchor-unanchored` bundle bytes; the anchor vector's `ots-upgraded-offline` artifact and its real block-**960767** upgrade group), substitutes the anchor section wholesale, re-encodes through `encode_bundle`, and asserts `AnchorState::Attested` **by state** with `plan.blocks == [960767]`. Under `ANTSEAL_EMIT_PAGE_FIXTURES=<ABSOLUTE dir>` it also writes `attested-ots-960767.sealproof` (**4 846 B**, matching D133 §1 (d)'s first run to the byte) and the wide-versus-narrow twin `attested-plus-invalid-ots.sealproof` (4 978 B). `scripts/verifier-page-browser.sh::materialise()` runs the emitter before its Python block and **asserts each fixture by name**, because the `ls` glob cannot fail.
  **Why it binds, and why the lean could not.** A25's campaign stamped `083f87df…69df`, which **is** the `anchor_digest` of the base F13 case — the manifest is never touched, so the digest is the same 32 bytes before and after, and the frozen `.ots` binds **by construction**. The lean (build around the A25-bootstrap upgraded `.ots`) is **impossible, not costly**: it stamps a **third-party crate's `LARGE_TEST`** digest, and `anchor_digest` is SHA-256 over the bundle's own manifest envelope and cannot be chosen. This row's own note (2) was right that a committed artifact reaches `Attested` and **pointed at the wrong one**.
  **Verified by the registrar, not transcribed**: `cargo test -p antseal-core --test page_fixtures` → 5 passed; the emitter writes 4 846 B and 4 978 B; `./scripts/vector-freeze.sh` and `./scripts/format-freeze.sh` **GREEN without `--update`**; `git status -- testdata/` **empty** — so D133 R12's classification (not a format, verdict or fixture event) holds as measured. `./scripts/verifier-page-browser.sh --check` re-run in Chromium: **12 bundles rendered**, one request (the page itself), zero CSP violations, both new fixtures named in the OK lines.
  **Two claims are the lane's observation and are NOT what any committed check asserts**: that the fixture's anchor *renders* `attested` in the page, and that `plan.blocks == [960767]` was exercised in a browser (seen as the two derived esplora URLs). `verifier-page-browser.mjs` asserts only `RESULT`/`FAILURE`/`TIMEOUT` per bundle — D133 §8 item 6 says so in the record's own words — so **no committed check distinguishes an `attested` render from any other verdict**. That is R27's parity gate and is registered there, not claimed here.
  **Corrections to D133, made in this act** (see that record's 2026-08-15 correction section): **R7** says *"the eleventh fixture"* where **R11** orders a second, so there are **twelve**, and its *"no further change"* is wrong about the glob, which cannot fail; **R11**'s closing sentence names an assertion that cannot exist — byte-equality of report, overlay and verdict-class across two fixtures, one of which carries an extra anchor and therefore an extra report slot — and the true assertion, which the lane wrote, is that the **narrow set is one row on both** while the **wide set names one height against two**. A third proposed correction is **REFUSED**: D133 §8 item 3's native `verify_with_host` promotion row is described there as **discovered work**, not ordered by §5.1, so it is an open follow-on and not a defect in the record.
  **What this row did NOT do, stated so it is not assumed**: the native promotion row from real material (D133 §8 item 3) is **not built** — the bundle-layer promotion path is exercised only in the browser lane, which `local-gate.sh` does not run — and the **receipt-bearing twin is deferred to R85** with the emitter already in place (add a `receipt` to `parts`; nothing else changes).

### R85 — A receipt sealed on another chain is affirmatively refuted by the page, not left unconfirmed
- Milestone: M3
- Size: XS
- Deps: after R24 (the row that performs the fetches and renders the outcome), D132 (which ruled the probe plan and the receipt target); A17 (the CLI's chain-id guard and receipt confirmation, which the page reproduces), D55/D66 (the pinned Arbitrum pair and its browser constraints), R18 (the frozen agreed-refutation sentence this row's defect renders)
- Spec: Verifier web page — online mode, *"two public Arbitrum RPCs"* (MVP-SPEC.md line 137); Anchoring — receipt as supporting evidence (line 110); Risks (line 186)
- Discovered by: **the R24 lane** (2026-08-12), as a residue of D132's ruling; **mechanism re-measured and corrected by the registrar** in the same act — the residue was first framed as fail-closed, and it is not.
- Problem: **the page pins one chain and cannot tell that it is looking at the wrong one.** `ARBITRUM_CHAIN_ID = 42161` is a page-side constant (citing `antseal_net::network::ARBITRUM_ONE_CHAIN_ID`), and the pinned RPC pair is mainnet's. Meanwhile **no document the page can see carries a network datum**: `ProbePlan` has exactly two fields, `blocks` and `receipt`; `VerificationReport` has seven, and the word *network* appears nowhere in it. So the page cannot ask which chain a receipt belongs to.
  **What actually happens to a development-network receipt** — measured against the mechanism, not assumed from the guard's name: the chain-id guard **passes**, because both pinned endpoints correctly report 42161; `eth_getTransactionReceipt` then returns `null` from both; the pair **agrees**; and the page renders the frozen line *"both RPC endpoints agree the recorded transaction is not on chain"*. That is an **agreed refutation** of a transaction which exists — on a different chain. The failure is not an absence of confirmation, it is a **false statement about the evidence**, and it is the one shape a fail-closed reading would have predicted could not happen.
  **`wrong-chain` is reachable, but only the other way**: it fires when a user overrides the endpoints to Sepolia RPCs, which then report 421614 against the pinned 42161. That path fails closed correctly. The default path does not.
- Do: **decide what the page may say about a receipt whose chain it cannot establish**, and record the decision rather than patching the string. Arms: **(a)** render the not-on-chain refutation **only** when the seal's network is known to be the probed one — which requires a datum that does not exist, so it is a format question and not a page question, and should be routed as one; **(b)** demote the both-null case from an agreed refutation to a non-conclusion, which costs the genuine mainnet refutation its strength and must be argued against D56 O7's precedent that a refutation needs a positive answer; **(c)** state the probed chain in the rendered line, so the sentence is true as written whatever chain the receipt belongs to — the cheapest arm, and the only one that touches R18's frozen set, which is why it is a decision and not an edit. The CLI shares the guard and should be checked for the same shape in the same act.
- Accept:
  - A receipt whose transaction is not on the probed chain does not produce a sentence asserting it is not on chain, or the sentence names the chain that was probed — whichever arm is ruled, asserted by a test with a negative control.
  - The `wrong-chain` override path is unchanged and still fails closed.
  - Whatever is decided is recorded where the guard lives, so the next reader of `ARBITRUM_CHAIN_ID` finds the reasoning beside the constant rather than in a wave record.
- Notes: **the citation this row arrived with is corrected**: no D132 §5 rule states the number 42161. The chain-id guard is **§5 R4**'s (*"the chain-id guard first (a mismatch is the `wrong-chain` class)"*), and **§5 R5** is the `endpoints.identities` rule — *"four entries on mainnet, receipt or no receipt"* — which is what the page's own source comment cites, and cites correctly. The number is the page's own pin. **Size stays XS for the edit and the row is not the edit**: as with R79 and R81, the ruling is the expensive half, and arm (a) is a format question wearing a page question's clothes.
- Notes: **[D133 §3 R9 + §9, 2026-08-14 — this row inherits R84's receipt-bearing fixture twin.]** R84's `Do` asked for *"a receipt-bearing twin"* and for a decision on *"what the receipt twin's transaction hash points at, given R85"*. That question **is this row's**, and it is open: a fixture whose `tx_hash` points at nothing would commit a **demonstration of the defect above** and would need re-doing the moment this row rules. **None of R27's four block cases needs a receipt**, so nothing is blocked by the deferral. The build is one line when the ruling exists: the emitter `crates/antseal-core/tests/page_fixtures.rs` (`ANTSEAL_EMIT_PAGE_FIXTURES=<ABSOLUTE dir>`) constructs the twin by adding a `receipt` to `parts`; nothing else changes, and the fixture is **materialised into `target/`, never committed** (D133 §3 R2). Whichever arm this row takes, the twin is the artifact that demonstrates it — so the ruling and its fixture should land in one act rather than two.
- Notes: **[Registrar, 2026-08-16 — wave 21 closing. CLOSED across TWO acts, all three Accept rows met. The split is itself worth recording.]** **Act 1 stopped at a write-scope boundary rather than half-landing D137's ruling, and that was correct**: D137 §3 R5 (*"the illegal state is unrepresentable"*) forces `ProbeLog::with_receipt(probe, chain_id)`, and the chain id is a **measurement made by the host**, so no design keeps it inside one crate — seven files outside act 1's scope move the moment R1–R4 move. **Landed in act 1**: D136 §2 R12's footer-label repair (`verifier-web/index.template.html:146` now reads `module build`, id `page-build` unchanged, exactly one `<code>`, longest hex run in the label = 1; eight negative controls, each RED by message); D137 §3 R10 (both receipt lines join `single_sourced()`, closing §1 (f)'s hole where a renderer could copy the frozen sentence and nothing reddened); D137 §3 R11 (`every_single_sourced_needle_occurs_in_the_rendered_set`, with a separately-asserted table-size floor so the loop cannot go vacuous); D137 §8 site 1 (the reasoning beside `const ARBITRUM_CHAIN_ID` in the page); and a cross-surface pin proving the CLI prints the module's sentence byte-for-byte.
  **Act 2, fourteen files, and the frozen sentence moved deliberately.** Three snapshot rows respelled and **two added — five total** — all inside the `── receipt echo, endpoints, change-only deltas ──` subsection; sections 1–10 did not move. The load-bearing one: `receipt: both RPC endpoints agree the recorded transaction is not on chain — supporting evidence only` → `receipt: both RPC endpoints agree the recorded transaction is not on Arbitrum chain 42161 — supporting evidence only`. **The two added rows are the Sepolia twins (chain `421614`) and they are why the snapshot has teeth**: with one instance each, an implementation that interpolated a constant would produce a byte-stable file forever. **Thirteen negative controls, each RED by its message**, including the one that matters most for the register's credibility — act 1's staged tripwire was verified to fire *before* it was flipped (`left: Object {"not-on-chain": Object {"chain_id": Number(42161)}} / right: String("not-on-chain")`), so the pin was proven real rather than assumed. The `wrong-chain` override path is **unchanged** and `chain_id_mismatch_is_unavailable_not_agreed` is green **unmodified**, which is Accept row 2. **Machine: this local host, for every clause.** Browser arm after the template and wording changes: `--check` EXIT=0, **100 OK rows** (86 R27's, 14 this row's), 72.6 s; `--self-test` 8.2 s; `--seed-test` 51.3 s.
  **Two corrections this row makes to D137, recorded at that record's citations.** (1) **§5 point 3 names a string the page never renders**: it asks the rendered receipt element to carry the failed line naming `wrong-chain`, but `probe_failure_class_label(ProbeFailureClass::WrongChain)` is `"wrong chain"` — with a **space**; the hyphenated form is the evidence-document **class token**, not display text. The lane asserted the **discriminator** instead — a failure line names the endpoints and no chain, an absence line names the chain and no endpoint — which is §3 R1's own dividing rule and restates no frozen string. (2) **§6.1's assertion ordering makes its own assertion 3 unfirable on the obvious plant**: assertion 2 pins each of the four renders to its full expected string and fires first, so assertion 3 can never fail without assertion 2 having already aborted the test. It was shown fallible only by planting the sentence **and** the expected strings — i.e. by simulating a lane that updates the test instead of the code. Assertion 3 is still worth having; it guards a narrower case than §6.1 implies.
  **One correction the wave's own brief needed, recorded so it is not repeated**: the brief warned that D137 §3 R9 *"undercounts its own snapshot diff"*. It does not. R9's literal sentence is *"Exactly **three** snapshot rows change and **two** are added"* and it tells the reviewer to check for *"exactly the five rows above"*. D137 was explicit; act 1's report is what mis-stated it, and the warning was noise.
  **A pre-existing driver defect fixed in passing.** `scripts/verifier-page-browser.mjs`'s `rmSync(profile, {recursive:true, force:true})` in `session`'s `finally` throws `ENOTEMPTY` on a loaded host — SIGKILL is asynchronous and chromium's helpers keep writing — and it threw **out of a `finally`**, killing the whole suite mid-run with a node stack trace instead of a row. Measured at the first online case; fixed with `maxRetries: 20, retryDelay: 100`.
  **Discovered work, named rather than hidden.** `ReceiptConfirmation::NotOnChain` (`crates/antseal-core/src/anchor/model.rs:550`) and `ArbitrumConfirmation::Absent` (`crates/antseal-anchor/src/arbitrum/confirm.rs:101`) still carry *"the transaction is not on chain"* — the same over-claim D137 §3 R8 corrected on the wasm token, in two more places; **registered as R87**, joined to the `WorkMetadata::signature_scheme` site rather than given its own id, because all three are one doc-sweep and R76/R77 are the same family in the same file. Still open and **not** minted: `ReceiptEchoOutcome` has no `token()` method (D137 §12's own list), and `crates/antseal-wasm/tests/boundary.rs` carries **no** receipt-bearing evidence document, so `ReceiptEntry::chain_id` has no Rust-side test — only the browser arm covers it.

### R86 — R25's Accept row 1 says "CI job enforces this", and no such job exists
- Milestone: M3
- Size: S
- Deps: after R25 (the row whose Accept clause this is), R83 (the mechanism that makes a packaged artifact the tree's, and without which two runners could agree on two stale builds); D63 §7 rule 1–2 (the path remap, discharged 2026-08-15, and the environment axes still unproven), D129 §5 R9 (the built-artifact assertions the job would run beside), Q1 (the CI skeleton and its lane conventions). Composes with R82 (a second machine surface nothing witnesses) and blocks nothing, but is named by **Q237**'s evidence rule.
- Spec: Page provenance — reproducible build and published hash (MVP-SPEC.md line 139); Risks — malicious verifier host (line 186); M3 milestone (line 156)
- Discovered by: **the R25 lane** (2026-08-15), on closing Accept row 1's first clause and finding the second had no instrument; registered by the registrar in the same act.
- Problem: **the property is demonstrated and nothing repeats it.** After the path remap, six reproductions at `08c074c` are byte-identical in module **and** glue — a 25-character checkout path against a 122-character one, and a different `$CARGO_HOME` — at module `baee3fc9…22a3`, 1 853 031 B. That is Accept row 1's first clause, met. Its second clause is *"CI job enforces this"*, and the enforcement does not exist: the two-runner comparison that **found** the defect (1 853 735 B on GitHub Actions against 1 853 543 B here, same commit) was run **by hand**, and `.github/workflows/pages.yml` has a single `publish` job on a single `ubuntu-latest` runner, which cannot compare a build with anything.
  **What the tree does have, and why it is not this.** `scripts/wasm-pack-build.sh` now scans the module and the glue for the builder's `$repo`, `$CARGO_HOME`, `/home/`, `/Users/` and `/root/`, and its `--self-test` plants a builder path and requires the scan to go red. That is a real instrument and it is the **local** half: it proves no path from **this** machine reached **this** artifact. It cannot prove two machines agree, because it never sees a second artifact — and the defect it replaces was invisible to every assertion that compared a build with itself.
  **Why the gap is not merely theoretical.** The remap is carried by one environment variable on one command. A wasm-pack upgrade that reorders its arguments, a `--config` refactor of the kind D63's own correction shows silently drops the getrandom `--cfg`, or a new dependency emitting absolute paths would each move the bytes on one machine and not the other; the failure surfaces as a footer digest that does not match a rebuild, which is exactly the class R83 was minted for and exactly what a human notices late or not at all.
- Do: **build the job that produces the module twice under deliberately different conditions and asserts byte-equality, and name its tier on the record.** The variation must include the two axes now proven load-bearing — the checkout path and `$CARGO_HOME` — because those are the two that were measured to move the bytes; a second build under identical conditions asserts only that `rustc` is deterministic, which D63 §1 (k) already measured and which is not the property at risk. **The tier is the decision, not the script.** Three shapes with three prices, against the maintainer's standing Actions-minutes constraint (the same constraint that keeps `pages.yml` and Q19 `workflow_dispatch`-only and not required contexts): **(a)** a required context on every push — the strongest and the most expensive, and it pays a wasm32 release build twice; **(b)** a second build inside the existing `pages.yml` job, so the deploy cannot publish a digest two builds disagree on — cheapest, and it only runs when somebody deploys; **(c)** a dispatch-only lane like Q19's, which is enforcement in name and a human's memory in fact, and would be this row's own defect one level out. Whichever is taken, the job must fail **naming the two digests**, as R83's guard does, so the reader is not left comparing hashes by eye.
- Accept:
  - A CI job builds the module **twice** with the checkout path and `$CARGO_HOME` deliberately different between the builds, and asserts the two modules **and** the two glue files are byte-identical, failing with both digests printed.
  - The job's tier is chosen **on the record**, with its Actions-minutes cost stated, and R25's Accept row 1 cites the job by name so the clause can no longer be read as satisfied by a manual measurement.
  - A **planted regression** — the remap flag removed from one of the two builds — turns the job red, verified by its message and not by exit code alone.
  - The job does not silently duplicate R83's local scan: the scan proves no builder path is present, this proves two builders agree, and the failure messages say which property failed.
- Notes: **this row is Accept row 1's second half and nothing else.** The first half is closed by measurement and recorded at R25 and in `docs/wasm-toolchain.md` §4.1 — the remap channel, the four candidates and why three of them silently drop `.cargo/config.toml`'s getrandom `--cfg`, and the six reproductions. **Two boundaries the row must not over-claim.** (1) The axes proven independent are the **checkout path** and **`$CARGO_HOME`**; the six builds shared a locale, `TZ`, `HOME`, user and host, so D63 §7 rule 2's list is only partly discharged and a job that varies nothing else proves nothing about the rest. (2) Byte-identity is asserted at a **commit**: it is not a claim that any two commits agree, and a job that caches across commits would assert the wrong thing. **The register's own framing is kept honest here**: nothing about this row is blocked by R83 — R83 is what makes it meaningful, because two runners each re-using their own stale `target/` would agree with themselves and prove nothing at all.
- Notes: **[Registrar, 2026-08-16 — wave 21 closing. CLOSED, and the residue is named so the tick cannot be read as "green in CI".]** **The job exists and its tier is on the record.** D135 §3 R1 ruled it **deploy-gated**: the comparison runs **inside `.github/workflows/pages.yml`'s existing `publish` job**, invoked from `scripts/pages-publish.sh --build`, positioned so a red aborts **before** `actions/configure-pages` — so a failure ends the job with nothing staged and nothing published. **It is a STEP, not a job**, and the register must say so: measured 2026-08-16, `.github/workflows/` contains no two-build byte-identity job at all, and the enforcement is the step named `build and check the page` (`pages.yml:78-79`). Any row citing *"R86's job"* by name is citing something that does not exist; cite the step and its workflow. The tier's price and its refused alternatives are pre-measured at D135 §10, and **D135 §3 R6 makes promotion raise-only by decision** — the pre-priced arm and the measured hit rate are recorded there so that decision does not re-derive the arithmetic. **Q238** owns the promotion, gated on a re-measured bill under the allowance, before the repository goes public.
  **What was proven, and where.** The two builds differ on the two axes measured load-bearing — **checkout path** and **`$CARGO_HOME`** — and the comparison asserts the **module and the glue** are byte-identical, failing with **both digests printed**, as R83's guard does. The **planted regression** required by Accept row 3 (the remap flag removed from one of the two builds) was driven end to end and turned the check red **by its message**, not by exit code alone. R83's local scan is not duplicated: the scan proves no builder path is present, this proves two builders agree, and the failure messages say which property failed.
  **The residue, stated plainly so no gate line launders it: this job has NEVER run on a hosted runner.** `pages.yml` is `workflow_dispatch:` and nothing else, so the step runs only when somebody deploys, and the only deploys on record are two — 2026-08-14 (`31847839638`, at `08c074c`) and 2026-08-15 (`31873422229`, at `9317a35`), **both predating this step**. Everything above was proven **on this local host** by driving `scripts/pages-publish.sh --build` and its planted-fault control directly. **Q237 must name that machine for every clause this row proves**, and must not record it as a CI green. **The exposure that follows is D135 §3 R4.1's, in its own words**: *"Nothing between deploys. A commit that breaks reproducibility is detected at the next deploy, not at the push that broke it"* — measured at **days to weeks**, and acceptable only while the repository is private and nobody has been invited to reproduce anything. That is the whole content of **Q238**.
  **Two boundaries this row still does not claim**, unchanged from its `Notes` above: the six reproductions shared a locale, `TZ`, `HOME`, user and host, so D63 §7 rule 2's axis list is only partly discharged; and byte-identity is asserted **at a commit**, never across commits — `ANTSEAL_SOURCE_COMMIT` alone moves the module's bytes (D135 §1.5), so a job caching across commits would assert the wrong thing.
  **One correction to R25's own words, applied at R25's Accept row 1 rather than here**: D135 §4 R3 re-words *"different runners"* to *"different environments — differing checkout path and differing `$CARGO_HOME`"*, because the historical two-runner comparison worked precisely because the runners happened to differ in `$CARGO_HOME`; the environment axis did the work and the runner identity did none.
  **`scripts/reproducible-build.sh` is UNTRACKED at the moment of this tick** (`git status --short` → `??`), and so is `docs/decisions/D135-*.md`. The row is ticked on measured behaviour in the working tree; a reader checking out `d8ce569` will not find either file.

### R87 — Three field docs describe an integration that already happened, and a renderer takes its wording from one of them
- Milestone: M3
- Size: XS
- Deps: after R18 (the wording set whose `NotEvaluated` spelling is derived from the first stale sentence), C14 (the stage whose landing falsified it), R12/A17 (the receipt confirmation the other two sites describe), D137 §3 R8 (the identical over-claim, already corrected on the wasm token — this row is the same repair at the two sites D137 left standing). **R76 is the same class on a fourth field and stays separately open.**
- Spec: Verifier — the signature-scheme label (MVP-SPEC.md line 97, line 132); Anchoring — the receipt as supporting evidence (line 110); Reveal bundle — report contents (line 114)
- Discovered by: **the R77/R78 lane** (2026-08-15) for the first site, and **the R85 act-2 lane** (2026-08-16) for the other two, independently and by the same method — trying to satisfy a doc and finding the tree had moved past it. Neither lane expanded its own row to cover it, which is the correct behaviour and the reason this needs an id.
- Problem: **three shipped rustdocs state as future what the tree has already done, and one of them is a renderer's source of wording.**
  **(1) `WorkMetadata::signature_scheme`** — `crates/antseal-core/src/verify/report.rs:271-273` reads *"C14 supplies the datum at integration; [`SignatureScheme::NotEvaluated`] until then."* **C14 has landed.** `crates/antseal-core/src/verify/pipeline.rs:610-611` is `// ── Stage 5: sig_policy + signatures (C14) ──` / `let scheme = check_signatures(&proof)?;`, unconditional — no option, no `cfg`, no branch — and its result is the `signature_scheme:` field at `pipeline.rs:634` (`check_signatures` itself at `pipeline.rs:1092`). Measured 2026-08-16: `SignatureScheme::NotEvaluated` is **assigned at exactly one site in the whole tree and that site is a test** — `crates/antseal-core/src/verify/mod.rs:550`, inside the `#[cfg(test)]` module opened at `:496`, in `empty_anchor_report_is_representable`. So the variant a production doc promises is produced by no production path. The same file repeats the claim at `report.rs:209-211` (*"a stage that has not run (`NotEvaluated` until C14)"*), and **`crates/antseal-core/src/verify/wording.rs:417-419` derives the rendered spelling from that sentence in as many words** — *"[`SignatureScheme::NotEvaluated`]'s spelling is dictated by its own field doc"* — producing the string at `wording.rs:425-428`. A wording set taking its instruction from a falsified sentence is the defect, not the variant's existence.
  **The precision a lane must not lose**: `StorageLinkageResult::NotEvaluated` is a **different variant on a different enum in the same file** and *is* returned in production, at `pipeline.rs:1215`, when `VerifyOptions::storage_linkage()` is off. A grep for `NotEvaluated` finds it and will suggest this row is wrong. It is not; the two names collide and only the `SignatureScheme` one is unreachable.
  **(2) and (3) — one sentence, two crates, and it over-claims.** `ReceiptConfirmation::NotOnChain` (`crates/antseal-core/src/anchor/model.rs:550`, variant at `:551`) and `ArbitrumConfirmation::Absent` (`crates/antseal-anchor/src/arbitrum/confirm.rs:101`, variant at `:102`) both read, byte-identically, *"Both endpoints agreed the transaction is not on chain."* That is **exactly the universal claim D137 §3 R1 ruled false** — two endpoints on Arbitrum One establish nothing about a transaction on chain 421614 — and D137 §3 R8 corrected it on the wasm evidence token while deliberately leaving these two, which no rule of that record reaches. The rendered sentences moved at R85 act 2; these two docs did not, so the tree now says one thing to a user and the opposite to a reader of the type.
- Do: correct all three at the doc, in one act, and record what each now says. **(1)** rewrite `report.rs:271-273` (and `:209-211`) to state what is true — the stage runs on every verify, the variant is a representable-but-unproduced state kept for report-v1 compatibility and for tests — then rewrite `wording.rs:417-419` so the renderer's instruction cites the corrected sentence rather than the promise. **Do not delete the variant**: `SignatureScheme::ALL` (`report.rs:340`) and `wire_name` (`:359`) are part of the frozen report-v1 surface and removing it is a **format event** under D105, not a doc repair. **(2)/(3)** re-spell both doc lines on D137 §3 R1's dividing rule — a sentence that asserts presence or absence names the chain it asserts about — matching the rendered lines R85 act 2 shipped. **Zero behaviour change**: no variant, no field, no wire key, no rendered byte.
- Accept:
  - No doc in `crates/` states that C14 is pending, and a test or scan names the corrected sentence so the claim cannot silently return; `wording.rs`'s derivation cites the corrected doc by symbol, not by restating it.
  - `SignatureScheme::NotEvaluated`'s doc says what it is — representable, serialized, produced by no production path today — and names the one test that constructs it, so a reader does not conclude it is dead and delete it.
  - Neither `ReceiptConfirmation::NotOnChain` nor `ArbitrumConfirmation::Absent` carries an unqualified *"not on chain"*; both read consistently with the R18-frozen sentence R85 act 2 landed, and the correspondence is asserted rather than eyeballed.
  - `REPORT_VERSION` stays 1, `SignatureScheme::ALL` keeps four members, no frozen wording byte moves, and `verdict-wording.txt` is unchanged by this row.
- Notes: **the Q112 class** — shipped code whose doc is falsified by the tree — so rule 8 mints a row rather than a ledger line, and the deciding fact is that a *renderer* reads one of the three sentences. **This is the third and fourth instance in `report.rs`'s docs**: **R76** (`AnchorResult::source`, M2, still open) and **R77** (`SignatureScheme::Other`, closed 2026-08-16) are the first two, and R77's Notes already say whoever takes one should take both. This row is the reason to take all of them in one act: four repairs in one file cost barely more than one, and the pattern — a doc written against a plan, outliving the plan — is what the sweep is for. **Sizing**: XS, and unlike R78/R79/R81 there is no ruling to make; every arm is settled by D137 §3 R1 and by measurement.

### R88 — No committed OTS artifact lacks a pending branch, so an agreed online refutation can never render `invalid` at the bundle layer
- Milestone: M3
- Size: S
- Deps: after R27 (the row that measured it while building the mismatch case), D133 §3 R8 / §5.2 (the two rulings that promise the unreachable outcome), D56 O5/O6/O7 (the classifier precedence that suppresses it), A25 (the campaign that produced every committed `.ots`), D93 (online-refutation precedence), R84 (the fixture route, and the freeze-event rules any new artifact pays)
- Spec: Verification — online must-agree overlay, advisory only (MVP-SPEC.md lines 136–137); Anchoring — OTS upgrade and attestation (lines 105–108); M3 milestone (line 156)
- Discovered by: **the R27 lane** (2026-08-16), by building D133 §5.2's mismatch case and measuring what it actually renders instead of asserting what the record predicted.
- Problem: **the strongest online refutation the committed corpus can exhibit is a suppressed one, and two rulings promise otherwise.** With both endpoints serving block 960768's real header against an embedded 960767 header, the rendered row is
  > `ots-1: agreed online evidence contradicts the embedded header (anchor-ots-online-header-mismatch), but a pending attestation out-votes the refutation; recorded, not promoted`
  — `NotPromoted{RefutationSuppressed}`, class `outcome`, **not** `refuted`. The cause is not a fixture choice: the artifact is A25's **merged** `.ots`, so it carries evaluable **pending** calendar branches, and `classify_ots`'s **O5 out-ranks O6/O7** saying so in as many words. The frozen corpus had already recorded the mechanism and D133 did not notice it — `ots-upgraded-online-block-absent` is pinned at `state: pending, suppressed: ["anchor-ots-online-block-absent"]`.
  **It cannot be fixed by choosing another fixture.** `testdata/vectors/v1/anchor/anchor.json` holds exactly **one** upgraded OTS artifact, so **no committed material can drive an agreed refutation to `invalid` at the bundle layer.** The consequence is not cosmetic: three documents promise an outcome no test can produce, and a lane sent to assert it will either weaken its assertion silently or plant a fixture to make the document true.
- Do: **decide which of the three arms the project takes, and correct every document that promises `invalid` in the same act.** **(a) Mint a no-pending-branch upgraded artifact** so O6/O7 can be reached at the bundle layer — the only arm that makes the promised row real, and it costs a **freeze event** over `testdata/` which must be argued as one under R84/D133's rules, not slipped in. **(b) Accept that `RefutationSuppressed` is the strongest outcome the corpus can exhibit**, assert exactly that, and correct D133 §3 R8's *"mismatch → invalid"* row, D133 §5.2's *"overlay row `Refuted` with code `anchor-ots-online-header-mismatch`"*, R27's inherited clauses and the M3 `Gate:` line — cheapest, and it is what the tree already does today. **(c) Reach O6/O7 at the classifier layer only**, where a unit test constructs a single-branch artifact in memory and never touches `testdata/` — buys the coverage without the freeze event, at the cost of never exercising the bundle-layer composition.
  Whichever arm: the **differential** the R27 lane already built must survive — a row proving *"carries the code"* is not true of every non-promoted outcome — because that is what distinguishes a suppressed refutation from an absent one.
- Accept:
  - One recorded ruling, cited from D133's correction section and from R27's row, so the promise and its refutation cannot be read apart.
  - The rendered outcome for the agreed-mismatch case is asserted **by its exact text and its exact class**, not by a substring, with a negative control that a different non-promoted outcome does not carry the same code.
  - If (a): the freeze event is argued on the record before the bytes land, `./scripts/vector-freeze.sh` is re-run and the digest change is the act's subject rather than a side effect.
  - If (b) or (c): every document promising `invalid` for this case is corrected at its citation — D133 §3 R8 (the perturbation table's outcome column) and §5.2, R27's D132 and D133 Notes, and the M3 `Gate:` clause — and no document is left restating the old prediction.
  - `REPORT_VERSION` stays 1 and no report byte moves: the overlay is advisory and this row changes no offline verdict.
- Notes: **the Q112 class by its consequence rather than its surface** — nothing here is broken code, but three shipped rulings describe an outcome the shipped classifier cannot produce from shipped data, and a row is what makes that a decision rather than a discrepancy. **The measurement is R27's and is not re-derivable from reading**: D56's precedence and the corpus's contents are each individually documented and the conclusion falls out only when they are put together against a real render. **Take it with the D133 correction**, which the registrar has already appended — this row is the product half of that ledger line and the correction is its evidence.

### R89 — `LiveBlobOutcome::FetchFailed` still carries a `String` where the channel is now a closed class
- Milestone: M3
- Size: XS
- Deps: after R79 (the ruling that closed the channel and minted `antseal_net::FetchFailureClass`), R11 (the taxonomy discipline the class follows), R18 (the frozen row `Transport`'s label already renders), R21 (the type this widens), D137 §3 R9 (the wording-snapshot event `BackendRefused`'s label would be)
- Spec: Verifier — the storage layer and `--live` (MVP-SPEC.md line 119); the sealer in the adversary position beside the display guardrail (line 121); M3 milestone (line 156)
- Discovered by: **the R79 lane** (2026-08-16), and named on the record rather than closed, because the type change reaches `antseal-core` and the wording half is a frozen-set event.
- Problem: **type-precision debt, not an open channel — and the distinction is the row.** After R79 the string that lands in `LiveBlobOutcome::FetchFailed { reason: String }` (`crates/antseal-core/src/verify/orchestration.rs`, enum at `:219`, variant `:227-237`, field `reason` at `:236`) is `FetchFailureClass::label`'s `&'static str`, produced by a wildcard-free `of()` over all nine `StorageError` variants (`crates/antseal-net/src/live.rs:129` for the class, `ALL: [Self; 2]` at `:147`). No adversary-supplied byte can reach a display line through it any more. What is left is that the **type still admits one**: any caller can construct `FetchFailed { reason: "…".into() }` with arbitrary content, and only convention stops it. The lane's own doc is explicit that it is **understated rather than falsified** — the guarantee holds today by the one production path, not by construction.
- Do: move the class into `antseal-core` beside `LiveBlobOutcome` and make the variant carry it, so `antseal-net`'s boundary mapping converts once and the free-form field disappears; add a `live_fetch_failure_class_label` in `crates/antseal-core/src/verify/wording.rs` beside `probe_failure_class_label` (`wording.rs:855`), which is the shape the label already imitates and where its own doc says it belongs. **Price the wording half before starting, because the two halves cost differently.** `Transport`'s label is `"transport failure"` — exactly what the R18-frozen snapshot already renders through `live_blob_fetch_error_line("unit 7", "transport failure")` — so that half **moves no frozen byte**. `BackendRefused`'s *"the backend refused the read"* is a **new rendered string with no R18 row**, so giving it one is a wording-snapshot event of D137 §3 R9's shape: a deliberate `ANTSEAL_BLESS=1` re-bless, justified in review, never a tidy-up.
- Accept:
  - `LiveBlobOutcome::FetchFailed` carries the closed class rather than a `String`, and constructing it with arbitrary text is a compile error.
  - The `antseal-net` → `antseal-core` conversion is wildcard-free and stays the only match over `StorageError` on the live path, so a new variant forces one decision and not two.
  - The label has exactly one home; `crates/antseal-net`'s copy is deleted in the same change rather than left to agree by coincidence, and the two docs cite each other as R79 requires of this pair.
  - If `BackendRefused` gains a frozen row: the snapshot diff is exactly that row, the re-bless is recorded as a wording-snapshot event, and `no_renderer_source_spells_a_frozen_verdict_string` is green with the new needle.
  - Exit codes untouched — D69 §3 R6 keeps the live layer exit-code-free at the type level, and this row must not reintroduce them.
- Notes: **R79 is closed and this is not a re-opening**: the hazard R79 was minted for — remote bytes reaching a display line — is shut at the boundary, and this row is about where the guarantee is *stated*. Recorded as a row rather than a ledger line because it moves a public type and, on one arm, a frozen string; both are product surfaces. **The measured cost is the reason it is XS and separable**: one label already matches the frozen byte, so the change can land in two steps if the snapshot event needs its own act.
- Notes: **[Registrar, 2026-08-17 — wave 22 closing. CLOSED whole, in one act, and the `Problem` paragraph above is now HISTORY: its locators (`orchestration.rs:219/227-237/236`, `antseal-net/src/live.rs:129`, `ALL` at `:147`) describe the pre-R89 tree and must not be followed.]** The class now lives at `crates/antseal-core/src/verify/orchestration.rs:263` (`ALL` `:282`, `token` `:286`); the variant carries it at `:331`; the label is `wording.rs:779`. `antseal-net` keeps only the boundary classifier `fetch_failure_class` (`live.rs:164`) and a re-export (`:74`), and the sole production construction site is `live/manifest.rs:413`. **Two of the closing lane's own claims were wrong and are corrected on the TODO row rather than repeated**: `live_fetch_failure_class_label` is **115 lines and a section banner** from `probe_failure_class_label`, not *"immediately above"* it; and the classifier has **three** match arms, not nine — totality over nine `StorageError` variants was measured and holds, but variants are not arms. **Two Accept clauses landed differently than written and both are better for it.** The clause *"the two docs cite each other as R79 requires of this pair"* is satisfied only on the core side (`wording.rs:766-767` correctly reads *"Until R89 the live half was spelled in `antseal-net` instead"*): the reverse direction is **four stale sentences in three files** that still attribute the class to `antseal-net`, one of them citing the deleted `FetchFailureClass::label` — outside the closing lane's write scope, registered as **R92**, not swallowed. And the `BackendRefused` clause's cost was **mispriced by this row**: D137 §3 R9's expensive case is *respelling* shipped sentences; R89 adds one line and respells none, so the review burden is R19's cheap case while the re-bless stays mandatory. **Three things the row never named, all landed:** the generator's hard-coded label became an `ALL` sweep behind an anti-vacuity floor; `single_sourced()`'s needle floor went 29 → 30 with the table at exactly 30; and the `--json` fetch-failure shape (`{"fetch-failed":{"class":"Transport"}}`) — a D65 tier-C surface that was **pinned nowhere at all** — is now pinned by `crates/antseal-core/tests/r89_live_outcome_json.rs`. **What R89 deliberately did NOT fix, and R91 now carries**: `token()` returns `"transport"`/`"backend-refused"` while the serde discriminant is `"Transport"`/`"BackendRefused"`, and the doc at `orchestration.rs:258-261` states that divergence as *intentional* without anything asserting either spelling against the other. **Venue**: first measured against the **uncommitted** tree at HEAD `496f1a6`, then committed by the orchestrator **during this registration** as `1830618` (with Q29); every locator was re-verified at the new HEAD rather than carried forward, and `git diff 496f1a6 1830618` on `orchestration.rs` is exactly the measured change. **No hosted runner has seen it** — the commit is local. The lane's plants and green suites are its own measurements on this local host and were **not** re-run, a `cargo test --workspace` having held the build lock throughout.

### R90 — The live section's unit rows are ordered by neither of the two promises made about them
- Milestone: M3
- Size: XS
- Deps: after R81 (the row whose end-to-end test observed it), R11 (`records_from_manifest`, whose rustdoc makes the manifest-order promise), R21 (the renderer that emits the section), D65 (the tier the `--json` array's order does or does not sit in)
- Spec: Verifier — the storage layer and `--live` (MVP-SPEC.md line 119); CLI surface — `--json` machine-readable output (lines 147–149)
- Discovered by: **the R81 end-to-end lane** (2026-08-16), whose own tests are deliberately order-agnostic about units precisely so they do not pin an order nobody has ruled. **Observed, not introduced.**
- Problem: **two builders order the same rows differently and nothing asserts either.** `collect_live` (`crates/antseal-cli/src/verify_host.rs:412`) emits unit rows in **bundle-reveal order** — covered reveals then non-covered, measured as `unit 0, unit 2, unit 3, unit 4, unit 1 (raw mirror), unit 5` on `shapes::multi_file()` — while `records_from_manifest`'s rustdoc promises the **work-global manifest order**. A user comparing two `verify --live` renderings of the same work built by different routes sees rows in different sequences, and `--json`'s array order is a machine-visible property that no test pins on either side.
- Do: **rule the order once and assert it at both builders.** The candidates are the manifest's work-global unit order (what the doc promises, and the order every other rendering of units in the product uses) or the bundle's reveal order (what the CLI actually does, and the order in which the ciphertexts are in hand). Then either correct the rustdoc or correct the builder, and pin the chosen order with a test on **each** producer — one that would go red if either changed independently, since the defect is precisely that the two are free to drift. Decide explicitly whether `--json`'s live array order is a D65 tier commitment or is documented as unordered; an unpinned array in a documented contract is the weaker of the two and should be said out loud if it is chosen.
- Accept:
  - One order is stated at one place, and both `collect_live` and `records_from_manifest` are asserted against it — not against each other, so a shared mistake cannot pass.
  - The manifest row's position relative to the unit rows is part of the ruling (today it is last, and R81's test pins that at one route only).
  - `--json`'s live array order is dispositioned against D65 — tier-committed with a test, or documented as unordered with the reason — and `ENVELOPE_VERSION` stays 1 either way.
  - No frozen wording moves: this row changes sequence, never a sentence.
- Notes: **XS and low-stakes on its own, which is why it is registered rather than left in a lane's report** — an unruled order is exactly the thing a later parity or snapshot gate accidentally pins, and then it is expensive. **Take it with R89 or with whatever next touches the live section**; both are small, both live in the same three files, and neither justifies a wave of its own. *(Registrar, 2026-08-17: **R89 closed without taking it**, so the pairing partner is now **R91** — the same shape one layer down, two producers of one vocabulary with nothing asserting either. R90 is ordering, R91 is spelling, and a lane opening `verify_host.rs` and `orchestration.rs` should close both.)*

### R91 — One `--json` document spells the failure vocabulary three ways, and the spelling nothing reads is the one that was designed to be read
- Milestone: M3
- Size: S
- Deps: after R89 (which carried the class into `antseal-core`, where the divergence is now visible in one file), R79 (which shipped both spellings), R21 (the composition that emits the live member), R11 (the taxonomy discipline), D65 (the tier the machine surface sits in), D64 §3 (the overlay's fixed structure)
- Spec: CLI surface — `--json` machine-readable output (MVP-SPEC.md lines 147–149); Verifier — the storage layer and `--live` (line 119); the online advisory overlay (line 137)
- Discovered by: **the wave-22 registrar** (2026-08-17), while re-measuring R89's evidence. **Observed, not introduced** — R79 shipped both spellings in `antseal-net` and R89 relocated them; neither row created the divergence and neither was wrong to leave it.
- Problem: **three surfaces, one vocabulary, no assertion between any two of them.** (i) `FetchFailureClass::token()` (`crates/antseal-core/src/verify/orchestration.rs:286`) returns `"transport"` / `"backend-refused"`, while the type derives plain `Serialize`/`Deserialize` with **no `#[serde(rename_all)]`**, so the wire discriminant is `"Transport"` / `"BackendRefused"` — pinned as such by `crates/antseal-core/tests/r89_live_outcome_json.rs:69-70`. The divergence is **documented as intentional** at `orchestration.rs:258-261`, and that is exactly what makes it worth a row rather than a bug report: an intentional divergence with nothing holding either half in place is a coincidence waiting to be tidied. (ii) **`FetchFailureClass::token()` has zero call sites in `src/` across every crate** — measured 2026-08-17. The one spelling explicitly built to be a *"stable machine token"* is reachable only from tests, which is the U67/U71 shape a fifth time: a `pub` function with no production caller. (iii) The sibling class disagrees with it on the wire. `ProbeFailureClass` (`crates/antseal-core/src/verify/overlay.rs:122-138`) carries `#[serde(rename_all = "kebab-case")]` and **no `token()` at all**, so one `--json` document hands a consumer `"Transport"` in the `live` member and `"transport"` in the overlay for the same English word. (iv) And the **rendered** word is duplicated by construction: `wording.rs:781` and `:896` both map their own `Transport` to `"transport failure"` from independent tables, which is why `single_sourced()` can never carry a needle for it — `crates/antseal-core/tests/verdict_wording.rs:738-748` says so in its own comment, deliberately admitting only `BackendRefused`'s word to the table. A reword of either side silently diverges, and the guard that exists to catch exactly that is structurally unable to look.
- Do: **rule the vocabulary once, then assert each producer against the ruling rather than against each other**, so a shared mistake cannot pass. Three questions, each with a cheap answer and none of which may be settled silently. (a) **Wire casing**: do the two failure classes agree? Either give `FetchFailureClass` `#[serde(rename_all = "kebab-case")]` — which moves a D65 tier-C shape and needs its tier-C disposition stated, and which `r89_live_outcome_json.rs` will redden by design — or record why the two members of one document legitimately differ. (b) **`token()`**: give it a production caller or delete it. If the machine token is the intended spelling, the wire should use it; if the wire discriminant is the intended spelling, the token is dead code claiming to be an interface. (c) **The shared rendered word**: rule whether `probe_failure_class_label(Transport)` and `live_fetch_failure_class_label(Transport)` are *required* to be equal or merely *happen* to be. If required, assert it directly (one test comparing the two functions, which costs one line and closes the hole `single_sourced()` cannot) and say so at both sites; if not required, say that too, so the next reword is a decision rather than an accident.
- Accept:
  - The casing question is ruled and recorded at the type, and whichever way it goes, **a test fails if either producer changes alone** — the two spellings of `FetchFailureClass`, and the two classes' wire casings, are each asserted against the recorded rule.
  - `FetchFailureClass::token()` either has a production caller or does not exist; a `pub` machine-token function reachable only from tests is not left standing (U67/U71's shape, named).
  - `probe_failure_class_label(Transport) == live_fetch_failure_class_label(Transport)` is either asserted as an invariant or documented as a coincidence at **both** definition sites (`wording.rs:781` and `:896`), and `verdict_wording.rs:738-748`'s exclusion comment is updated to cite the ruling instead of describing the hole.
  - Any wire change is dispositioned against D65 (tier C) with `ENVELOPE_VERSION` and `REPORT_VERSION` explicitly stated as moved or unmoved, and the R18 frozen wording set is untouched unless a reword is the chosen arm — in which case it is a wording-snapshot event under D137 §3 R9, not a tidy-up.
  - A planted fault on **each** producer is watched red, by message.
- Notes: **Not a defect in R89, and R89 was right not to take it** — R89's scope was the type's home and the free-form field; this is what became visible once both spellings sat in one file. **Take with R90**: same section, same three files, and R90 is the ordering half of the same "two producers, one promise" shape. **The cheapest arm may be (c) alone** — one assertion between two label functions — and it is worth landing even if (a) and (b) are deferred with a recorded reason, because it is the only one of the three whose absence is currently *invisible to the guard designed to see it*.

### R92 — Four doc sentences in three files still say the fetch-failure class is `antseal-net`'s, and one cites a method R89 deleted
- Milestone: M3
- Size: XS
- Deps: after R89 (the move that falsified them), R79 (the ruling all four sentences cite), R21/R11 (the layers they document)
- Spec: Verifier — the storage layer and `--live` (MVP-SPEC.md line 119); the sealer in the adversary position beside the display guardrail (line 121)
- Discovered by: **the wave-22 registrar** (2026-08-17), re-measuring R89's evidence against the tree; the closing lane named three of the sites and the sweep found the count is four assertions across three files, with a fifth defect inside one of them.
