- Problem: **R87's class exactly — a doc that describes an integration as pending after it has happened — and this time one of the sentences cites an API that no longer exists.** All four sites are in files the R89 lane could not write, and none of them breaks a build or a link, which is why nothing caught them. (1) `crates/antseal-core/src/verify/overlay.rs:102-105` — *"`antseal-net`'s `FetchFailureClass` is the storage half … whose label is a `&'static str`"*: the type is `antseal-core`'s and has no `label` method at all. (2) `crates/antseal-core/src/verify/overlay.rs:112-117` — *"One deliberate difference survives, and it is the reason `antseal-net` spells its own labels … nothing forces the live class into core"*: the whole premise is dead, `antseal-net` spells no labels and the live class **is** in core. (3) `crates/antseal-cli/src/verify_out.rs:342-344` — *"the fetch-failure detail is `antseal_net::FetchFailureClass`'s `&'static str` label"*: wrong crate, and the detail carried is now the class, not a label. (4) `crates/antseal-cli/src/verify_host.rs:530-534` — *"the string that lands in `LiveBlobOutcome::FetchFailed` is now `FetchFailureClass::label`'s `&'static str`"*: stale twice over, because it is no longer a string **and** `FetchFailureClass::label` was deleted by the very change that falsified the sentence. A reader who greps for that method finds nothing and cannot tell whether the doc or the code is wrong.
- Do: **repair all four in one act, and take the sweep with them.** The replacement clause the R89 lane wrote is *"the class is `antseal-core`'s since R89; `antseal-net` classifies into it via `fetch_failure_class`"* — correct, and **sufficient for sites (1)–(3) but not for (4)**, which must also drop the dangling `FetchFailureClass::label` citation rather than merely re-attribute it. Site (2) needs more than re-attribution: its *"one deliberate difference survives"* paragraph argued a position R89 reversed, so it is rewritten or deleted, not patched. Follow R87's precedent for the guard: assert the corrected sentences **present** and any known-open site **present** too, so whoever repairs the next one turns the test red by design and must delete the entry in the same act.
- Accept:
  - All four sentences state the post-R89 truth, and `grep -rn 'FetchFailureClass::label' crates/` returns nothing.
  - A test asserts the corrected wording is present at each site and fails if any site reverts — R87's `r87_doc_currency` is the shape and may be extended rather than duplicated.
  - Zero frozen bytes move: this row changes rustdoc and comments only, and `verdict-wording.txt` is byte-identical before and after.
  - The two sites **outside** the crates — `docs/decisions/D130-…:390` (which cites `orchestration.rs:231-234` and block-quotes a doc comment R89 deleted) and this tracker's own pre-R89 R79 locators — are explicitly **out of scope** and stay in `docs/instrument-ledger.md`; a lane must not quietly widen into `docs/decisions/`.
- Notes: **Minted immediately under protocol rule 8's Q112 clause, on R87's precedent**: these are shipped-crate docs, not instrument prose, and a renderer's neighbouring documentation is what the next lane reads before changing behaviour. **XS and mechanical**, but it should not ride along inside a behavioural change — R89 could not take it (write scope) and R91 should not (its own diff is where its risk lives).

### R93 — The guard that exists to close the wasm export surface compares names only, so the one change it was built to catch is invisible to it
- Milestone: M3
- Size: S
- Deps: after R23/R25 (the page and its build), D18 §5 R4 (the closed export list this guard enforces), D134 §4 item 2 (the ruling that nominates this row), D129/D136 (the browser lane the guard runs beside)
- Spec: Verifier page — zero-install verification (MVP-SPEC.md lines 133–139); page provenance (line 139)
- Discovered by: **D134's lane** (2026-08-16), which found it while ruling the paper-key unlock export shape and named it in §4 item 2 as *"New row candidate (R or Q domain, S)"*; re-measured by the wave-22 registrar 2026-08-17.
- Problem: **the closed-list rule is enforced by a set comparison over `Object.keys`, and a signature is not a key.** `scripts/wasm-boundary.mjs:94-103` builds `actualExports` from `Object.keys(module_).filter(name => typeof module_[name] === "function")` and compares it against `EXPECTED_EXPORTS` with two `includes` set-differences. **`Function.prototype.length` is never read anywhere in the file, and no type information is inspected.** So widening `verify(bundle_bytes)` to `verify(bundle_bytes, key)` leaves the name set identical and the guard green — and D134 §2 R11 refused exactly that widening *because* it would be *"invisible to the guard that exists to police the boundary"*, choosing a sixth named entry instead precisely so the guard could see it. The rule survived only because the ruling went the guard's way; it would not have survived the other arm. D134 states the defect independently at `:124-129`: *"The guard that enforces the closed list checks names only … Arity and signature are invisible to it."*
- Do: **assert each export's arity alongside its name**, so the closed list is closed in the dimension a change actually travels in. `Function.prototype.length` is free, needs no build-system change and no new dependency, and gives the guard a second axis; whether to go further than arity (a smoke call per export, or a generated signature manifest checked against the `.d.ts` wasm-pack already emits) is the row's judgment call and should be recorded either way. **Plant a fault per rule** — the house discipline, and the reason this row exists at all is that an unplanted guard was believed for the length of a milestone.
- Accept:
  - `wasm-boundary.mjs` fails when an export's arity changes with its name unchanged, and the failure names the export and both arities.
  - A planted fault is watched **red by message** for each rule the guard carries — the name rule and the new arity rule — not merely by exit code.
  - The expected-arity data lives beside `EXPECTED_EXPORTS` as one table, so adding an export forces one edit and not two.
  - If arity is judged insufficient, the reason and the chosen stronger check are recorded at the guard; if it is judged sufficient, the residual gap (same name, same arity, different types) is stated at the guard rather than left implied.
  - No page bytes move: this row changes a guard, not an artifact, and the module digest is unchanged.
- Notes: **Nominated by a resolved decision, which is why it is a row and not a ledger line.** Its subject is an instrument, so protocol rule 8 would ordinarily send it to `docs/instrument-ledger.md` — but D134 §4 item 2 rules it a new row candidate, D134's owning task is **U32** on the M4 ship path, and the boundary is the thing D134 §2 R11's ruling leans on. **Milestone M3 with a recorded reason** (rule 6): the guard's subject is the M3 page surface and it sits with the other R-domain page rows, even though the ruling that ordered it is due M4. **Land it before any signature-changing work reaches the boundary**, which is the only ordering constraint it has.

### R94 — The anti-out-of-context guardrail is pinned on the CLI surface and not on the page a counterparty actually uses

- Milestone: M3
- Size: S
- Deps: after R19 (the redaction view), R22/R24 (the wasm binding and the page), R25 (the page's packaging); consumer of MVP-SPEC line 121
- Spec: Reveal — *"Every reveal displays position + total size (anti-out-of-context guardrail); unrevealed units render as sized blackout blocks"* (MVP-SPEC.md line 121)
- Discovered by: **Q21's lane** (2026-08-17), while writing §2.7 (sealer as adversary) and looking for the guardrail's pin on both surfaces.
- Problem: **the spec's one anti-out-of-context rule is swept on the CLI and asserted on the page by one third of a predicate.** On the CLI, `crates/antseal-cli/tests/redaction_view.rs:187` (`every_unit_row_of_every_shape_states_position_and_total_size`) sweeps five real verified `.sealproof` bundles (`cases()`, `:51-90`) and requires each row to carry `" byte(s) at offset "` **and** `" of "` **and** `" declared"` (`:179-181`), with a floor assertion and a genuine negative control at `:213-236` that plants three degraded rows. On the page there is one candidate, and it is weaker than it reads: `crates/antseal-wasm/tests/boundary.rs:307-321` (`the_document_states_position_and_total_size_for_every_block`) claims in its own comment to enforce line 121 *"for BOTH surfaces"*, but checks a **single substring** (`" byte(s) at offset "`) on the JSON document from **one** bundle, never the other two thirds of the predicate its failure message names, and never a DOM. The browser lane (`scripts/verifier-page-browser.mjs:367-403`) scrapes the rendered rows only to compare them **string-for-string against captured CLI stdout** — a parity check that stays green if both surfaces drop the figures together — and matches none of `at offset`, `byte(s)`, `declared` or `position` anywhere. The guarantee is genuinely structural: the page composes nothing, assigning pre-rendered strings from the shared `RenderedRedaction` (`verifier-web/index.template.html:207-229`, built at `crates/antseal-wasm/src/api.rs:175` from the same core type the CLI uses at `redaction_out.rs:84`). It is simply not pinned on the surface a counterparty opens.
- Do: Raise `boundary.rs:307-321` to the CLI's full predicate — all three fragments, over the same five shapes `redaction_view.rs` uses rather than one — and add a positive assertion in the browser lane that the rendered rows carry position and total **in their own right**, not only that they equal the CLI's. Plant a fault at the fold (a `RenderedRedaction` row with the denominator removed) and watch both surfaces redden, which is what proves the shared-type argument rather than assuming it.
- Accept:
  - The page-side assertion uses the same predicate as `redaction_view.rs:179-181` and the same five shapes, and its failure message describes what it actually checks.
  - The browser lane asserts the rendered rows carry position and total independently of the CLI-parity comparison, so a synchronised regression cannot pass.
  - A planted fault at the shared `RenderedRedaction` seam is watched **red by message on both surfaces**.
  - The structural argument — the page composes nothing — is recorded at the site with its citation, so a later refactor that starts composing rows knows what it is breaking.
- Notes: **Correction to the brief.** The wave-23 notes say *"no browser-level test asserts the page's rendered rows carry position and total"*. True at browser level, but a wasm-level test **does** exist and claims both surfaces in its comment — so a lane taking this row on the brief's wording would find it and conclude the row was wrong. What the test actually asserts is one substring on one bundle; naming it here is the difference between a row that survives contact and one that gets refused.

### R95 — The verifier page retains the whole bundle for the tab's lifetime, and nothing pins that it ever stops

- Milestone: M3
- Size: S
- Deps: after R23/R24/R25 (the page and its packaging), C21 (the zeroization audit and its browser caveat), Q21 (§2.10's M4 cross-check, which records the widening)
- Spec: Vault — *"documented caveat: the WASM verifier cannot guarantee this for bundle-supplied keys in browser memory"* (MVP-SPEC.md line 143)
- Discovered by: **Q21's lane** (2026-08-17), executing C21's standing obligation to cross-check §2.10 once the shipped page existed.
- Problem: **C21's caveat assumed the browser-side copies were accidental and garbage-collectible; the shipped page holds a deliberate, strongly reachable one for the life of the tab.** `verifier-web/index.template.html:394` declares `let sessionBundle = null` at module scope, sets it to the whole bundle at `:418`, and clears it at `:427` — reachable only from `disarmOnline()`, whose single call site is `:291`, the top of the **next** file drop. There is no `beforeunload`, `pagehide`, `visibilitychange` or timeout handler and no `.fill(0)` anywhere in the file (grep: zero hits). It exists so that *confirm online* need not re-read the file, and `:778` re-passes the whole bundle into wasm for a full re-verification at an arbitrary later moment — extending the window rather than closing it. Because the retained object is the **bundle** rather than the derived keys, everything §2.10 scopes (`k_u`, `k_m`, salts) stays fully re-derivable from browser memory until the tab closes. §2.10 now records the widening in prose (`docs/threat-model.md:1958` onward, Q21's M4 cross-check) — **nothing pins the behaviour**, so a change to the retention shape in either direction is invisible to every check.
- Do: Decide the retention deliberately and then pin whichever answer is chosen. The arms: keep the reference and add a lifecycle clear (`pagehide`/`beforeunload`) plus an explicit clear after the online overlay renders; or drop the reference and re-read the file on *confirm online*, which costs a second read and a re-picked file handle in some browsers. Then assert it: a test that the page holds no bundle reference after the online path completes, or after a lifecycle event, with a planted regression watched red. Record the decision in §2.10 beside the cross-check it belongs to.
- Accept:
  - The retention shape is a recorded choice with its cost stated, not an artifact of the `confirm online` implementation.
  - A test asserts the chosen invariant on the page itself — presence or absence of the reference at the named moment — and a planted regression is watched **red by message**.
  - §2.10's cross-check subsection is updated to describe the shipped behaviour after the change, so the threat model and the page do not part again.
  - The report-side copy §2.10 also names (the canonical report crossing into immutable JS strings at `:303` and `:778`) is dispositioned in the same act, or explicitly deferred with a reason.
- Notes: **What could not be determined, recorded rather than glossed:** the wasm-bindgen glue is not committed — `git ls-files verifier-web/` returns exactly one path and no `pkg/` directory exists — so per-call linear-memory copies were not audited and this row makes no claim about them. **The value of the row is the pin, not the finding**: Q21 already wrote the widening into §2.10 with the correct mechanism and the correct line numbers, so what is left is that no check would notice if the page's retention changed tomorrow.
